in writing. − the consensus procedure means that the council must seek the consent of the european parliament before taking certain decisions. since the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, the consensus procedure has been applied, for example, to agreements with significant budgetary implications for the union, to accession agreements or to the extension of rights conferred on union citizens. parliament's prominent role in the consensus procedure is ensured by its veto power. if parliament does not give its assent, the council cannot adopt the act or agreement in question. the purpose of this report is to clarify the way in which consent is given and to bring parliament's rules of procedure into line with practice.

in writing. − the financial participation of employees in the revenues of companies is an area that depends to a large extent on the legal frameworks and conditions of the member states. the fiscal policies and taxation of the member states are key in this area, so the principle of subsidiarity needs to be fully respected. the union may establish framework conditions and provide guidance through the exchange of best practices. involving employees in corporate governance can help them think longer term about their employers. opportunities for innovative financing may arise as employees become interested in investing in the company they are employed by. nevertheless, the level of employee involvement is relatively low, especially when compared to the us level. guidelines are therefore particularly useful in this area.

in writing. − social protection is at the heart of the european social model, and one of its main functions is to promote social inclusion and social justice. member states must ensure responsible long-term financing of social security systems in order to enable social and economic goals to be reconciled and to help sustain and develop the economy. member states must also provide a guarantee of social security for the most vulnerable social groups, such as the unemployed, the disabled, single-parent families, large families with four or more children, the young, pensioners and young families, and they must ensure that social services are accessible to vulnerable social groups and people with long-term care needs, particularly in rural areas and disadvantaged regions. practical steps must be taken to combat poverty and social exclusion by providing an appropriate minimum income and a social security system with a particular focus on marginalised communities and those at risk of poverty. specific standards and an implementation framework should be defined in accordance with national practices. furthermore, member states and the commission must act to combat all forms of discrimination in the labour market, including that against women, and must take measures to prevent the dismissal of employees during pregnancy or motherhood.

on behalf of the s&d group. my colleagues! unfortunately, neither the european court of justice's decision nor the european commission's proposal is a solution for european beekeepers. the solution is contained in the amendment proposal of the environment committee, which would exempt honey as a natural product from the obligation to indicate the ingredients. however, the question of whether or not to label pollen from genetically modified plants is a major concern for european beekeepers and consumers. this is a very important need for all consumers, as consumers have a fundamental right to know. this is also in the interest of the vast majority of european beekeepers. obviously there are states where there are genetically modified crops. the situation for bee-keeping organisms is more complicated there, but we have to indicate on the label at european level whether the pollen, the pollen powder, comes from genetically modified plants or not. the commission proposal is not a solution at all, because mrs girling has already said that, on the basis of the commission proposal, no honey will be labelled as containing genetically modified pollen. i therefore urge the european commission, on behalf of the socialist group, not to sweep the problem under the carpet. the current proposal is not a suitable solution. it is in the interest of hungary and other central european beekeepers to strengthen their position and show that their product does not contain genetically modified pollen. this would strengthen europe's position against competitors from latin america, china and canada. please support the environmental committee's proposition 10 in tomorrow's vote, which is a solution to this problem.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! we are discussing a very important issue for european honey producers today. the european commission refers in its proposal to the fact that pollen is a natural constituent of honey, not a component. at first sight, there is not much difference between the two concepts, but if we look deeper, we see that there is a huge difference, because if we accept the position of the european commission, then there is practically no need to indicate the presence of genetically modified flowers in honey. i think we have to take into account the interests of european consumers. we need to provide them with access to information, not least because in many european countries it is the express aim of governments - and there is a country like hungary, which has enshrined in its constitution that people should have access to gm-free food. so i think that if we take these principles into account, there can be no question that we should consider pollen as an ingredient, and we should indeed list the pollen content of genetically modified plants on it if its quantity is set at nine tenths of a percent of the pollen content. i do not think that the members of the european parliament should have the interests of european honey importers in mind. we should rather take into account the needs and expectations of european honey producers. i think we have all spoken to beekeepers in the past, i have spoken to the hungarian beekeepers, for example, but i am also aware of the position of the european beekeepers' association, which clearly states that it is a clear competitive disadvantage for them not to have to indicate the gmo content of their honey. so i think we should accept the amendments proposed by the committee on the environment. i myself have tabled a similar amendment which makes it compulsory to indicate the gmo content without imposing heavy costs on honey producers.
consumer confidence is important, but it also requires that the bottle of honey be labeled with the contents. i got a little bottle of honey this morning from some lobbyist, and it was written in very small letters, microscopic letters, that it was a mixture of eu and non-eu honey. and that's what you have to put on a bottle under european rules, even if that bottle contains 1% eu honey and 99% imported honey. and they still say it's a mixture of eu and non-eu honey. i have suggested many times that the percentage of eu honey and the percentage of non-eu honey should be indicated precisely, so that the consumer knows how much eu and how much non-eu honey is in the bottle. beekeeping is important for hungary, and it's not by chance that we have the highest density of bees, and this is thanks to the diligence of beekeepers, and the fact that hungary has the largest amount of acacia trees. the hive is an excellent apiary. yet there are those who pursue the akak. they won't let you plant acacia trees. they are not allowing the replanting of acacia forests, and a commission proposal recently even wants to ban the planting of acacia for energy purposes, for energy forest purposes, although the acacia is not only important for the bees, but also brings income to farmers. it can be produced profitably in places where other trees do not grow, and is also important for renewable energy production. the hungarian beekeepers are demanding that you install a hive. he who respects the beekeepers, loves the honey, should not chase the hive.
in writing. the crisis in the european union has also affected the knowledge sector. we need to contain the spread. europe's industry, which used to account for 20% of gdp, has now fallen to 15%. industrial production is now 10% below pre-crisis levels, with 3 million jobs lost in the industrial sector. the deterioration of the industry is a threat to the union's competitiveness. the union needs an industrial policy approach that integrates competitiveness, sustainability and fair work. currently, 34 million people in the european union are employed in industry. the union needs a unified strategy to address the crisis, in which the development of industry/knowledge industries can play a key role. as a first step, the communication of industrial policy to young people needs to be made more effective. many recent graduates leave hungary in the hope of a better life, but they must be kept in their homeland. this can be achieved by developing knowledge-based industries such as medicine, energy, computer technology. keeping young people at home would stop the slide into the middle class, but it would also create economic recovery and real wages. the main priority would be to preserve strong production sectors and know-how. the change would be felt within six to eight years. in recent years, hungary has been building with eu funds. the development of the knowledge industry should be given high priority. it would be a real sacrifice and it would really bring the desired result.
madam president, i welcome the fact that the greek presidency has given priority to fostering economic growth in order to promote social cohesion and political stability, with special regard to striking a balance between fiscal consolidation and growth-enhancing policies. being a hungarian mep and representing the hungarian ppe group here, it will be a very interesting period for us to share our experiences with you, because we have been coming up with some good policies. it was also one of our greatest challenges – and it is the same for you at european level – to find a way to grow out of the economic recession and simultaneously maintain and develop our social achievements. we know that the most vulnerable are hit the hardest, so we know that racism and xenophobia are growing because of this further weakening of the protection of the most vulnerable. the decision that the council made on 9 december thus represents a considerable step forward in this matter by adopting its recommendations on effective roma integration measures in the member states. this is a really serious expectation of us on the part of parliament. we hope that the greek presidency will further promote the real inclusion of the eu’s largest minority. to this end, it is very important that the greek presidency is committed to moving forward the implementation of the eu 2020, the compact for growth and jobs and the european semester. i would also like to welcome the presidency’s focus on enlargement for the roma population of the western balkans. the instrument for pre-accession may be an effective tool for underpinning national initiatives and we should seek ways to influence policy-making, also through the stabilisation and association process, which has not been used to the extent that it should be.
, in writing. the greek presidency of the eu will focus on fisheries and the integrated maritime policy. it is necessary to reiterate that freshwater fisheries and in particular aquaculture are significantly different from marine fisheries and marine aquaculture. while the latter carries serious environmental and conservation risks, such as the release of farmed fish species and the harmful discharge of organic matter into the sea, freshwater fisheries in closed systems or in brackish ponds pose no threat to the environment and even increase biodiversity. the lagoons provide habitat for numerous protected bird and animal species. in the debate on the new common fisheries policy, i have therefore consistently called for a legal separation between marine and freshwater aquaculture. unfortunately, the new regulations regulate these two different areas in a uniform way. therefore, freshwater aquaculture will be subject to a number of unjustified restrictions which take into account only the characteristics of marine aquaculture. i ask the greek presidency to find a solution to the scientifically based legal definition of marine and freshwater aquaculture.
in writing. – mr prime minister, i warmly welcome the programme of the greek presidency and the emphasis put on growth, employment, social cohesion and increased democratic accountability of the european union. it is crucial in order to re-orientate europe and restore the confidence of our citizens. i am particularly pleased with the ambitious goals set regarding gender equality and the fact that the greek presidency recognises equality between women and men not only as a fundamental value but also as a key driver for growth and social equality. therefore, the focus on ‘women in the economy’ and cooperation with the european institute for gender equality are, in my opinion, crucial, especially in view of this year’s midterm review of the 2020 strategy. we need more accurate indicators and a more ambitious overall strategy with more efficient gender mainstreaming. i also hope that the planned high-level conference on violence against women will be followed by concrete action. finally i look forward to collaborating with you on the regulation on the statute and funding of european political parties and foundations in order to reach a positive outcome by the end of this legislative term.
, in writing. - mr prime minister. the citizens of hungary recognize the spectacular achievements of the greek government in crisis management. we are not unfamiliar with the situation: in 2009, hungary, like greece, was in a very serious financial situation and the troika's bailout was needed to avoid a state bankruptcy. in both countries, the irresponsible economic policies of the previous socialist governments brought about bankruptcy, and the people's governments were left with the thankless task of putting the economy in order. the fidesz government, which took office in 2010, was surprised to find that, unlike several euro area countries, it had not received any concessions or advances to achieve the deficit target, and was therefore forced to introduce innovative measures. the risks and the resistance were great, but the hard work has paid off: the budget deficit is below 3%, the number of people employed is at a record high, over 4 million, unemployment is falling and new productive investments are announced almost every week. the hungarian economy grew well above the eu average in 2013 and we have good prospects for this year as well. the success of the hungarian model demonstrates that crisis management is not possible by orthodox methods alone. reducing the deficit and debt is achieved not only by cutting back on salaries, pensions and other social transfers but also by involving economic operators in public burdens. i wish you every success in the transformation of the greek economy and in the implementation of the presidency's ambitious programme. thank you, sir.

in writing. − public procurement markets are of great economic importance, with a weight as % of annual gdp of 16% in the eu, 11% in the us, 18% in japan and a similarly high weight in emerging countries. however, the openness of these markets is highly variable and it is therefore desirable that the union has an instrument to encourage trading partners to open their own public procurement markets to an eu-level level in bilateral and multilateral international negotiations. it is essential to ensure consistent compliance with national and union labour law standards. international cooperation should contribute to the sustainable economic, social and environmental development of developing countries, to improving employment conditions and to reducing poverty. reducing unemployment and creating new jobs has become particularly important in the face of the crisis. in view of this requirement, a compromise proposal in the union interest should be developed.
in writing. − the european parliament's plenary session voted against the interests of european and hungarian non-gmo honey producers in today's abstentionist vote on the amendment of the honey directive. the report adopted today favours the import of honey containing gmos from third countries outside the union and seriously infringes consumers' freedom of choice. we have lost the battle, but the war is not over, because today's vote is only the first step in a long negotiation process, because the negotiations with the council are still ahead of us, where together with the allied member states our country will continue to fight for the interests of hungarian honey producers.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the rapporteur's and the commission's proposal to consider pollen as a natural constituent of honey. in support of my position, i would like to give the following reasons. first of all, i think it's important to emphasize that honey should be considered a healthy, natural product. therefore, the possibility of labelling honey with genetically modified floral material as an ingredient clearly affects the reputation of honey as a natural product, which would have a direct and indirect negative impact on the beekeeping sector. i also consider the impact of the report on smes to be of paramount importance. the classification of pollen as an ingredient would significantly increase the cost of testing to obtain the information on the labels. i think the bees that would be most affected by this change are the ones that produce the lowest quantities. this may result in some amateur beekeepers ceasing their activity. on the other hand, the increased production costs resulting from the additional testing requirements will be accompanied by an increase in retail prices. for the above reasons, i agree that meps should work together on this issue!

in writing. the main objective of the hercule iii programme is to strengthen the fight against fraud, in particular the fight against cigarette smuggling. the european parliament's committee on budgetary control examined the dossier from november 2012, followed by tripartite interinstitutional consultations, and finally, thanks to the efforts of the irish and lithuanian presidencies, the latter was able to conclude the negotiations on 15 october 2013. the parliament was able to pass important points such as strengthening coordination at eu level, the definition of kpis to measure the results of the programme, or increasing the co-financing rate from 80% to 90% in member states that were more exposed to such fraud. hungary will also receive a significant share of the eur 104 918 000 budget allocated to the hercule iii programme. i supported the report in the committee and in the plenary.

in writing. − i consider the report to be of paramount importance as it would give the commission implementing powers to collect, organise and evaluate data in an appropriate way in order to produce statistical data of a high quality on the trade of goods between member states. this initiative should be particularly highlighted and supported, as it would help to integrate this new statistical system into the overall european statistical system, where it is essential to improve coordination between national authorities and eurostat. the national data sets on goods under customs procedure , i.e. export , collected in a harmonised system will in future allow a clear picture of the precise extent of intra-eu and extra-eu trade. furthermore, this report even envisages that this data can be exchanged between member states, confidentially but in compliance with all safety standards, free of charge, which i believe will promote even better integrity of the internal market in the long term.

the news of the past few days has painted a picture of the ideal eastern european worker, who is welcomed in western europe. the good eastern european worker works as hard as he can, for a strict minimum wage, no more, no less, so as not to cause social dumping or extra costs for his employer. she does all the jobs that the locals can't or won't do, takes care of old western european parents, raises the kids, takes care of herself when she's sick. the good eastern european worker leaves his family behind, does not burden the education and health care system of the host country with them, does not claim family allowance for his children, and does not go to the doctor when he is sick. he doesn't even send his salary home, he spends it all on the spot, letting domestic consumption grow. he lives in a workhouse or an abandoned shed because he doesn't want to increase demand and beat the already high rents. he's quietly tolerating being labeled a charity tourist, a free-spirited person, or an undesirable person who's contributing to a deterioration in crime statistics. if you send him home, he'll go home, come back when he's called. but the ideal eastern european is not a worker. he's a billionaire businessman, so it's okay if the source of his wealth isn't entirely clear. he's granted citizenship and freedom of movement. but this is not a europe of solidarity, of common values. we eastern europeans did not join this.

, in writing. the safe harbour convention was created by the european commission and the us to bridge the gap between the problems posed by different data protection directives. us companies operating in the eu can transfer european data to the us without any review. the ftc monitors whether companies comply with the seven principles of the convention when they transfer data. after the nsa case broke, many in the european parliament were concerned that sensitive data of european citizens and businesses could be transferred to us databases without any special controls. the european commission's 13-point proposal would improve the convention in terms of transparency, redress, enforcement and access to data. many in the european parliament, however, argued for the immediate suspension of the convention. i myself am inclined to wait for the commission's report in the summer of 2014 on whether the us has complied with the commission's recommendations and to decide on the possible suspension of the convention in this light. the majority of the ep is also in favour of suspending tftp. i would consider such a move rash and irresponsible. the tftp is a key instrument in the fight against terrorism. since neither the commission nor europol are aware of any misuse of the information provided by the tftp, i would not consider it fortunate to have a sudden weakening of the fight against terrorism and the creation of unnecessary security risks.
on behalf of the s&d group. - mr president! i welcome the clear words of the commissioner and i welcome the fact that this debate is taking place today, because it gives us the opportunity to reflect together on the relationship between national and european citizenship and to express our commitment to european values. throughout europe, there is a growing trend of events that call into question our basic values. these are partly the consequences of the crisis, but if we do not address them in time, they could undermine the whole of the european construction. one of the most important acquis of the union is citizenship, which includes freedom of movement, the right to vote, eligibility and consular protection. the value of this is illustrated by the fact that in a monday advertisement in the financial times, which has 2.2 million readers, a company offers its services for a presumably good price to obtain citizenship or residence permits in several eu countries. more and more countries are seeing money-making opportunities in the direct or indirect sale of european citizenship or residence permits and the competition is creating a downward spiral. in many places, the principle that there should be a direct link between the state and its citizens seems to be falling into the background. some governments look to mass citizenship for direct political gain, not financial gain. the treaties leave the question of establishment and citizenship to the member states. but these, like many other decisions, can raise problems at eu level, affecting the question of trust between countries, the external relations of the union, directly affecting other countries because of the freedom of establishment and, in particular, the freedom of movement within schengen. the privileged citizenship of the richest is neither fair nor equitable, it creates a new kind of inequality. europe today tends to keep out those who don't bring immediate benefits. in the mediterranean region, it leaves refugees stranded, who are trying to reach the union territory at great risk, for lack of money. what is the response of the member states, of the union to this discrimination? i call on the commission to examine, in the spirit of eu values, the different programmes in the member states which grant residence permits and, directly or indirectly, eu citizenship, and to make recommendations to the member states which can later serve as the basis for a common policy.

in writing. – can citizenship be treated solely as a commodity? the present government of malta seems to think so – an attitude that can fairly be described as market fundamentalism gone mad. indeed, why stop with citizenship? why not start selling everything else that makes money? the answer is obvious, or should be: some things cannot be treated as objects for sale and citizenship is one of them. why? because citizenship is at the heart of the relationship between citizens and their state. make it a commodity and the relationship will erode. can we envisage citizens who have no relationship with the state of which they are citizens? because this is what we are dealing with. and a final point – maltese citizenship is, at the same time, european citizenship and all the member states of europe are stakeholders in malta. the maltese government should confront this reality.
the following joint debate item is balkans/results reports: statement by the council and the commission on the 2013 results report on serbia (2013/2880 ((rsp)) and statement by the council and the commission on the european integration process in kosovo (2013/2881 ((rsp)).
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, let me commend mr kacin’s report. it is very good and cooperation with the shadows has been excellent. i think we can also all agree with what mr kacin said: this has been a key year in serbia. it is a major achievement. serbia deserves due praise for this, and above all for the breakthrough in relations with kosovo. the historic significance of the belgrade-pristina dialogue is hard to overestimate; coming to terms with the loss of territory is very hard to bear for any country. at the same time we should not allow ourselves to be mesmerised by the kosovo issue, emotionally crucial though it is. there are several other problems on the agenda. first, there is the serbian constitution, which still regards kosovo as an organic part of its territory. clearly this will have to change, sooner or later. serbia should address the centralising mindset that plays such a strong role in policy-making. the slow encroachment by the centre on the autonomy of the province of vojvodina, and indeed of local government, is evidently pointing in the wrong direction and runs counter to subsidiarity and effective governance. then there is the deep level problem of corruption. it is a commonplace that corruption flourishes where the private sector meets the state unless there is a strong, independent administration of the law. with the best will in world it would be difficult to assert that the serbian legal system is in this category. sadly, the opposite appears to be true. these issues are interconnected. the very praiseworthy efforts by the government to root out corruption have involved centralisation and political action, which is excellent. but in the longer term a balance will have to be struck between the anti-corruption drive, the relative weakness of the legal administration and over-centralisation. finally, the most serious issue facing the serbian authorities is that of the economy, which is in a very poor state. the many years of misgovernment in the past have generated grave distortions to market activity. in this area action is extremely urgent.

on behalf of the ppe group. on behalf of the european people's party, i welcome this joint debate and the draft joint resolution. i think it is important to point out that homelessness is unfortunately not a problem of a few countries but of all the member states of europe, even if the degree of its seriousness varies somewhat. we agree with the commissioner that homelessness is one of the most extreme and extreme forms of poverty and unfortunately it is also true that in recent years the rate of homelessness has increased in almost all countries. it is clear that a multilateral political response is needed, as this is a complex phenomenon. it is also important to note that the primary responsibility for ending homelessness lies with the member states. at the same time, the current eu framework calls for more ambitious and closer coordination and cooperation. many european institutions, epsco, the council, the committee of the regions, the european economic and social committee, and we in the european parliament have already called on the european commission to create a homelessness strategy. i was pleased to hear the commissioner speak about monitoring, about asking for suggestions. i was pleased to hear that they are planning to include proposals on homelessness in the country-specific recommendations. but i don't think that's enough. the commission should develop a strategy for cooperation, with a view to closer coordination of programmes. we think it would be good if you set up an expert committee and it would be good if there was real progress in the collection, management and analysis of data to enable comparable joint analyses and comparability of programmes.
we welcome the commission's intentions as expressed by the commissioner, although we do think that it is late in the cycle, but there may be further steps. homelessness is the most brutal form of social exclusion, which also seriously violates human dignity. we know that the economic crisis has increased poverty, increased homelessness, but we don't have the exact figures. you mentioned, officer, that they're doing a survey. it would be very important to know the exact extent of homelessness. and we would also ask the human rights agency to look into the human rights implications of extreme poverty and homelessness. while homelessness is a member state responsibility, we must ensure that governments and local authorities seek solutions based on our common european values. today, there has been a lot of talk about the criminalisation of homelessness, which is unacceptable and requires action at european level. the european union funds, the regional development fund and the social fund can help to improve the situation. we therefore ask the commission to take this into account in the partnership agreements.

in writing. − on the initiative of the maltese socialist government, citizenship of malta and, therefore, automatically of the eu can be purchased for €650,000. malta does not impose any additional administrative conditions on the buyer, so there is no need to prove that the buyer has a lasting commitment to malta. the socialist proposal has caused a great deal of outrage among maltese citizens and in the public opinion of the eu member states. the implementation of such an initiative would depreciate the value and credibility of union citizenship and would also jeopardise the international acceptance of the union passport. the sale of citizenship without guarantees would also entail serious security risks, creating new threats to both the eu and member states' law enforcement agencies and to the transatlantic security system as a whole. the irresponsible and cynical initiative of the maltese socialists underlines the need to define more clearly than ever the status of eu citizenship associated with national citizenship in the unity of rights and obligations.
in writing. − with serbia, the european council approved the opening of accessions in june 2013. the resolution welcomes the council's decision and the serbian government's commitment to european integration. the commission also calls for further reforms and measures in the areas of justice, the fight against fraud and corruption and the protection of minorities, which is also important for the hungarian minority. on 21 january, accession negotiations between serbia and the eu began, and i supported the resolution in the plenary vote. however, it is important that minority rights are given priority during the negotiations.
in writing. − the decision by the june 2013 european council to launch accession negotiations with serbia is to be highly welcomed as well as the commitment shown by the serbian government to the european integration process. despite several commendable achievements however, there remain some serious issues to be addressed. efforts on implementing the legislative framework related to minorities throughout the country must be continued and serbia must address more vigorously the situation of roma. anti-discrimination legislation must be fully harmonised with eu policies and a holistic approach towards roma inclusion must be applied, including effective monitoring of inclusion measures in order to narrow the gap between legislation and its implementation. furthermore, serbia must step up its efforts to combat trafficking in human beings and to this end must formalise the roles and responsibilities of officials and service providers in identifying the victims and referring them to the services concerned.
in writing. − i would like to welcome the april 2013 agreement on normalisation of relations between belgrade and pristina under the eu-facilitated dialogue, and kosovoʼs launch of negotiations for a stabilisation and association agreement with the eu in october 2013. kosovar authorities must make further progress in a multitude of pressing issues however. both central and local-level institutions must implement the legislation on human rights effectively and contribute to the further development of a multi-ethnic society, in particular by addressing the situation of roma as one of the key human-rights issues in kosovo. kosovar authorities must, furthermore, implement fully the convention on the rights of the child and revise the policies regarding child poverty, including modification of the social assistance scheme and the introduction of a universal, means-tested child benefit scheme. organised crime – and more specifically trafficking and exploitation of women and underage persons for sexual exploitation or forced begging – must also be combated effectively. a comprehensive, multi-disciplinary and victim-oriented approach is needed to this end, with unhindered access to assistance, support and protection for victims. efforts to prosecute, convict and sentence sex and labour trafficking offenders also need to be strengthened.

2014 will be an exceptional year in europe in several ways. on the one hand, we can look back on the first ten years of the membership of the central european and baltic states, including hungary, in the eu, and on the other hand, this year we celebrate the quarter-century anniversary of the end of the cold war confrontation, of the changes in the central european system, of the hungarian negotiated revolution. 1989 was indeed the year of miracles and a fitting end to the 1956 revolution and freedom struggle. we can be proud that the fall of the berlin wall and the reunification of germany could not have been possible without the courage of hungary. we can be proud of the government of németh miklós, foreign minister gyula horn, who had the courage to open the borders to tens of thousands of east german refugees, to break down the iron curtain that divided europe. on the 25th anniversary of the regime change, we hungarians can proudly say that we did our part.

in this debate, virtually all the speeches were about the fact that the european union is based on the principles of equality, equality between people and equal treatment and non-discrimination. maybe that's the theory, or maybe that's what everyone thinks is right. in practice, however, the opposite is true, particularly between nationals of member states. it is simply a lie that the european union has brought the principle of equal dignity or non-discrimination into european life. compare a cashier or a salesman at a hungarian food chain with a fellow employee of the same foreign food chain in, say, mrs. lunacek's country, austria, or germany. one earns 200 or 250 euros and is totally deprived of his right to work, the other earns several times as much and is entitled to protection of his right to work. this is where the anti-discrimination movement should start, and then i think everyone would have a better understanding of discrimination and equal treatment.
a high level of consumer protection ensures high quality products and increases consumer confidence, thus contributing to the efficiency of the internal market. i agree with mr rochefort that the provisions of the directive in force are satisfactory and that there is no need for an in-depth revision of the text at the moment, but that it is essential that the directive is fully implemented and properly applied by the member states. there are of course still shortcomings as regards the need for stricter application of directive 2005/29/ec. the commission should therefore closely monitor the application of the legislation and, where appropriate, take action against member states which infringe, fail to implement or apply the directive correctly, in accordance with the treaty on the functioning of the european union. i also consider it imperative to ensure that consumers have effective, quick and low-cost redress options. in this regard, member states should also fully implement the directive on alternative dispute resolution for consumer disputes and the regulation on out-of-court settlement of disputes arising from online transactions.

in writing. i would like to draw your attention to the fact that the un has declared 2014 as the international year of family farming. the opening event of the year will be the 'world forum and exhibition on family management' conference, to be held in budapest from 4 to 7 march 2014. i believe that family farms form the backbone of the european agricultural model. they play a central role in both global and local food security. i therefore welcome the askersky report on the future of small farms. i agree with the objective that the commission and the member states should take greater account of the specific needs of family farms in the measures planned to be introduced both under the new cap and for the period after 2020.
in writing. in hungary there are about half a million farms with less than five hectares of land. a significant proportion of them have not been involved in any eu support programme to date. the new common agricultural policy, starting in 2015, will allow for flat-rate support for small farms of less than five hectares under the simplified small farmer support scheme. the cap system allows the area-based payment condition to be reduced to 0,3 hectares. in this case, 200 000 families and small farms could receive eu support. this could help improve the living conditions of half a million rural residents by eur 350 000 a year. i therefore call on the next hungarian government to set the limit of the eu aid at 0.3 hectares in favour of four million people living below the subsistence level, mainly in rural villages. it is equally important that the state supports the market access of small farms by developing integration and cooperation, as well as financial and advisory tools. the survival of small, self-sufficient or semi-self-sufficient farms is key to ensuring farmers' jobs, a decent income and preventing rural depopulation. the viability of european agriculture can only be ensured by a multi-level agricultural competitiveness model: we need large farms that are globally competitive, medium-sized farms producing for the regional market and family and small farms that meet local needs. the balanced ratio of the three levels ensures the stability and diversity of european agriculture.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the legislative proposal which aims to create an appropriate legal framework for collective management organisations to manage rights collectively on behalf of rightholders. the proposal establishes rules to ensure better governance and greater transparency of collective management organisations. it shall also encourage and facilitate the multi-territorial licensing of authors' rights in musical works by collective management organisations. in view of the digital world, online licensing should facilitate online access to musical content in the member states. common rights management organisations in the european union will also issue licenses for foreign music works to online service providers established abroad. in my view, collective management organisations in the eu should continue to play an important role in preserving cultural diversity and should become partners in managing rights for european and international artists. joint rightholders should ensure that authors are remunerated swiftly and should help to clarify for authors who represents them in respect of which rights.

in writing. - the european parliament, in its own initiative report, responded to the 2013 eu justice scoreboard, which assesses the effectiveness of national justice systems, at today's plenary session. the main message of the parliament is that any comparison of justice systems should be based on objective criteria, with full respect for the sovereignty of the member states and the different legal and constitutional traditions. we aim to improve the quality and efficiency of justice at both eu and national level. predictable, efficient and timely justice is also essential to enhance competitiveness. thanks to the new hungarian basic law and the judicial reform, hungary now fully meets these requirements, and we are therefore interested in developing an objective evaluation system in which the commission acts impartially and without applying double standards. the judicial system of each member state should be assessed on the basis of objective criteria not on the basis of public opinion polls or press reports. hungary was subject to subjective political criticism in 2012, when the commission, following an analysis of the justice systems, made specific recommendations concerning certain member states. today, we have stopped the socialists' attempt to use the judicial scoreboard for political purposes, thus jeopardizing the real purpose of the tool, which is to evaluate objectively and statistically.
in writing. - i would like to welcome the initiative aiming at contributing to the development of national judicial systems and strengthening their quality, efficiency and independence. it may also contribute to the effective application of eu legislation in cases when national courts apply eu law as ‘union courts’. the scoreboard, however, must be based on equal treatment between member states and must respect the individual traits and different legal traditions in each country. any political misuse of the initiative must be strictly avoided and the regular evaluations must be founded on reliable and comparable data. i hope that, as a non-binding tool operated in the context of an open dialogue with member states, the scoreboard may help define better justice policies and identify judiciary issues that deserve particular attention.
in writing. - the main message of parliament in relation to the eu judicial scoreboard 2013 is that any comparison of justice systems should be based on objective criteria, with full respect for the sovereignty of the member states and the different legal and constitutional traditions. we aim to improve the quality and efficiency of justice at both eu and national level. predictable, efficient and timely justice is also essential to enhance competitiveness. thanks to the new hungarian basic law and the judicial reform, hungary now fully meets these requirements, and we are therefore interested in developing an objective evaluation system in which the commission acts impartially and without applying double standards. the judicial system of each member state should be assessed on the basis of objective criteria, not on the basis of public opinion polls or press reports. we call on the commission to take greater account of the differences between national justice systems in the future when collecting data and defining indicators, thus ensuring equal treatment for all member states in the assessment of their justice systems.
in writing. - the steel industry has a long tradition in europe and plays an important role in the economic life of the union. innovative steel products provide durability and energy efficiency in a number of strategic sectors. with more than 500 steelworks in europe directly employing 360 000 people, it is crucial for economic and security of supply reasons as well as social considerations that europe has a strong steel base in the future. the main industries representing the markets for steel products have been in crisis since 2009 and therefore today many european steel mill owners have been forced to partially reduce capacity or to close down completely. the difficult situation is further aggravated by the fact that the union steel industry is at a serious competitive disadvantage due to high european energy prices, strict environmental standards and trade restrictions imposed by developing countries. the issue is also of key importance for hungary, as the steel industry is a strategically important sector in hungary, and the stable operation and long-term profitability of dunaferr is therefore of prime hungarian interest. the commission also found that the union industry's sales volume increased by more than 10% during the period considered. the european parliament calls for stronger eu action against international trade restrictions on raw materials and steel products. the decision adopted today calls for the fight against unfair competition from third countries to be tackled by making more effective use of the appropriate trade defence instruments at its disposal.
only international trade based on mutual benefits and fair procedures will produce a satisfactory result. if either of those two is missing, then this trade will not be sustainable in the long run. the european union must therefore act against unfair trading practices, especially as the european union is one of the most open trading partners in the world and unfortunately there are more and more people who are almost accustomed to unfair trading practices against the european union. in particular, dumping and distortions of raw material and energy prices, such as the tensions over rare earth metals. these are causing serious damage to the european union's economy and threatening jobs. consequently, trade defence instruments should be used much more effectively, small and medium-sized enterprises should be involved in such procedures as much as possible, penalties should be applied to products on the road so that retaliatory measures cannot be circumvented, and in particular, in raw materials and energy, deterrent duties should be applied, which should be retaliatory in nature in order to discourage anyone who tries to do so.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the commission proposal before us is aimed at modernising both the anti-dumping and the anti-subsidy rules. anti-dumping duties should be imposed at the level of the injury caused to european producers by dumping. we must ensure that the present proposal is strengthened, i think, because we must raise the duties to a level which is also a penalty, otherwise they will not be retrograde, dissuasive. i believe that the protection of european industry requires that we use all the means at our disposal to strengthen european smes, so that we can not only preserve existing jobs but also create new ones by strengthening our market position and increasing our competitiveness. so i think we don't have to hold him back from raising tariffs. not only should it be damaging, but it should be deterrent.
minister! let me express my appreciation on behalf of parliament for taking part in the debate which was not scheduled. i'll close this discussion. the vote will take place on wednesday 5 february 2014. the commission shall be assisted by the committee established by article 15 of regulation (eu) no 182/2011.
the following point is the council and commission declaration on the need to swiftly adopt a broad financial transaction tax (2014/2513 ((rsp)).
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, many experts suggest that the financial transaction tax (ftt) is a wunderwaffe to solve all underlying problems of the banking crisis. nothing is further from the truth. the ftt cannot even be considered a major source of revenue for the eu budget. in any case, it is not something to be called ‘own revenue’. it is indispensable to define precisely the objectives we wish to achieve when designing new solutions to the still unfolding banking crisis. firstly, we want to make retail banking safe and retail deposits secure. that can be best achieved by three actions: separation of commercial and investment banking with a new glass-steagall arrangement; strengthening deposit insurance at the european level; and applying no tax, i repeat, applying no tax on either deposits or lending. look no further than the hugely negative impact of the all-encompassing banking tax in my country. we have now a government-orchestrated credit crunch and no growth at all. secondly, we would like to avoid contagion by ring-fencing potentially toxic investment banking assets. here, the best way would be to apply the volcker rule. the volcker rule, as many people know, prohibits banks, or institutions that own a bank, from engaging in proprietary trading that is not at the behest of their clients, and from owning or investing in a hedge fund or a private equity fund. president obama endorsed the volcker rule four years ago. thirdly, reduce volatility in securities and derivative trading in order to tame herd behaviour. that can be partially achieved by slowing down high frequency trading. here a modest tax or a flat fee, for that matter, applicable on selected securities and derivative transactions can be helpful. in order to preserve liquidity, market makers should be able to gain exemptions as well. as it must be clear to all by now, the ftt is no panacea for curing all problems of banking. we support its limited application only within the framework of a comprehensive and global – and i repeat global – financial sector reform which would strengthen the efficiency and competitiveness of european banking. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 149(8))
question for the house of commons. in his speech, he mentioned that in hungary, the special taxes imposed by the government had frozen the credit market and thus caused the economy to decline. i would ask you, if you know the figures for 2013, to give me an idea of how much the hungarian economy grew in 2013 and how much the average economy of the european union grew.
answer to a double-edged question if it weren't for this unreasonable, unprofessional banking tax in hungary, we could have achieved three times this economic growth. all options shall be compared with those that would have been available if this had been applied. you know that hungary is almost the only country in eastern europe that has not even reached the gdp of 2005. this is largely due to the blessed activities of your government.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the optimist sees opportunity in every emergency, the pessimist sees danger in every opportunity. i think churchill summed up the two different types of people well. i am very pleased that the european commission and the council have also started to work on the introduction of a special tax, a transaction tax that can help to implement major european programmes. however, i think it is worth looking at the practices of the member states, for example hungary, where in 2010 a bank tax was introduced, followed by a transaction tax, precisely in order to make the impossible situation caused by eight years of incompetent socialist government in hungary a common burden on the way out of the crisis. i think we should continue to look at how these resources are collected and used, not centrally, but at the level of the member states. this could be more effective.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! the debate on the 2030 climate and energy targets between member states and within the commission seems to have been resolved by a more ambitious 40% emission reduction target, while a more moderate 27% renewable energy target has been set. the material itself acknowledges that in lower income countries, burden sharing at eu level is more costly. for those member states below 90% of the eu average gdp, this means €3 billion in investment, a surplus per country, and therefore needs to be compensated. i also believe it is of the utmost importance that the member states retain full autonomy in defining the energy mix, including nuclear energy and shale gas.
, in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the union's long-term commitment to climate and energy policy is essential for europe's security of energy supply, economic competitiveness and prosperity. the commission should also be able to assess the impact of the union's climate change policy on the union's climate and energy policies, including the impact of the union's climate and energy policies on the union's climate and energy policies. long-term investment cycles require ambitious targets that provide adequate incentives to deploy low-carbon technologies and strengthen the union's green economy. at the same time, a balance of objectives must be sought, as we cannot ignore the performance of the economy, the evolution of the global energy market and the modest emission reduction ambitions of international actors. taking into account those factors, a binding carbon reduction target for 2030 should be introduced, which takes into account economic realities and the outcome of international climate negotiations. moreover, a greater focus should be placed on striking a balance between ambitious climate objectives and the competitiveness of union industry. this is the only way to avoid the migration of certain strategic industries from europe. finally, in addition to ambition and balance, feasibility must be a key consideration, as member states have different capacities. it is therefore necessary to ensure that climate objectives take into account the performance of member states and do not impose an excessive burden on citizens by incorporating appropriate flexibility tools.
my colleague mr bach has done an excellent job in the field of air passenger rights in recent years. i remember well a few years ago, we had a very difficult discussion about the rights of bus passengers, and it was difficult for me to explain that disabled people and people with reduced mobility are not the same. those are two separate categories. i would like to point out that the european parliament has now clearly recognised the importance of this and is handling the situation well, but the european commission is not. this had to be corrected in the report, for example, in relation to the commission's proposal. now i am pleased to see that in the area of disability rights, there has been a huge advance in passenger rights. providers, whether they are ground-based operators or airlines, have a duty to provide a quality service and to ensure a clear and transparent system of obligations and rights. i welcome the involvement of civil society organisations representing disabled people in this process and i welcome the fact that personalised services are required by law, because this is true equality of opportunity.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, i also thank my friend, our colleague, georg bach, for putting this very important report on our table. with air traffic increasing significantly year on year and forecasts showing that this will continue for decades to come, it is very important that we ensure that passengers are properly transported safely, comfortably and, perhaps most importantly, arrive at their destinations exactly as they are supposed to. it is therefore important to regulate the conditions for compensation to which passengers are entitled as strictly as possible in order to ensure that airlines take the safety and punctuality requirements of travel seriously. i think it's very important that the legislation requires that there be accessible points where passengers can file claims and live with that at airports.
i voted no to the report on the rights of so-called seasonal workers coming from outside the european union to a country in the union, which i consider to be as inhuman and hypocritical as the european union in general. what is it? the right to enter and reside in the european union is granted to workers who can be granted employment by an eu employer for nine months of the year. that's a lot of repetition. and since the principle of non-discrimination is a principle of the union, at least in principle, that worker from outside the union should be paid the same as if he were an employee from within the union. now, either that's a lie, because in reality, the person is a victim of total exploitation, or if not, then, i ask, why doesn't this particular employer choose employees primarily from within his own country, and secondly from within the european union, where we know that unemployment is high?
in writing. - i consider it important that today's proposal will make life easier for third-country workers who take up seasonal work in the union in the future. the acquisition of permits is essentially simplified, as the worker is granted both a work permit and a residence permit. the development of rapid and flexible admission procedures and the clarification of the legal situation of seasonal workers can also be a safeguard against exploitation, which has hitherto posed a serious threat to those working only seasonally in tourism, agriculture or horticulture. finally, i would like to stress that today's proposal will make it easier for transcarpathiaans living in ukraine and hungarians living in serbia to have access to seasonal work opportunities and the necessary work permits and residence permits throughout the union.
in writing. - given the enhanced vulnerability of seasonal workers to exploitation, it is important to have a clear and unambiguous legislative framework of rights and obligations, as well as effective mechanisms to enforce regulations. i would therefore like to welcome the fair compromise introducing a special procedure for third-country nationals applying to reside in the eu for seasonal employment and to define the rights of these seasonal workers. most importantly, the report introduces a fast-track admission procedure with a deadline of 90 days, based on a common definition and common criteria and also covers the re-entry of seasonal workers in a subsequent season, the protection of health, safety and working conditions, as well as the related duties of the employer. another relevant achievement is the reduction of bureaucratic procedures for both the applicants and the employers, with a simultaneous limiting of inappropriate restrictions and conditions governing seasonal work and assuring maximum safeguards for applicants to protect them from illegal forms of exploitation.
in writing. - i welcomed and voted in favour of the report on the ban on imports of atlantic bigeye tuna from bolivia, cambodia, equatorial guinea, georgia and sierra leone. it is important to note that the iccat measures to control the fishing and trade in tuna in particular of valuable species such as bluefin tuna and bigeye tuna and the fisheries and trade in these fisheries deserve increased attention, as their current complexity shows that illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing is still a significant factor in the atlantic fisheries for tuna and tuna-like species. first of all, i would like to point out that the regulation in question was born in the 1990s, when iccat was at the forefront of the fight against illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing, even before that term was generally accepted. at that time, iccat pioneered innovative measures and introduced a ban on imports from countries suspected of allowing vessels flying their flag to flout international rules and standards. iccat was the first or at least one of the first to blacklist vessels caught in illegal or unlawful fishing. i believe that this chapter, which is a key part of the international fight against undeclared and unregulated fishing, deserves a thorough analysis.
in writing. - i find the report supportive as it initiates the exchange of best practice in member states on natural disaster risks, geography and climate and draws attention to the fact that natural and man-made disasters require different types of insurance and belong to two different insurance markets and therefore cannot be treated together, even though in some cases human decisions increase exposure to natural disaster risk. the report also calls on member states and public authorities to take appropriate preventive measures to mitigate the consequences of natural disasters, which can be particularly useful in the event of disasters such as the red-soap floods or other natural disasters that have unfortunately already occurred in hungary.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the candidate, as i had the opportunity to see in person at the hearings of the committee on economic and monetary affairs his professional qualities and the important work he is doing as vice-chairman of the supervisory board of the european central bank in the context of the review of banks and the future supervisory role of the banking union.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the agreement on competition law with the swiss confederation, which includes provisions on the exchange of evidence obtained by competition authorities, which will help to ensure that competition rules are enforced more effectively. switzerland is the eu's third largest economic partner and the second largest recipient of foreign direct investment, and it is therefore of the utmost importance for the eu to address anticompetitive practices that hinder crossborder trade in order to facilitate foreign investment as soon as possible, including through this treaty.
in writing. - the european union has already concluded a number of competition law agreements with its main trading partners. the agreement with switzerland is broader than the previous ones, as it includes provisions for the exchange of evidence obtained by the competition authorities investigating specific cases. i support any solution which is a trick of the multinational companies and which can be an effective weapon against cartels and abuse of power, including this agreement.
in writing. - i welcome the proposal for european union cooperation agreements on the implementation of competition policy. it is important to note that following the competition cooperation agreements with the united states, canada, japan and south korea, the commission agreed an initial second generation agreement with switzerland. i believe that international cooperation is urgently needed to enforce competition rules, because i too believe that anticompetitive behaviour is a non-tariff barrier to trade. i also believe, on the basis of the above reasoning, that increased efforts to promote international cooperation in the fight against anti-competitive practices are essential for the regulation of trade and foreign investment and for the creation of a level playing field.
in writing. - i am convinced, in full accordance with the motion, that respect for human rights is the basis of the common values on which the european union is founded and that, under the treaties, trade policy should contribute to respect for human rights as part of the eu's external action. i fully support the aim of the treaty to establish uniform international standards at the highest possible level in order to contribute to international and regional peace, security and stability and to reduce human suffering. it is therefore imperative that the obligation for member states to establish national systems for the control of arms transfers, as set out in the report, is implemented. in my view, calling on the eu and its member states to provide support to third countries that need help in meeting their obligations and allocating €5.2 million to the voluntary fund set up by the treaty are necessary and effective steps towards greater security.
in writing. - the arms trade is the second largest business in the world, slightly behind the pharmaceutical industry. certain types of weapons, such as weapons of mass destruction, anti-personnel mines, have legally binding global restrictions. an international treaty to restrict the trade in conventional arms was developed and adopted by the un in april 2013. this report shall invite the member states to sign the relevant treaty. fortunately, there is still a minimum of national leeway, or at least the appearance of it, in international legal acts. of course, i support the fight against illegal arms trafficking, but are legally sold weapons more humane?
in writing. - i voted in favour of the recommendation for a council decision authorising member states to sign the arms trade treaty in the interests of the european union, as i consider it important to note that, unlike weapons of mass destruction and anti-personnel mines, the trade in conventional weapons is not regulated by a legally binding global instrument. the arms trade treaty aims to fill this gap by adopting the most stringent arms trade regulations. it should be emphasised that the att sets uniform international standards to regulate international trade in conventional arms and, to some extent, ammunition and arms parts and components. the treaty also aims to prevent and eliminate the illicit trade in such weapons. i welcome the fact that the treaty prevents the illicit trade in arms, international terrorism and organised crime and limits serious violations of international human rights norms and international humanitarian law. i also support the rapporteur on the importance of respecting the terms of the contract.
in writing. - the eu economy is the most open economy in the world. more and more people are trying to take advantage of this, resorting to dishonest practices. unfortunately, we are increasingly seeing imports that are being dumped or illegally subsidized. these prohibited activities cause serious distortions in the markets for raw materials and energy in particular. in my view, trade defence instruments should be designed to be able to deter all unfair trade practices. that is why i support the involvement of smes in the initiation of dumping proceedings. however, i do not agree with the exemption of goods on the road from the penalty duty. the anti-dumping and anti-subsidy duty should be extended to shipments on the way to the place of unloading and traders should be deterred from unfair practices. a lower level of the penalty duty corresponding to the injury suffered by the union industry and therefore not containing a retaliatory element should not be applied in cases where the sector concerned is directly exposed to a threat. this includes activities that could distort the commodity and energy markets and i voted accordingly.
in writing. - the eu is back on the horse. for the first time, it allowed, and even encouraged, the export of technological knowledge accumulated in europe to third countries in the interest of profit at all costs. now he's fighting the imports from there under a dumping regime. the history of western europe is one of extremes: slave labor, colonization, war. organic, ethical, sustainable development has no roots. i'm sure there's no intention. yet, to protect the few remaining jobs in my poor, eu-oppressed, colonized country, i must support this distorted solution.
in writing. - the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon on 1 december 2009 and the redefinition of competences contained therein require adaptation of european legislation, which entails a reorganisation of the competences conferred on the european commission under the previous comitology procedure. in no case does the adjustment imply the transfer of the co-decision powers of parliament, but on the contrary, in the case of the procedure for delegated acts, it implies the conferral on parliament of new powers of ex post control or even new powers of co-decision. at this stage, when parliament has to share its powers with the european commission or with the european commission and the experts from the member states, the question of the governance of the eu institutions needs to be thoroughly examined. moreover, although circumstances and the shortness of time may require a strong response and may therefore justify the conferral of powers on the commission also in respect of non-essential provisions, delegated acts may in no case alter the spirit of the act, which remains subject to the codecision procedure. this logic makes it all the more necessary for parliament and the council to be vigilant, since these institutions must limit potentially significant transfers of power through effective, systematic and effective controls.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the rapporteur's and the commission's proposal, in support of which i would like to state the following reasons. the report sets out some of the principles to be respected in line with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon on 1 december 2009 and the new definition of competences contained therein, in the light of which parliament exercises its scrutiny of legislative acts. first of all, i support the idea that the european commission should ensure that when adopting legal acts, it guarantees the safety of seafarers, chooses the least complex and most economically efficient scenario and takes into account the three pillars of sustainable development. i consider it of the utmost importance to point out that this adjustment does not in any way imply the transfer of the co-decision powers of parliament, but on the contrary, in the case of the procedure for delegated acts, the conferral on parliament of new powers of ex post control or even new powers of co-decision. i also welcome the point in the proposal which calls attention to the need for parliament and the council to be vigilant, since these institutions must limit the potential for significant power transfers through effective, systematic and effective controls. i am convinced that the proposed implementing acts on the marking and identification of vessels, fishing gear and watercraft, the proper installation and use of satellite tracking devices and the consideration of cost-effectiveness and efficiency are all common objectives.
in writing. - standardization is needed in all economic areas to achieve the unification of technical indicators. this is particularly true when it comes to occupational safety, and even more so when it comes to the regulation of activities in particularly hazardous conditions. the protection of my employees is of the utmost importance to me under all circumstances. to do that, you have to take on some extra administration.
in writing. - mining and certain industries routinely use explosives. there were regulations before. a provision has now been developed to facilitate a more transparent and uniform implementation of technical standards. such harmonisation contributes to raising the standard of protection of life and property. it also has an aspect of improving safety and working conditions. it is useful and appropriate and therefore to be supported. i don't feel that there's any danger of national jurisdiction being taken away.
in writing. - more and more people, unfortunately, are barely leaving their homes and surrounding themselves with all kinds of electrical and electronic devices. but that's what most of us are surrounded by in the workplace. well, what's electric, electronic is also electromagnetic. after you've worked for it, bought it, and brought it home, you want it to work properly and not interfere with the proper operation of other devices in your environment. this is indicated by the marking ce on it. i would strongly recommend that everyone get back to nature as much as possible, get back to the natural and avoid using unnecessary devices. but i also have to protect those who can't or won't make it happen.
in writing. - inconsistent implementation and enforcement of existing eu harmonisation legislation in the member states and complex regulation have made it increasingly difficult for both economic operators and authorities to interpret and apply the legislation correctly, leading to unequal market conditions and the placing on the market of unsafe products. i voted in favour of this harmonisation package because i am convinced that the merchandise package provides a comprehensive framework for the rationalisation of product legislation. the new legislative framework introduces a simplified regulatory environment for products and allows for a more uniform implementation of technical standards, which will help improve the functioning of the single market by ensuring equal treatment of non-compliant products and economic operators and equal assessment of notified bodies across the union market. i believe that the alignment of the nine product directives to this new legislative framework will also increase confidence of both producers and consumers, as it clarifies the obligations of operators and gives member state authorities more effective tools to carry out market surveillance checks, all of which will lead to the withdrawal of non-compliant and unsafe products from the market.
in writing. - the european parliament has done an excellent job in the field of air passenger rights in recent years. the hard work of the tran and imco committees has led to a significant increase in the protection of disabled people, for example in the area of passenger rights, and to their inclusion in a number of areas, taking into account their special needs. i voted in favour of the report. i think it's a worthy addition to the existing regulations.
in writing. - flying has become incredibly popular in recent years, thanks in large part to the advent of charter airlines. but this development was not without its wild spots. based on the experience of the last 10 years, it is appropriate to adjust the regulatory environment. the consequences of the bankruptcies of spanair and malév are also reflected in the material. the report shall include changes that are beneficial to air passengers in a number of respects, within a framework that can be considered reasonable. as with all positive things in consumer protection, i supported this. i have questions about the malév bankruptcy, but not in the context of this story.

in writing. the seizure of power by armed rebels last march triggered a serious crisis of confidence and a series of bloody clashes in the central african republic. the clash between the peaceful muslim and christian communities has cost thousands of lives. the seleka coalition, which came to power in 2009, has been led by muslim militants, many of whom are foreign rebels, who have targeted the country's christian majority, committed war crimes against them, destroyed their mosques and churches, and indiscriminately killed children, the elderly, women and men. thus, a veritable religious war has developed out of a bloody conflict rooted in social politics. only the reconciliation of the two communities can bring lasting peace to the region. i therefore welcome the decision of the european union to provide 500 troops to the un and african union peacekeeping forces. the unity of the central african republic will be threatened if we do not act immediately to end the rampant violence, if we do not protect the victims of religious hatred and if we do not achieve respect for fundamental human rights. i therefore propose that the eu provide a framework for religious reconciliation between the christian and muslim communities, contributing to lasting peace by promoting justice.
