in writing. while montenegro has already temporarily closed two accession chapters, bosnia has no chance of moving from one to two without a political and social consensus surrounding accession. the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, as a candidate country, has been waiting for eight years for negotiations to begin. i consider it important that the european union continues to support the western balkan countries in their preparations for accession. one good example of this is the fact that croatia handed over the full trial documentation to montenegro. in bosnia, active european mediation is needed to bring the constituent entities of the country to an agreement on amending the discriminatory constitution. it would be a great achievement of the current greek presidency of the eu to reach an agreement on the name dispute with the former yugoslav republic of macedonia. the situation of press freedom in all three countries is a cause for concern. laws severely curtailing freedom of the press and expression were passed in the former yugoslav republic of macedonia late last year. although parliament has since amended some of the contested provisions, the legislation still does not fully meet international expectations. it is also unacceptable that in montenegro intimidation and physical abuse of journalists is not uncommon and that the perpetrators are not held accountable. i ask the commission and the member states to take a firm stand in defence of press freedom and to make the countries concerned aware that full guarantee of fundamental rights is a condition not only for accession but also for the opening and continuation of negotiations.
madam president, despite the growing recognition of the problem from the global health community, as well as from national authorities, the figures remain staggering – both inside and outside the eu. this represents not only a blatant violation of the most fundamental rights of women and girls on multiple counts, but also a serious cause for concern regarding their health and a major barrier to women’s empowerment. the deeply rooted social traditions behind this harmful practice make it particularly difficult to address. it also calls for greater improvements in terms of education, prevention and dialogue with all stakeholders. several successful programmes, including those launched by the eu with unicef, show that a comprehensive approach, based on dialogue and backed by genuine political will, have produced encouraging results. now is the time to step up efforts and to incorporate the elimination of female genital mutilation into a broader action plan on combating gender-based violence. in this respect, and given the lack of reliable data at eu level, i welcome the fact that the eu institute for gender equality is developing resources and tools to tackle female genital mutilation. i also believe that we have to be extremely tough towards healthcare providers who engage in such practices, and that the principle of extraterritoriality applied in some member states – which makes it possible to prosecute the practice, even when it is committed outside the countries’ borders – should be extended. i would just like to thank commissioner füle for being here and for the excellent job he has done as a commissioner. i believe that he should come back because we need an engaged guy like him.
madam president, while we need more sustainable and diversified means of transport, improving the development of european inland waterway transport is crucial, especially for freight. in this respect, the naiades ii programme is essential. we know that this sector has largely suffered from the crisis and that the navigability of european waterways, as well as the development of efficient intermodal systems, can and needs to be improved. i share the views of my colleagues that, despite the interesting proposals included in the communication, a clearer and more comprehensive global strategy is still needed. bottlenecks along the inland waterway network need to be resolved. integration into a genuine intermodal system and access to finance and sufficient funding for projects from the connecting europe facility (cef) and horizon 2020 are also essential. dear commissioner, i strongly believe that an ambitious strategy for inland waterways transport can be a strong lever for green growth, innovation and development that would also boost regional and cross-border cooperation. let us work together to improve this programme and make sure it is fully implemented.

in writing. - i did not support the own-initiative report of parliament on the 2030 climate and energy package because the amendments adopted by the plenary resulted in a decision that does not take into account the competitiveness of individual member states, the achievements of the member states in terms of reducing emissions and the performance of european industry. in my view, the realistic regulatory path would have been to define a headline emission reduction target, which would have allowed member states the flexibility to implement it, including full autonomy in defining and shaping the energy mix, as proposed by the european commission. in my view, the best way to reconcile climate policy objectives with competitiveness considerations is to focus on the transition to a low-carbon economy rather than pushing for different solutions and targets, such as reducing emissions, energy efficiency and making renewable energy competitive.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposals for a framework for climate and energy policy for 2030. i am convinced that the eu attaches great importance to the climate objectives, sustainable development, security of energy supply, economic and technological competitiveness and the achievement of the single energy market. my view, which is consistent with the motion, is that environmental factors affect human health and that the objectives of the union's energy policy should therefore include the functioning of the energy market, security of (energy) supply, energy efficiency, energy saving and that the union's environmental policy should contribute to preserving, protecting and improving the quality of the environment. i also welcome the linking of climate and energy policies for 2030 to climate change for social and economic recovery and sustainable growth, and the careful consideration of pressing economic and social issues such as energy security, high energy costs for industry and households, the need for job creation and economic recovery and the transition to a sustainable growth model. i therefore fully support the measures envisaged in the motion, both in terms of environmental protection and in economic and social terms.
in writing. - as chief whip, my duty is to ensure the integrity of the people's party group's voting, which is reflected in the fact that i am the one who sets the position for the whole people's party group during the plenary votes. however, the hungarian people's party delegation is actively working to promote the interests of our country, and we represent our own national interests in the european parliament, so it happens that the position of the hungarian fidesz-kdnp delegation differs from the people's party position. in order not to be contradicted by my duty as vice-president in the vote on the 2030 framework for climate and energy policies, when the group voted differently from the hungarian members in the nominal votes, i did not participate directly in the vote. i did not press the ballot button, but i did raise my hand to indicate to the pp group the voting position that had been agreed upon previously.
in writing. - this report, which focuses on the fourth and final pillar of banking union, namely bank resolution, deserves our full support, as it is in the common interest of european citizens that the banking supervision system, the insurance guarantee systems and the european system of financial supervision should be replaced by a common resolution mechanism in the event of a bank failure. the forthcoming single resolution fund for banks, which is estimated to have €55 billion at the end of the 10-year transition, could be considered a well-founded reserve. the ten-year transition period may seem a bit long, so it would be worthwhile to build a separate financial safety net. we are not clear about that yet, so the framework remains the same. so in the event of a bank failure, the banks will first try to recapitalize themselves internally, then the national resolution funds will come, and finally, as a last resort, the esm will still be available, which will be able to recapitalize banks directly at some point in the future. the optimal solution would in any case be to create a fully autonomous, independent fund at the end of the 10-year transition period, to which the banks would contribute and which would operate at the rate of the contributions paid, relieving the taxpayers of the burden of the failing banks. the objective that should be set in the negotiations with the council is to separate the sick banks from the healthy ones and to protect the taxpayers.

the rapporteur. - this parliament adopts a great many reports, including many own-initiative reports, which are then virtually unread, uninformed and unaddressed by their authors. the report that's on the agenda now, i hope it's not like that. and not because it's some really great report or because i made it, but because it's actually a self-help book. a handbook for all colleagues who meet other institutions, the council, the commission, in the course of legislation, negotiations, or trialogue. the lisbon treaty introduced a new rule on the control of the european commission long ago, under which parliament acquired the same rights as the council when it exercised control over the commission. the old comitology procedure has been replaced by delegated and implementing acts. the commission's proposal is to be adopted by the council. what this means is that when this parliament adopts a legislative report, it normally loses its influence in the areas where it has delegated powers to the executive body, the european commission. that was the case in the past, but today our colleagues, the rapporteurs, the european parliament itself, can retain its influence within the framework of this procedure, within the details of the delegation procedure, and can withdraw this delegation from the european commission if it does not like some of the legislation. of course, the delegation is an important thing, because parliament cannot deal with every detail. we decide which issues we delegate to the european commission and which we want to deal with ourselves. of course, although the treaty of lisbon has long since entered into force, the full transposition of legislation in this area has not yet taken place. and my colleagues, when they negotiate with the council, with the european commission, when they consult with the trialogue, very often see that the council regrets having inserted such passages in the treaty of lisbon, that it has given parliament the opportunity to exercise control. this is often the case, but i think that in the context of cooperation, and we would like to see this report adopted not only by parliament but also by the council itself, we could finally establish a standard procedure whereby we do not have to re-establish the rules every time, because very often at the end of negotiations they say that these are technical issues, we should not deal with them now, we should not deal with these issues anymore, we should agree on them. parliament, and my fellow members, that is the essence of this report and that is what this report is giving guidance to my fellow members cannot give up its right of further scrutiny. he must insist on these matters in order to maintain control in the future. we must not adopt legislation which, and we will adopt tomorrow the three omnibus reports which the commission has presented to us, transposes these rules into the rest of the legislation, but we must not adopt legislation which does not comply with them. ladies and gentlemen, i would like to conclude by saying that unfortunately we have not been able to reach an agreement with the council, there has been no agreement at first reading in this case. that is a task for the next parliament.

in writing. the most important element of the european semester is based on changing economic strategies. this should cover employment, the economy and the budget. the only way forward is to promote the single market, economic growth, employment, job creation and reducing unemployment, rather than austerity, which is a hindrance to development. low wages and pensions are not going to get the economy back to growth. the european economy is still in danger, not in a shaken place. to be competitive, the eu must invest in research, education and new technologies. this requires the cooperation of all member states, not just the efforts of those in difficulty to put things right. each member state must share the burden. it is therefore necessary to establish a common european financing to repair the damage caused by the restrictions. it is important to strengthen the position of the parliaments of the member states in the european semester, in order to highlight the consequences of the social and economic crisis. this will contribute to sustainable economic growth, primarily through improving the quality of employment and raising social standards.
in writing. - four years ago, the treaty of lisbon entered into force, bringing major changes to the legal order of the european union. the treaty made the european parliament a co-legislator on an equal footing with the council and gave it a greater role vis-à-vis the european commission. parliament and the council, the two european legislatures, cannot deal with every detail on their own. however, parliament should have extensive powers of scrutiny in respect of the powers delegated to the european commission. the new legal system gives parliament very wide powers. we need to make it clear to the other eu institutions that the ep stands firmly for its rights and insists on maintaining control. this means that directly elected representatives can now, with the power of parliament and the power of legislation, control and veto the implementing decisions and decisions taken by the european commission.

in writing. - the community trade mark system, which promotes the differentiation of goods and services, is a successful european instrument that has been in existence for more than 15 years and should be revised in order to further increase its effectiveness. the agency, known as the office for harmonization in the internal market, is a key element of the system, with a continuously expanding remit. so we need to rethink the agency's governance rules, and we need to come up with a new name that's more user-friendly. the report also provides further guarantees of the agency's independence. it also provides for the possibility of reviewing the charges to be paid by users.
in writing. - i welcome the directive which seeks to harmonise the member states' freezing and confiscation systems at eu level with regard to proceeds of crime (e.g. forfeiture of valuables, extended confiscation, non-conviction based confiscation and third party confiscation) by laying down minimum rules. this will strengthen mutual trust and effective cross-border cooperation between member states in order to combat organised crime (including mafia-type criminal organisations) as effectively as possible. one of the most effective methods of law enforcement is to identify and confiscate the means and profits used in the commission of crime. in these cases, extended confiscation provisions prove to be a very effective tool.

in writing. - the commission has proposed merging the two eu agencies, europol and cepol (european police college), citing synergies and savings, but this reference lacks, in my view, sound and substantiated arguments. i myself am opposed to the merger of cepol with europol, as the objectives and tasks assigned to them by these two organisations are very different. it is no coincidence that the european council also did not support the merger and, by its decision last october, designated budapest as the next headquarters of cepol. hungary will provide highly favourable conditions for the efficient and sovereign functioning of the european police college. that is why i am pleased that at the libe committee meeting last week, a large majority agreed to the transfer of cepol to budapest.

in writing. - one of the main aims of the proposal is to make the european union more attractive to talented immigrants from third countries and to provide a legal immigration framework for the various third-country nationals (students, researchers, volunteers, trainees and au pairs). different national rules exist within the european union on applications for entry and residence permits, the harmonisation of which would facilitate and simplify procedures and safeguard against exploitation of certain groups such as trainees and au pairs. it is important for the european union to make better use of the potential of researchers and students from third countries by facilitating their employment in the union after completing their studies. this requires the introduction of a general rule, although in exceptional circumstances it should be possible for member states to take account of their national labour markets without jeopardising the right to work.

in writing. - violence against women in all its forms is a deeply condemnable social phenomenon which not only violates the human rights of the women and girls who are victims but also hinders the achievement of equality between men and women, and therefore it is of paramount importance and in line with the fundamental objectives of the european union to act against it. the report in question is therefore to be supported in its essential aims and as a whole, but the text adopted contains a number of controversial points. perhaps most importantly, it proposes that the council activate the so-called bridging clause, i.e. that it adopt a unanimous decision that violence against women and girls is also an offence under article 83 (1) of the treaty on the functioning of the european union. most member states - including hungary - criminalise forms of violence against women through the provisions of the criminal code, but in my view these do not fall within the classical 'cross-border' acts and therefore within the framework of eu criminal law cooperation. the abovementioned treaty allows the establishment of minimum standards for the regulation of particularly serious crimes which, by their nature or their effects, have a transnational character or for which it is particularly necessary to combat them on common bases. however, the vast majority of forms of violence against women are not.
in writing. - violence against women in all its forms is a deeply condemnable social phenomenon which not only violates the human rights of the women and girls who are victims but also hinders the achievement of equality between men and women, and therefore it is of paramount importance and consistent with the fundamental objectives of the european union to act against it. the report in question should therefore be supported in its essential aims, but the text adopted contains a number of controversial points. perhaps most importantly, it proposes that the council activate the bridging clause, i.e. adopt a unanimous decision that violence against women and girls is also an offence under article 83 (1) tfeu. most member states - and hungary too - have made the various forms of violence against women punishable by a number of provisions of the criminal code, but these provisions do not, in my view, fall within the classical transnational offences that are therefore to be prosecuted within the framework of eu criminal law cooperation. the abovementioned treaty allows the establishment of minimum standards for the regulation of particularly serious crimes which, by their nature or their effects, are of a transnational character or for which it is particularly necessary to combat them on common bases. however, the vast majority of forms of violence against women are not.
in writing. - four years ago, the treaty of lisbon entered into force, bringing major changes to the legal order of the european union. the treaty made the european parliament a co-legislator on an equal footing with the council and gave it a greater role vis-à-vis the european commission. parliament and the council, the two european legislatures, cannot deal with every detail on their own. however, parliament should have extensive powers of scrutiny in respect of the powers delegated to the european commission. the new legal system gives parliament very wide powers. we need to make it clear to the other eu institutions that the ep stands firmly for its rights and insists on maintaining control. this means that directly elected representatives can now, with the power of parliament and the power of legislation, control and veto the implementing decisions and decisions taken by the european commission. my own-initiative report is essentially a handbook for members of parliament to use to promote the interests of citizens.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report, as it rightly sets out the objective of further strengthening the cost- and non-cost-based competitiveness of the eu economy as a whole in the global economy, in particular by increasing competition in the product and service markets in order to increase productivity, resource efficiency and reduce prices. however, it is important to note that this process may be negatively affected by the public and private indebtedness of euro area member states; the community should therefore move beyond the growth model based on excessive debt accumulation and resource consumption. i agree that the main obstacle to growth (especially for smes) is the difficulty in accessing finance; therefore, new and innovative eu financial instruments to support smes' access to capital markets should be introduced and fully exploited, and the bureaucratic burden on smes should be further reduced.
in writing. - i welcome the fact that tackling the social consequences of the crisis is one of the five priorities listed in the annual growth survey for 2014. it is very important that the employment and social affairs sector is not overshadowed by the focus on fiscal consolidation and that the headline target of increasing employment is firstly kept on the agenda, and secondly, further promoted by investing in sustainable job creation. that consolidation measures will not have any further negative impact on people at risk of poverty and/or social exclusion must be ensured. the commission should put more emphasis on guiding member states in coordinating their national reform programmes with the goals of the europe 2020 strategy, in particular increasing employment and introducing effective incentives for work. the successful launch of the national roma inclusion strategies and its european framework is indispensable in this respect, since roma constitute a huge reserve of idle workforce and are disproportionately overrepresented among those living in poverty. increasing the employment of roma so that it would approximate the regional average would by itself trigger substantial economic growth that many countries hit by the sovereign debt crisis and with a significant roma population are so desperately in need of.
in writing. - the un predicts that the world's population will rise from 7 billion today to over 9 billion by 2050. to meet growing demand, fao estimates that food supplies would need to increase by 70 percent over the next 30 to 40 years. in addition to increasing production, it is necessary to develop crop varieties that can adapt to changing environmental conditions, provide sufficient yields and are of good quality. the research shall be extended to all plant species meeting the above conditions, not only to indigenous species. it is therefore necessary for the commission to allocate financial resources within the horizon 2020 research programme to adequately support research and practice in plant breeding.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the fourth railway package contains a number of important regulatory issues. i would like to make a few comments on the directive on the single european area. the directive provides for the complete separation of track operation and railway undertaking. i can accept the principle as such, but it is important to ensure the conditions for economic viability. there are two railway companies in hungary: máv, the successor to the former socialist giant, which operates the majority of the lines. for them, the time constraints of the transformation represent a risk, as the establishment of the holding structure is a time-consuming task. the company is much smaller than the company, which is affected by the planned quantitative restriction. of course, these problems are not unique to hungary, and that is why my colleagues and i have tabled an amendment to ensure that the operating conditions of the companies concerned are not made impossible.

the following item is added to the agenda of the joint debate on the following reports: report by olga sehnalová, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on the deployment of an on-board e-emergency system (com ((2013) 0316 - c7-0174/2013 - 2013/0165 ((cod)) (a7-0106/2014) and report by philippe de backer, on behalf of the committee on transport and tourism, on the deployment of an eu-wide interoperable e-emergency service (com ((2013) 0315 - c7-0173/2013 - 2013/0166 ((cod)) (a7-0482/2013)
mr president, i should like to thank the two rapporteurs for their work and also to say thank you for the good collaboration between the two committees, the committee on transport and tourism and the committee on the internal market and consumer protection. these reports conclude a long process for the harmonised implementation of an interoperable eu-wide ecall service, which has been on the agenda of the commission since 2005. it is a priority action for the improvement of road safety and the deployment of an intelligent transport system in europe. the comprehensive strategy is built on a threefold regulatory approach encompassing the in-vehicle system, the telecommunications network and the public safety answering points. ecall will therefore contribute to the new targeting introduced into the road safety action programme in order to reduce the number of injuries in europe. i would like to insist on an approach through this report that will ensure an eu-wide emergency system that is public, simple and affordable for all. for this system to be effective on time and in order to complete the implementation of the equal strategy, it is crucial that member states upgrade their public safety answering point infrastructure required for the proper receipt and handling of ecalls. it is a priority for us that psap points and ecall system are ready by october 2015. this is something that can make a real difference. it is effectively accessible to all eu citizens throughout the european union and i therefore call on the member states to swiftly act on the psap infrastructure. moreover, the effectiveness of ecall will also largely depend on the familiarity of eu citizens with the service, which is currently far from being the case. it is therefore of crucial importance that the commission and member states launch an eu-wide awareness-raising campaign to explain to citizens the functionalities of the new system. with ecall we have the opportunity to save lives and reduce injuries on european roads, so let us work together in order to make it a reality.
in writing. i welcome the fact that the european parliament is finally firmly in favour of fitting all passenger cars, regardless of make or type, with electronic emergency call equipment, so that citizens with various disabilities can use it without hindrance, in the same way as safety belts. the e-call issue is important to the european parliament and to me, because in 2011 it was precisely on the subject of the 112 emergency call that the overwhelming majority of meps were able to adopt the most supported written declaration of the current term, whose philosophy is in line with the report to be voted on. this is to ensure universal and unhindered access to services such as e-call for all. the work of the colleagues who produced the report deserves special thanks for this.
on behalf of the ppe group. allow me, ladies and gentlemen, to begin by congratulating my colleague werner langen on this balanced report and on the fact that it covers and represents the activities of all the stakeholders and interested parties in the insurance brokerage market. unlike the honourable member before me, i believe that it protects and extends consumer protection to the fullest extent possible, while also drawing attention to risk, risk sharing and the need to provide information at the right time and in the right place, before the contract is concluded. to be published and to be known. i also consider it of great importance that the public can be called upon to help in cases of interest and that member states can ask insurance intermediaries to make public information on insurance premiums, remuneration, commissions or other non-financial benefits. i am convinced that if similar rules had been in place before the crisis, this could have helped to avoid the risk of having to pay and being able to take out insurance that was liable to mislead consumers by the fact that there was possibly incorrect information available. i think it is very important to ensure that there is adequate training in this market and that there are requirements and that there is an online registration that allows you to follow insurance mediation in this way. it is important to simplify administration, i.e. to make the system transparent, and thus to increase trust. madam president! i think that this report is so balanced that it is in any case suitable for our support, and i would therefore ask my colleagues to vote in favour of this report.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! the quality and accessibility of healthcare is of paramount importance to all eu citizens. i believe it is important to invest as much as possible in prevention, because although it may seem more expensive in the short term, it will mean significant savings for member states in the medium and long term, while improving the quality of life of our citizens. however, i would like to draw attention to an unfortunate amendment which is extremely disadvantageous for countries with weaker economic performance, including hungary. under the european commission's original proposal, low-income countries, such as hungary, would have received 80% of eu co-financing in joint actions instead of the 60% co-financing normally available. this proposal was supported by the european parliament but rejected by the council by a blocking minority. unfortunately, the commission has changed its original proposal, so that it now proposes a compromise solution that is hurting low-income countries. we cannot support or accept this.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen of the house. i would like to draw your attention to a very important element of the report that we have before us, which is the project called the safer internet programme, which is about child protection and promoting safe internet use. i was very sad when, on a proposal from the commission, the funding for it development was cut from the previous €9.2 billion to €1 billion. at that moment, it seemed that this successful program, whose running and significance i could follow closely, because as vice president of the hungarian association of the international child rescue service, i saw it work. in hungary alone, more than 60 000 children have been trained in recent years and we have reached more than 200 000 children, which at european level means 2 million children in the 27 member states. i thank the rapporteur, mr tošenovský, and my colleagues for supporting my amendment, which will keep the programme alive and will enable it to continue, under the name 'better internet for kids', to protect the greatest number of children from harmful content on the internet.
, in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the report by mr evžen tošenovský on trans-european telecommunications networks is of great importance, as telecommunications and the internet are becoming increasingly important tools for services, education, society and business. in connection with the report, let me highlight a few points which i consider particularly important. first of all, i want to draw attention to the importance of designing a child-friendly internet. the report highlights the innovative safer internet programme, supported by the hungarian government, including safer internet centres in the member states, awareness-raising nodes, helplines and the child-friendly internet. secondly, the report focuses on consumer rights. ensuring that consumers can easily access and share the content, services and applications of their choice through a single internet subscription is essential for the achievement of the union's digital single market. thirdly, i consider it a great step forward to build up broadband infrastructure in rural areas and in economically and socially disadvantaged areas. this can help to improve communication, especially for people with reduced mobility or living in isolated circumstances. we can improve access to services, encourage the development of smes in rural areas and contribute to the creation of new jobs and services in these areas.
the committee on employment and social affairs has adopted an opinion. - madam president, members of the house. first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague chatzimarkakis on his very valuable presentation. it was a pleasure to be part of this joint work. my fellow officers! without wanting to use big words at this late hour, i consider that digital, information communication and internet accessibility are above all a moral issue. we are living up to the most basic rules of human decency when we make public services websites accessible to our fellow europeans with disabilities. in this respect, perhaps again not exaggerating this proposal is revolutionary. it's clear that everything has an impact on the digital agenda, in terms of economic life, in terms of social consequences. it also has an employment effect. this digital accessibility could change the situation where only 45% of our tens of millions of disabled european citizens are employed. for our disabled european citizens who are otherwise able to work. so i'm glad this presentation came to our table.
the committee on culture and education has tabled an opinion. - my fellow officers. the draft directive broadly supports the commitments of the member states to make their websites accessible. i want to highlight two things from my comments that have already been made, the problem of aging and the type of websites that are being circulated. the union's population is ageing. as a consequence of an ageing society, i think accessibility is becoming more important not only for people with disabilities but also for older people. in addition, the number of people living with disabilities may increase in europe precisely because of the ageing problem. secondly, i think it is important that the report completes the range of types of websites that member states should make accessible by making those websites that have cultural and educational content accessible. we have accepted the compromises, so i personally recommend that the report be adopted.
as president of the disability intergroup, i welcome the broad consensus that has developed in the european parliament on behalf of people with disabilities. as is well known, the level of accessibility of websites and various internet-based services is currently at an extremely low level, so action was needed. as negotiator in the committee on employment and social affairs, i and my colleagues succeeded in ensuring that the interests of persons with disabilities were taken into account in the regulation on the new cohesion funds, both at the programming and implementation levels. i think it is fair to say that the present draft regulation is the first practical implementation of this cohesion regulation. i think we need to move on this as soon as possible. i would like to call on the european council, as a co-legislator, to start negotiations as soon as possible in order to bring legislation to bear as soon as possible so that our disabled colleagues in the european union can have full access to services as soon as possible.
when most people hear about accessibility today, they still think about physical accessibility. however, web accessibility for the disabled and the elderly is also becoming increasingly important. today, it is the main source of information, it gives equal opportunities in administration, access to benefits, services, active participation in society, and the provision of independent living. it is important that accessibility to the web takes into account the needs of everyone who lives with a physical, intellectual or sensory disability that may limit their equal participation in society. i would like to draw your attention to our own responsibility. we must also ensure equal access to our own websites in the run-up to the european elections. the fact that we recently published information on my work in parliament on my website in easy-to-understand language has had a positive response. many expressed hope that this would find a following.
on behalf of the s&d group. we are pleased that a significant number of member states have taken their task seriously and have drawn up a national implementation plan for the youth guarantee. we urge member states to ensure that the programme does not only help young people in relatively good standing, but also those most at risk of social exclusion and poverty. it is also important that member states align the youth guarantee with other programmes that can be funded by the esf that aim at prevention, that they also finance programmes in the field of education and training that help to prevent early school leaving, that they support early development and education programmes and non-formal education and learning methods, that they make higher education accessible to disadvantaged young people, that education and training are in line with the opportunities offered by the labour market, and that there is a transferability of work and education. the commission is also asked to continue to monitor closely the implementation of the youth guarantee in the member states and to facilitate the exchange of best practices.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. it is a great pleasure to hear the report on the implementation of the programme, less than a year after the youth guarantee was adopted. one of the biggest challenges of the economic crisis is reducing unemployment. within this group, young jobseekers are one of the most vulnerable groups. the european union took a big step last year to tackle youth unemployment when it adopted the youth guarantee. the problem needs to be tackled proactively and the creation of jobs needs to be given a high priority in the member states. the implementation of the youth guarantee will require the cooperation of education and training institutions, employment organisations, youth organisations, trade unions and employers. the programme started at the beginning of 2014 and hungary will be able to support young people through, for example, training, retraining, traineeship programmes and measures to reduce contributions through employment centres. i suggest that we return to the question of implementation of the programme at another time to get more information on the successful initiatives of each member state.
in writing. – the financial losses due to the disengagement of young people from the labour market amount to hundreds of billions, which also represents a serious social and economic burden. enabling disadvantaged young people to fulfil their potential and participate actively at local, regional, national and european level is therefore not only an economic imperative and an essential factor for the achievement of greater social inclusion but also a necessity for the sound and sustainable development of their communities. it must be noted that, despite the economic crisis and the dismantling of workplaces, there are over two million unfilled vacancies in the eu, mostly because there are no workers with the required skills in the local job market. part of the solution for youth unemployment may be the development of high-quality vocational education and training systems, with highly-qualified teachers and trainers, innovative learning methods, high-quality infrastructure and facilities, high labour market relevance and pathways to further education and training. vocational education is more closely linked to the labour market than other sectors of education or training, yet the potential of work-based learning, and in particular apprenticeships, to tackle youth employment remains to be fully exploited in many countries.
in writing. – the epp group welcomes the european commission’s commitment in fighting youth unemployment. however, we deeply regret the delayed implementation of the guarantee scheme. as youth unemployment is a problem which concerns all member states, we find the exchange of best practice of utmost importance, and we support the commission’s guidance in this matter. however, we should not forget that employment policy belongs primarily to member states’ competence. the eur[nbsp ]6[nbsp ]billion dedicated specially to the youth guarantee scheme is a good start; on the other hand, it covers only one fourth of the funds needed, therefore we insist on the frontloading of the money, so that member states can practically kick-start the programmes to support youth employment. the epp group also regrets that we are still discussing plans, although we should be already discussing the member states’ experience relating to the implementation of the scheme. moreover, we would like to hear how the european commission is planning to make the rules easier and more streamlined so that relevant stakeholders can access the funds in the easiest and most efficient way.
in writing. i find it unacceptable that 11 of the 28 eu member states have still not submitted their operational programmes for the implementation of the youth guarantee to the european commission! how is it that 11 eu member states are not interested in reducing youth unemployment? it is commendable that the hungarian government has already prepared its operational programme, but the eu's 15 billion forints for seven years is not enough to help young people to thrive. to reduce youth unemployment in hungary, quality higher and vocational education, real internships and decent working conditions are needed. we must help every young hungarian and european, regardless of their financial background, to study and work according to their abilities and to live at a european standard of living! that's why we need to start a talent-building movement in our country and across the eu, to build a new system of people's colleges. since 2004, the mszp european parliament delegation has provided nearly 300 talented, often disadvantaged young hungarians with an opportunity to gain experience in brussels through the gyula horn internship programme and related training and after-school care. i hope that this practice will continue after the 2014 european elections.
in writing. - i supported the report because i believe that the single market should be defined as the third pillar of the european semester in order to have clear priorities for the real economy. i agree that country-specific recommendations should take into account the efforts and corrective actions already undertaken and completed by each member state and that national measures to implement single market legislation should not necessarily follow a universal approach but rather should be strictly adapted to the needs and specificities of the member states enhanced by the effectiveness of the measures taken and the tangible results of the policies implemented. it is clear that easier access to finance for smes would help alleviate liquidity difficulties and increase the working capital of smes; it is therefore of the utmost importance that the development of alternative forms of financing for smes is highlighted among the priorities identified by the commission in its 2014 annual growth survey. the report strongly supports eu-level initiatives to complement national efforts to increase micro-credit and boost social enterprise, including supporting development banks to offer loans at lower interest rates than those offered by commercial banks; in particular the importance of supporting smes through the cosme and horizon 2020 programmes.
in writing. - the european parliament adopted a report on the implementation of the cohesion policy programmes for the period 2007-2013 at its plenary session today. the crisis has made job creation a top priority. from 2014, more instruments will be available to support employment, job creation, in particular youth employment, and support for smes with significant job creation potential. hungary shall allocate 60% of eu funds directly to economic development between 2014 and 2020. under the operational programme for economic development and innovation, some huf 580 billion is earmarked for improving the competitiveness and employment of enterprises, and nearly huf 600 billion for promoting employment and developing the adaptability of enterprises.
in writing. - in the draft report by savisaar-toomast, it was stressed that the economic and financial crisis had hit the union's weaker regions particularly hard and that fiscal consolidation measures had reduced growth-enhancing expenditure while increasing the role and importance of cohesion policy. many of the less developed member states and regions are therefore heavily dependent on cohesion policy funding. i considered it necessary to add five motions for amendments to the draft report. i consider it important to emphasise that, given the high dependence of less developed member states and regions on cohesion policy resources, any potential macroeconomic sanctions should be preceded by a consideration of the negative impact of sanctions on member states' development. i emphasised that cohesion policy is more than a means to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy (as the draft report confirms). the objectives of economic, social and territorial cohesion must not be forgotten. i agree with the importance of multi-level governance, but the involvement of the regional and local levels should not lead to an excessive increase in administrative burdens. finally, i stressed that the european social fund can best contribute to the achievement of employment objectives when its use is combined with that of the european regional development fund. i am glad that other colleagues have also considered this aspect important and have, for example, proposed amendments to the commission to analyse the effectiveness of the european social fund and its impact on unemployment and job creation.
in writing. - disparities in development between european regions have increased as a result of the economic and financial crisis, and convergence regions are not able to overcome their difficulties alone. it is necessary to boost infrastructure investments, in particular through cohesion policy support, in order to tackle regional disparities and the effects of the crisis on groups at risk of poverty and exclusion. different approaches are used by the member states in preparing for the implementation of the future cohesion policy. it is equally vital, however, that member states and regions take the necessary steps to prepare the partnership agreements and operational programmes in a timely and efficient way so as to be ready to implement the funding as soon as possible, once the agreement on the common provisions regulation is reached. it is vitally important that projects can begin as soon as possible at the start of 2014 so that the use of european funding can be maximised in order to stimulate growth, and consequently social cohesion and inclusion, in the regions that are most in need, as well as to support transition and rce regions in their efforts to meet the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy.
in writing. - eu support to the outermost regions is not just about solidarity, but also helps the union to achieve its growth objectives by connecting these regions. while the remoteness of the outermost regions from the continent and their many geographical characteristics are major problems, the union should see an opportunity in this. the rapporteur considers that the union does not attach sufficient importance to the integration of the outermost regions. as the eu's main instrument of solidarity, cohesion policy should focus on supporting the regions most in need.
in writing. - the treaty allows the use of a new tool for final accounts, the so-called performance-based financial evaluation, in the various eu policies. the european commission is required under article 318 to prepare its annual report together with this evaluation. in this context, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control will in future also take into account the results achieved and the effectiveness of union policies when granting discharge. the parliamentary report calls on the european commission to prepare its annual report accordingly in the future. the report was adopted by a large majority in the committee and i supported the adoption of the report in the plenary vote.
in writing. - according to a report adopted by the european parliament today, e-call will be available in all new passenger cars from october 2015. today's decision is an important step forward for all european citizens who use cars, including those with disabilities. the equipment ensures that medical and technical assistance can arrive at the scene of an accident as quickly as possible, saving thousands of lives in europe every year. the e-call to emergency services is inactive in normal operation but in the event of an accident or collision it is activated automatically and notifies the nearest 112 call centre of the exact location of the accident and allows the 112 call centre to establish direct contact with the driver or passengers involved in the accident. this equipment has so far been found in less than 1% of the vehicle fleet. every year, 1.5 million citizens are involved in accidents on european roads and some 28 000 people lose their lives. preliminary estimates suggest that the emergency call could save some 2,500 lives a year. the cost of the application will be on average about huf 15,000 per vehicle.
in writing. - every year, one and a half million citizens are involved in accidents on european roads and some 28 000 people are killed. however, an electronic emergency call system would ensure that medical and technical assistance could arrive at the scene of an accident as quickly as possible, saving thousands of lives in europe every year. i therefore voted in favour of the proposal that from october 2015 the electronic emergency call system should be fitted to all passenger cars, irrespective of make and type.
in writing. - the european parliament voted against article 49 (3) of the agreement on political dialogue and cooperation, which deals with the mutual readmission of illegal immigrants. the readmission clause is an essential condition for cooperation with costa rica, el salvador, guatemala, honduras and nicaragua. these readmission agreements are an important tool for managing migration flows, which is why i support the report.
in writing. - article 145 of the treaty requires member states to develop a coordinated strategy for employment to ensure that all member states of the european union have a skilled, flexible and competitive workforce able to adapt to the challenges of the labour market. the report makes recommendations to the member states on how to increase employment and achieve the above objectives. the report's shadow reporter for the people's party was guard csaba. the report was adopted without a vote against in the committee, and i voted in favour of its adoption in the plenary.
in writing. - i would like to welcome the reinforcement of the 2010 employment policy guidelines that form a part of the eu 2020 strategy’s integrated guidelines. in cooperation with social partners, the member states must increase the employment rate through activation measures, in particular for the low-skilled and for people requiring particular protection or support, especially in marginalised communities, such as the roma. the support for social economy is very important in this context, and the member states must remove the barriers which make it more difficult for people to enter the labour market for the first time, support the creation of jobs, foster social innovation and increase the quality and effectiveness of job placement services, including public employment services. they must also actively support job seeking, also by introducing new forms of work organisation, such as atypical temporary work, part-time work and teleworking or homeworking, bearing in mind that any reduction in individual and collective labour rights and social protection must be avoided.
in writing. - i supported the report because i agree that the system for collecting the common vat should be harmonised at european level by means of on-line training materials and electronic vat return forms. the commission estimates that the introduction of the single tax return will reduce the administrative burden of vat collection by up to eur 15 billion per calendar year and therefore supports the intention of the report to reduce red tape and related costs for member states' tax authorities and taxable persons, thereby increasing the efficiency of vat collection. the single taxation system will not only improve the efficiency of the internal market in the future, but also significantly reduce the problem of tax evasion and avoidance.
in writing. - the proposal lays down the necessary internal measures to be put into practice between the eu and turkey for the readmission of persons residing without authorisation. the readmission agreement is linked to the visa liberalisation roadmap of turkey, which was signed and implemented by turkey on 16 december 2013. the obligation to readmit is also extended to family members (e.g. spouses, irrespective of their nationality) of its own nationals. the obligation on third-country nationals and stateless persons has preconditions that are applicable only three years after the entry into force of the agreement.
in writing. - the main objective of the fourth eu legislative package is to make rail more competitive and more attractive to citizens by opening up national markets. the most important part of the draft legislation, which comprises six pieces of legislation, would introduce the complete institutional separation of track operation and transport activities in the field of railway infrastructure management. however, the hungarian example also shows that flexible exceptions to the main separation rule should be built into the system. in the case of máv, the successor of the former socialist giant, the structural transformation is hampered by a number of inherited factors. given the much smaller size of gysev, it would be highly disadvantageous for operational reasons to operate separate management and separate services. i therefore voted in favour of the position that member states should continue to rethink the structure of the railway sector and, if necessary, change it within their own competence, or even switch to another model.
in writing. - i consider the report to be of the utmost importance and have voted in favour of it, since the transparent and correct functioning of the internal market and the insurance market is inconceivable without the precise content of insurance contracts, the persons involved and the precise definition and fixing of the individual premiums and charges associated with insurance. we have often seen in the wrong light insurance contracts concluded at the expense of consumers, in a non-transparent way or against very expensive commissions, or in cases where it was not always clear what they were. they were only good at making the average citizen skeptical and mistrustful of insurance transactions for life. the primary and perhaps most important aspect of this report was therefore to restore european consumer confidence. consequently, i can only welcome the fact that this report rightly targets forms of greater transparency and accountability, such as the online registration of insurance intermediaries, which is also officially supervised, or the fairness and non-misleading nature of the various marketing strategies provided to consumers. i also support the idea that all information relating to an insurance contract, such as the exact type of insurance, the obligations arising from the contract, the exact range of risks covered and not covered, and the level of premiums and the exact method of payment, should be transparent and should in any case be available to the consumer before the contract is concluded .
in writing. - i would like to welcome the fact that the budget for the european union’s upcoming action programme in the field of health available to national authorities, public and private bodies, international organisations and ngos has increased by 5.7% with regard to the current period. i truly hope that limiting the programme’s scope to areas where eu action can bring genuine added value in the healthcare sector will enhance its effectiveness and improve its performance. it is very important that granting access to healthcare for all eu citizens and reducing health inequalities by addressing the health-related, social and economic challenges remains a general objective of the programme. i would like to further welcome the proposal for developing comparable union-wide health indicators in order to help reducing inequalities in healthcare as well as the call for conducting gender-sensitive studies, analyses, investigations and sex-disaggregated data in order to identify, disseminate and promote validated best practices for cost-effective prevention measures addressing gender-specific health conditions and diseases. it is also very important to boost health promotion policies, as well as secondary prevention, including vaccination programmes, early diagnosis and appropriate treatment with special regard to marginalised communities and equally to promote health education and patient awareness.
in writing. - the european parliament voted today in strasbourg for legislation requiring that the websites of public sector organisations and organisations carrying out vital public services be made accessible to people with disabilities in the future. the report adopted today suggests that in the future, everything from websites providing certain basic services to the public (media prices, basic online banking and insurance services), to networked basic services (gas, district heating, electricity, water) and postal or transport services should be accessible to people with disabilities. the same should apply to online information on health and social care, but also to websites containing cultural or tourist activities. wider access facilitates access by persons with disabilities to news, cultural and entertainment content, and to services and information that facilitate the exercise of their rights and freedoms throughout the union. i voted in favour of the report.

the european parliament has repeatedly and categorically said no to this question. the luis michel report is the fifth parliamentary document calling on the european commission and the council to establish a community system of controls and sanctions to prevent serious and systematic infringements and to allow for effective, rapid and equitable intervention. the crisis has highlighted the interdependence of the member states in financial and economic terms and has forced closer integration. however, there is no effective economic union without closer political integration. the eu's inability to protect fundamental rights has a negative impact on economic performance and calls into question the union's credibility in international relations. we are convinced that the treaty still allows for a regular assessment of the rule of law and democracy in the member states and for more effective eu intervention in the event of systemic problems. international organisations shall regularly publish data and indicators which may serve as a basis for the annual evaluation of the fundamental rights agency. however, the development of the new copenhagen mechanism may require the treaty to be amended. we sincerely hope that the commission's forthcoming communication will propose all of this and that the council will have the political will to make the eu's core values a reality. in writing. - the common european sales right is an optional instrument to facilitate transactions between parties at a distance. the proposal provides for a european system of procurement rules which is identical in all member states within each national legal system, while retaining the procurement rules of each member state. thus, for example, parties to online sales are free to choose whether to rely on national sales law or on a european sales rules system that is the same in all member states. i am voting in favour of the creation of a 'common legal language' in europe which will greatly facilitate trade between parties with links to different eu countries and thus have a positive impact on european economies.

in writing. - solvit is a community problem-solving tool that contributes to better compliance with eu law on the single market by helping citizens and businesses to solve their problems. it also plays an important role in identifying obstacles to the implementation of single market legislation, joining the eu-wide range of assistance services and enforcement tools. despite the encouraged institutional efforts, solvit and the opportunities it offers continue to be underutilized by those concerned. solvit is a successful tool that has produced tangible results and the steps taken to promote it are therefore welcome and should be supported.
in writing. - i welcome the rapporteur's work to facilitate the work of judicial authorities in obtaining evidence in criminal proceedings involving several eu countries. the proposal allows authorities to request their partner organisations to carry out investigations and share evidence. for example, when swedish detectives are looking for criminals hiding in portugal, they can ask their portuguese colleagues to search the homes of suspects. the measures set out in the directive are of course accompanied by strict data protection requirements, with respect for fundamental rights in criminal proceedings, and in no case obliging member states to act in a way that deviates from the standards laid down in their own constitutions. the european parliament seems to have struck the right balance between effective law enforcement, effective judicial cooperation and the protection of the rights of the defence.

in writing. - the development and deployment of drones, i.e. unmanned aircraft for military use, in the eu is a pressing issue in the common european security policy. their application raises a number of questions, including which international conventions regulate the use of tools to modify the rules of warfare. the camp of those who advocate the development and deployment of drones has now grown quite large. they argue that drones are an established tool in modern warfare and are becoming indispensable in both military and civilian operations (peacekeeping, border policing, surveillance). the commission has also joined the ranks of donors by providing eur 315 million under the horizon programme for the development of paper-based devices for civilian use, but which are in fact largely for military purposes. the ep's report, adopted last year, focusing on the exploitation of the potential of a single european defence industry, underlined the sector's strong potential for innovation and growth. the most pressing issue for european drone technology is whether individual member states are willing to support efforts to bring the defence industry into the community. the creation of a european drone would undoubtedly point in that direction. in order to ensure the smooth functioning of the single market, the union's external action should be geared to the needs of the union's citizens and to the needs of the union's external action. the members agreed to develop military drones from 2020. their goal is to reduce their dependence on israeli and american defense companies.

in writing. - the protection and promotion of fundamental rights is crucially important for the whole of the european integration process. however, the proposed creation of a ‘new copenhagen mechanism’ – to ensure that the fundamental rights and the values of the union are respected – would seriously put at risk this objective, because it may miss the target by becoming a tool for power display and political hammering. the real objective is not to oppose individual member states, but to directly address the specific issues of human rights violations. one of the most worrisome of these serious human rights gaps is the situation of roma communities, for the inclusion of whom the eu has adopted the eu framework for national roma inclusion strategies in 2011, but without visible results so far. member states must effectively implement their national strategies to foster real inclusion, particularly in the field of fundamental rights, such as combating the segregation of roma children and their illicit placement in special schools. the commission must present comparable and reliable indicators to monitor progress in member states and to ensure, during the establishment of partnership contracts and the national operational programmes, that eu funds promote equal opportunities and prevent discrimination and segregation.
in writing. - today, the ep voted by a large majority on the future of the european arrest warrant. the european arrest warrant system, as it was known in hungary in the tobin case, needs to be improved and this report makes proposals to eliminate the backlog of irregularities in the application of the instrument. the most tangible achievement of the union for citizens is the freedom to cross borders without checks. this has brought with it a number of positives to the satisfaction of all of us. it also means that the perpetrators of crimes can cross the border without hindrance. this is why all community instruments are important to help law enforcement across borders and to assist the judicial authorities of the member states in tracing the perpetrators of crimes and bringing them to justice, regardless of where they are. it is in the common interest of all of us that the guilty should not escape accountability. it is also essential that member states should not be deprived of the opportunities offered by this instrument and that mutual trust between member states should be strengthened. the report on the arrest warrant, which has just been voted, highlights the weaknesses of the system and makes proposals to eliminate them, without jeopardising this essential mechanism of mutual cooperation.
in writing. - the european arrest warrant (eww) aims to simplify lengthy extradition procedures between eu member states, which it implements through a judicial surrender procedure, so that an eww issued by the judicial authorities of any member state is valid throughout the european union. since its introduction in 2004, the commission has produced three implementation reports. while the overall assessment is positive, deficiencies in the functioning of the system have emerged, mainly due to the unbalanced nature of the criminal justice system at union level. such cases may include, for example, excessive use of the epp by some member states, failure to apply proportionate checks or wording inaccuracies when initiating an investigation as to whether the requirement of suspicion and reasonable suspicion satisfies the requirement of 'reasonable presumption' when a criminal offence within the competence of the eppo is committed or has been committed. i support the commission's development of horizontal proposals on procedural rights of suspects and on the combined application of the various mutual instruments.

, in writing. one of the major problems with the commission's proposal for reform of plant propagating material law is that it would confer on the commission the power to adopt delegated acts on fundamental policy issues. unfortunately, experience so far shows that this type of authorisation is abused. the commission regularly and seriously exceeds its powers. this problem is frequently raised in the draft delegated acts concerning the common agricultural policy. the commission would impose new requirements which it is not empowered to impose under the basic regulations. what he couldn't get through the political agreement, he's trying to smuggle in by way of a loophole. the agriculture committee has already repeatedly listed the passages where the commission infringes union law. if the commission does not correct all the infringing provisions, parliament should veto the delegated acts. this is a clear violation of parliament's powers, and we cannot allow it! a prime example of this abuse of power is the case of wood-based energy crops. the legislation on direct payments clearly empowers member states to determine the types of trees that can be planted. therefore, the commission should not require in a delegated act that only native tree species may be planted. i demand that the commission immediately eliminate all violations.

on february 23, the ukrainian parliament adopted a language law. this language law seriously infringes the rights of minorities, in the sense that it allows the use of a minority language where the percentage of minorities reaches 50%. this new regulation, this new law seriously violates and restricts the rights of the hungarian minority living in ukraine. beyond limiting these rights, i believe it is contrary to eu standards. i find it regrettable that one of the first decisions taken by ukraine in its move towards the european union was to restrict the rights of minorities. i think that intervention is needed, i ask the president of the commission to convey my concerns to the ukrainian parliament, the legislature and the ukrainian government to legislate, to define a language law that is in line with eu standards. and i think that by addressing this concern, this request, we can create a better and more appropriate grammar.
mr wałęsa's excellent report also demonstrates that since 2009 the committee on petitions has become the conscience not only of the european parliament but of the entire european union. in recent years, we have had to give hope to citizens who have turned to the eu institutions for violations of their fundamental democratic rights. by abolishing the old-age and service pensions in hungary, tens of thousands of people were unjustly deprived. these cases are still pending. we also had to act on issues like the 2007 confirmation of the venice decrees, which stated the principle of collective guilt, or the slovak citizenship law. the eu cannot remain silent and inactive when fundamental rights are violated, whether it is the removal of acquired rights or the collective stigmatisation of the hungarian upland community. unfortunately, what mrs mcguinness has been talking about lately is that the european commission is not living up to the letter and spirit of the charter of fundamental rights.
in writing. – anyone can be a victim of torture - children or adults, religious people or atheists, intellectuals or uneducated people. i welcome the fact that the report refers in detail to the situation of children facing torture and proposes concrete actions to be taken in order to improve their protection. action by state governments is also indispensable. states should develop child-friendly justice systems that empower children not only to assert their rights but also to report violations. the member states can play an exemplary role in this field, as some of them have already implemented action plans, adopted or amended relevant laws, and developed special websites in order to improve their justice system and make it more child-friendly. i also welcome the fact that the text recommends that eeas and the commission pay special attention to individuals belonging to other vulnerable groups such as ethnic, linguistic, religious and other minorities, as these particular groups might be exposed to torture more often. torture needs to be eradicated. i hope that this current report will be another step forward in this global struggle and will positively influence eu foreign policy on this issue.
first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague, rapporteur balz, who has done an excellent job. obviously, we are talking about one functioning authority, two functioning authorities to be precise, and this report makes some very good and precise suggestions for changes in the powers of these functioning authorities. we all know that the financial crisis has highlighted that there are specific major shortcomings in these areas, and there are systemic problems that need to be regulated. i think that consistency is very important here and that we should be able to regulate these areas in a uniform way in all the member states of the european union. there is no doubt that there is a new need to clarify the role and the stability, accountability and transparency of these authorities. this, like the establishment of the authorities, was preceded by a very important preliminary work, which resulted in this report, which fits into this system. i also think it is important to stress that this report is not an end in itself, but that it integrates the work of these authorities into the real economies, where there is a real need for job creation and economic growth. i therefore believe it is important to establish a single set of rules which will serve as a basis for the enforcement and monitoring of these rules. i would like to highlight a few areas, the most important of which i think is the otc, the regulation of these derivatives transactions, which we have discussed several times in our committee. but i think it is very important that they are properly regulated and controlled and that they have higher capital requirements. the last thing i want to mention is the importance of integration, which should also apply in this area. mr president, my final point is that this report is about transparency, which is why i consider it of the utmost importance and will support it.

in writing. - i supported the report as it sets out the rules on the quality, timeliness, transmission and quality control of statistical data compiled and transmitted for the purpose of the procedure for the detection of macroeconomic imbalances and the prevention and correction of excessive macroeconomic imbalances in the union (mip data). i agree that clear requirements and procedures should be established for the early detection and monitoring of macroeconomic imbalances to ensure high quality statistics. however, it is particularly encouraging to follow the logic of the macroeconomic imbalance procedure in the development of this legislation. in this context, it is important to underline the need for full alignment with both regulation (ec) no 223/2009 of the european parliament and of the council of 11 march 2009 on european statistics and the european statistical system (ess) and the european system of central banks, which are both responsible for producing many of the statistics underlying the mip data. finally, i agree that the competent committee of the european parliament should be given the opportunity to invite the heads of the national statistical institutes to participate voluntarily in the hearings.
