in writing. - i supported this report because it stated that the financial crisis had exposed significant weaknesses in financial supervision, both in individual cases and in the financial system as a whole. the supervisory bodies were also needed because national supervisory models were not able to keep pace with financial globalisation and there were serious shortcomings in the areas of cooperation, coordination between national authorities, consistency in the application of union law and trust. the supervisory framework, which is also part of this report, had to be strengthened in order to reduce the risk and severity of future financial crises. it is appropriate to note in this material that, in addition to the establishment of supervisory authorities, it is appropriate to develop a single rulebook to ensure consistent harmonisation and uniform application of the rules. it is clear that in the post-crisis era, we must ensure that, for example, otc transactions are sufficiently deregulated. it is therefore appropriate to apply higher capital requirements to otc derivative transactions that could not be centrally cleared in order to adequately reflect the higher risks associated with them. furthermore, i am convinced that greater integration of european supervision is needed to ensure that supervision reflects the increasing integration of financial markets within the union.
in writing. - the 2008 financial crisis exposed a number of banking and investment speculation and fraud. anyone who is even a little bit interested in the subject can access more and more background information and wonder how money could have gotten so far away from its original function and become such a self-contained wealth-creating tool. it's not right in a world where maybe only three or four percent of money is doing its job. the report makes some progress in this area, i.e. in restricting speculation, and should be supported.
in writing. - this report is highly welcome and important as it sets rules for payment service providers to transmit data on the originator and the beneficiary at all stages of the payment chain, thus contributing to the prevention of money laundering and terrorist financing. in order to combat money laundering, which costs billions of euros each year, it is essential that the law enforcement authorities of the member states respond in a coordinated manner and that a standardised procedure for financial institutions and payment service providers is introduced. the purpose of this regulation on money transfers is to achieve the overall objective of combating money laundering and terrorist financing more effectively by increasing the transparency of all types of transfers of funds, both domestic and cross-border, thereby facilitating the tracking of electronically transferred funds by criminals and terrorists by law enforcement authorities. i agree with the commission proposal which aims to set up a system to record transfers of funds in any currency sent or received by payment service providers established in the union, including any information relating to the originator and the recipient of the funds. an important objective of this material is to ensure that payment service providers are not only suspicious of missing or incomplete information, but that their risk-based approach enables them to identify suspicious transfers and take appropriate action, including on transfers of unusually large amounts or executed in an unusual manner.
in writing. - i support the commission's proposal to improve the transparency of financial transactions by introducing clearer rules, while ensuring that the fight against money laundering and terrorist financing is not compromised while making it more effective. this is closely linked to the money transfer regulation, which aims to provide law enforcement authorities with access to the client data accompanying money transfers, facilitating the detection of terrorist funds. the nature of money laundering and terrorist financing has changed significantly with technological developments and the proliferation of new tools available to criminals. given the diversity of threats, the financial action task force (fatf) was created to prevent money laundering through banks and financial institutions. the aim is to develop a uniform international standard that does not differentiate between domestic and cross-border transactions. at the same time, the risk of illicit financial flows from developing countries, which may also target european and north american financial institutions, should not be ignored.
in writing. - the economic clean-up and the successive reforms of european financial supervision are unfortunately not enough when billions of euros are lost to money laundering and uncontrolled, dubious international movements of funds. the primary focus should be on prevention, in particular, in preventing the laundering of proceeds from transactions arising from various criminal activities through the international financial system. the report would improve the european regulation of money laundering and illicit financial flows in three ways. first of all, the functioning of the business registers needs to be improved, since it is indeed absurd that in a transaction we do not know the identity of the final beneficiary. i agree that it's a company's responsibility to know its customers and to know who the ultimate beneficiaries are. i also support the idea of somehow linking and making interoperable the business registers in the member states and making them accessible to the authorities and to the obliged entities. in any event, the precise risk assessment required to assess money laundering would require a comprehensive assessment covering the extent of money laundering, the risks associated with each relevant sector and the most common means used to launder illicit proceeds. the third part of the report, which is part of the holistic approach, deals with the prevention objectives, which must be targeted and proportionate, while respecting, of course, the eu legal order and the right of european citizens to data protection. for these reasons, i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - i welcome the commission's proposal to review directive 2005/60/ec to help fight terrorism and organised crime and to strengthen the eu's financial system. as money laundering is a well-known cross-border crime, it is essential that the eu plays a central role in efforts to curb illicit capital flows. one of the main challenges facing counter-terrorism in detecting terrorist financing is that of non-profit organisations, non-governmental organisations (ngos), which often operate under the guise of charity and channel large sums of money to terrorist groups. however, there is a significant difference between classic money laundering and money laundering for terrorist financing. the purpose of traditional money laundering is to funnel black money from a crime that has already been committed into legitimate financial systems. however, terrorist groups funnel money through legal or apparently legal entities to get the money to groups that can use it to finance their activities. the problem for prevention and detection is that very often they operate under umbrella organisations, but the umbrella organisation itself is legitimate. in order to prevent such situations, it is appropriate to require that non-profit organisations operate with the utmost transparency, use only official bank accounts and make transfers through verifiable channels. i therefore support strengthening cooperation between member states' financial intelligence units and making the risk assessment of money laundering at eu level clearer.
in writing. - you can only support it if you want to prevent money laundering and terrorist financing. meanwhile, the eu is also home to a number of tax havens and multinational companies, and the banking system is largely speculative. there are some limited results, but the real solution would be to return to the practice of regulating banks and other financial institutions that was followed for three decades after the second world war. commercial and investment banking should be separated, the megabanks should be broken up and the monetary system should be used for its original purpose.

in writing. - i welcome the report on the proposal for a directive on electronic invoicing in public procurement because i strongly believe that paper-free public administration and the use of electronic invoicing in public procurement is to be supported. unfortunately incompatible standards in individual member states as well as many different requirements, standards, networks and rules governing electronic invoicing are excessively complex for enterprises and give rise to legal uncertainty. in addition, they form obstacles in cross-border procurement procedures and therefore enterprises tend to avoid the hassle of doing such. for this reason i believe that these obstacles need to be removed in order to promote competitiveness and better functioning of the internal market. i entirely support the importance and relevance of the aim of this proposal which first and foremost is to solve the problems of interoperability in cross-border invoicing by developing a european standard. i find it necessary to highlight the fact that the directive does not impose a binding european standard but calls for linking the national standards by which it facilitates bringing these national systems more in line with each other. smes could particularly benefit from these simplifications.
in writing. - we live in a digital world, with all its benefits and drawbacks. most trade and services take place on the international stage in general, but also in public procurement. however, there are currently a number of legal requirements and technical standards for electronic invoices which need to be eliminated in order to facilitate cross-border payments. this technical regulation is therefore clearly to be supported and also helps to preserve forests. the icing on the cake would be to give the new member states a decent role in the procurement of the old member states.
in writing. - i voted for the eib's annual report, as the bank has contributed to europe's economic growth in recent years by providing extended loans to smes. it should be borne in mind that the eib's stated mission and primary tasks include ensuring the balanced and equitable development of the internal market with a view to helping to implement the union's priorities by selecting economically viable projects suitable for investment in the union. it is well known that smes, which account for more than 80% of private sector employment, are considered to be the backbone of the eu economy and the main driver of growth and employment in europe; i therefore welcome the fact that the bank's annual report places particular emphasis on promoting better access to finance for smes and also targets more than eur 19 billion in loans to them. however, the eib continues to pursue a risk-averse policy in its lending and thus limits the options available to potential borrowers. the lack of transparency in the field of credit facilities is another problem, in particular as regards information on final beneficiaries. therefore, i agree with the report that calls on the bank not to act as a commercial bank but to increase its internal risk-taking capacity, and therefore to identify and select projects with higher added value and higher risk, and to provide a comprehensive assessment and data on its various loan placements.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the report, which reaffirms that equality between women and men is a fundamental right enshrined in both the treaty on european union and the charter of fundamental rights of the european union. i welcome the emphasis on the need for further action, as there are still many inequalities between men and women, whether in women's rights as human rights, career and employment opportunities and pay, access to education and health services, participation in the economy and decision-making processes or political representation. i would also like to highlight the economic dimension of the problem i have outlined, as the european union is currently facing the most serious economic and financial crisis since the great depression of the 1930s, and i believe that gender equality is a key economic tool for promoting fair and inclusive economic growth. reducing occupational inequalities is not only an objective for equality but also for the efficiency and flexibility of the labour market. uncertain employment particularly affects women and this situation also leads to inequalities in access to public services. i believe that the european commission should step up its efforts to ensure that eu directives on equal treatment for women and men are enforced in all member states.
in writing. - eighty years after the first international women's day in 1917, the treaty of amsterdam in 1997 made gender equality a community objective. despite the many advances that have been made in the european union since then, and largely thanks to the union, these gains are too unevenly distributed among different groups of women, and millions of girls remain unreachable today. the number of women living in poverty has increased disproportionately compared to their male counterparts and women represent a majority of all groups at risk of poverty and social exclusion. women members of the continent's most populous and most marginalized minority, roma women, are the most vulnerable group in the european union, according to numerous studies. roma women are even more likely to be poor than roma men, and roma women who are otherwise disadvantaged, such as women with disabilities and the elderly, single or widowed single parents or those in foster care, are at increased risk of social exclusion and marginalisation. in addition, in disadvantaged rural areas, roma women often work in the informal economy, are not registered on the formal labour market, or work on short-term contracts, which raises serious questions about their social rights, such as their rights during pregnancy, maternity leave, pension rights and access to social security.

in writing. - the present and future of the eu's review of freedom of information and public access to documents has been discussed on several occasions in this parliamentary term. however, the positions were not converging: the majority of the european left wants to ensure full and practically unlimited publicity and access to virtually all documents of the institutions of the european union. as a former opinion-maker in the committee on petitions, i myself supported certain objectives, in particular the objective of ensuring transparency, since trust in public institutions is one of the most important indicators of a democratic society. the efforts to make the work of the eu institutions more transparent and to develop good administrative techniques can only be welcomed. however, the proposals, which are mainly from the left, do not contain specific guarantees and clear statements that the exercise of the right to information does not infringe on the one hand the right to privacy and on the other the protection of classified information whose disclosure would endanger the security of citizens. i believe that the overriding public interest cannot in itself override either data protection considerations or legitimate encryption in the field of law enforcement. in my draft opinion for the committee on petitions, i also sought to find a way of ensuring the desired balance between publicity, privacy rights and the enforcement of security in eu legislation.

in writing. - i welcome the report in the light of its emphasis on the absolute prohibition of torture under international law and humanitarian law, as well as under the un convention against torture. it also stresses that torture is one of the most serious violations of human rights and fundamental freedoms, causes terrible harm to millions of individuals and their families, and is not justified under any circumstances. i would like to stress the importance of the application of accountability and rehabilitation policies and support the inclusion of the three measures on the eradication of torture in the eu action plan on human rights and democracy, but specific and measurable benchmarks are needed to assess their implementation. i note with regret that the implementation of the eu guidelines on torture remains inadequate and therefore urge the european external action service (eeas) and the member states to give new impetus and priority to the implementation of the guidelines. i support more ambitious and concrete measures to eradicate torture, such as more effective sharing of information and burdens, and the implementation of training and joint initiatives, and believe it is essential that the guidelines are accompanied by detailed implementing measures, which are also communicated to eu heads of mission and member states' representations in third countries. i welcome the call for a regular review of the instruments used in torture or capital punishment.
in writing. - the un has taken numerous significant steps to prohibit torture and other cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment and to make the prohibition an international norm. such steps include the establishment of the committee against torture and the implementation of the un convention against torture and its optional protocol. sadly, despite all of this, the practice of torture continues to be practiced around the world. in 2012, the european union updated the guidelines on torture, which clearly commits member states to fulfilling international obligations to prohibit torture and degrading treatment in the fight against terrorism. however, it is a fact that torture is used as a method of interrogation by law enforcement agencies in many countries. support any eu effort or commitment to eliminate the practice of torture and other inhuman treatment.
on behalf of the ppe group. in an age where virtually everything we do, from making a phone call to paying with a card to sending an email or using social networks, is done online and involves the recording of our personal data, it is vital to adopt a regulation that meets the challenges of the age, that allows for opportunities but prevents abuse. i remember well the time when we were fighting for our freedom to not be monitored, to not have access to our data. grotesquely, a generation or two later, our young people today are doing this voluntarily. they make their personal information available to everyone. our citizens are not always aware that the issue of protecting their data, their rights, their right to be forgotten or erased, is as important as the issue of gaining our freedoms decades ago. we all have an interest in good, usable, practical regulation that helps to ensure the protection of personal data of individuals, but that does not hinder our small and medium-sized businesses and does not undermine the existing and well-functioning data protection standards in each member state.
i would like to quote the main finding, the first point of the chapter, from the so-called snowden report, or the surveillance scandal report, which is being debated today in the european parliament's plenary. the information available and the investigation provide convincing evidence of the existence of extensive, complex and technologically advanced systems developed by the intelligence services of the united states and some member states to collect, store and analyse, on an unprecedented scale, indiscriminately and without any specific suspicion, communications from all citizens worldwide, including content data, location data and metadata. my fellow citizens of europe! if anyone has had any illusions about so-called western-style democracy, i think they can forget it now. that was, by the way, one sentence from a 50-page report that i quoted, and if anyone has any illusions about the european parliament being more than some kind of umbrella organisation for various financial and economic interest groups, please look around this room. of the nearly 800 members of parliament who are debating such a significant report, we are again about fifty. this speaks for itself, especially in the light of the european elections on 25 may. i thought the mainstream parties would at least bring some representatives here for show, as a bio-scene, but even that was not considered.

blue-card question. – may i ask you why you are saying no? i mean, is this your concept of democracy, that your political enemies are not allowed to use the same kind of procedural rules as you do? if i may, i would like to tell you that, under the case law arising from the european convention of human rights and several other international obligations of european member states, people should not be extradited to countries where they might face either the death penalty or torture. knowing the united states’ practice, i think edward snowden would face both options: the death penalty and, being aware of guantanamo and all the rest, there is a good chance that he could face torture. so would that not be in the picture when we talk about the extradition of snowden?
the draft legislation to review and revise the single european sky rules also raises a number of important issues. i fully agree with the european commission that the current ses framework needs to be reworked as it is still not working effectively. the commission's proposal before us is flawed, however, because it clearly favours the larger operators and makes it impossible for smaller operators, especially those in the new member states, including air traffic controllers in hungary, to operate. the separation of air navigation services from supporting services is precisely the opposite of the objective of the ses ii plus concept. it leads to the further strengthening of the large providers that already have a dominant position on the market and thus to an increase in costs. it creates a barrier to entry that restricts competition in the long term, posing a risk from a strategic and even national security perspective.
, in writing. i would like to congratulate my colleague, mr marinescu, on the preparation of this report. we have had an excellent working relationship for many years, but i have to make my comments: the amendments proposed to the draft regulation clearly show a further strengthening of the commission's powers (prb, easa, possibility of delegated acts, performance plans, etc.) and the spread of industrial partnership concepts, which may create a disadvantage for hungary. however, there were two areas where partial backtracking was made from the commission's original proposals. on the one hand, the mandatory legal separation of support services has been removed from the text (although the concept itself has not disappeared), on the other hand, the rules on the involvement of airspace users have been relaxed, only consultation is required instead of approval. overall, the adoption of the sesii+ draft law could create a worrying situation, and i therefore urge all my colleagues to exercise extreme caution when voting on the report. long-term interests must be considered in the first place.

first of all, congratulations on your work, mr. mayer. we are talking about a very important new regulation for consumer protection, which overrides legislation that was adopted 14 years ago. the world has changed dramatically in the last 14 years. online bookings have become commonplace, with providers now offering not only accommodation and travel, but also a package of services, making it easier for customers to make decisions. however, this also entails risks, as service problems and customer complaints often present difficulties. the practical examples showed that when customers complained, the service providers tried to evade responsibility by pointing fingers at each other. the interest of european citizens requires us to make regulation clearer, more consumer-friendly, and to ensure that our citizens receive the services they buy at the quality they paid for. i welcome the legislative effort to promote this important consumer protection aspect.
on behalf of the ppe group. i am very pleased that after lengthy negotiations, a compromise was reached that is acceptable to all, and that will soon bring this important legislation into force. i thank the rapporteur, bas eichourt, for his willingness to compromise in all negotiations, and i thank my shadow-preservation colleagues for their cooperation. as the shadow rapporteur for the european people's party, i had a difficult task, as i had to bring together a number of demands from across the political spectrum, but i feel that i have succeeded. it was not an easy task, as the ambitious environmental objectives and the coordination of the needs of the market players required a great deal of coordination and a willingness to compromise on the part of all the players. we could not go beyond certain limits, because on the one hand, too strict regulation would have had a serious price-pushing effect, which we could not have caused to the population of europe, which was hit by the crisis. on the other hand, we also had to take into account the competitiveness of small and medium-sized enterprises, since in times of recession, every job is particularly valuable. you can see, ladies and gentlemen, that we have all been under a lot of pressure during these negotiations. allow me to mention some of the details of the new regulations. where possible, we have allowed for a reasonable period of time for all operators to prepare for the planned deadline for the introduction of market bans. we didn't put in a pre-load ban, instead we put in a tracking system to ensure that abuse is filtered out. we have not allowed the introduction of the allocation fee, instead the commission will prepare a report on the operation of the new system by 2017. so i think we've finally passed an excellent piece of legislation, and i thank you all for your cooperation.

in writing. - i welcome the report because i am convinced that ensuring the free movement of workers is one of the cornerstones of the development of a genuine eu labour market, as it allows workers to have more job opportunities and to make better use of their skills. i support the idea that workers should be granted freedom of movement to move to another member state for the purpose of work and to reside there for that purpose. i am pleased to note that on 15 december 2011 the european parliament adopted its resolution on the free movement of workers within the european union, which clearly calls for measures to ensure the application of this right to eu citizens in the interests of the union as a whole, with a view to accelerating the creation of the single market and the creation of the eu labour market. i also think it is important to highlight in the report the importance of transparency and the importance for both workers and employers of eliminating unnecessary administrative burdens when employing nationals of other member states. such simplification can not only make crossborder employment more efficient, but also ensure that both parties have a clear understanding of their rights and obligations, resulting in stronger and more effective legal protection. at the same time, i welcome the emphasis in the report on the need to promote equal opportunities across the union, which should be a priority when considering employment within the union.
in writing. - it cost billions of euros to build the satellite navigation system and it will cost taxpayers' money in the future. the benefits are uncertain, mostly in terms of independence from other external providers, or in terms of greater accuracy as a new technology. there would have been a better place for that money, but if so much was spent on the system, then the agency budget associated with its operation had to be approved with the appropriate framework. it is sobering, however, that the agency's headquarters is in a small country.
in writing. - i did not support the report because, while the majority of the proposals made to protect fundamental rights are correct, i believe that two factors, the increasing emphasis on security and technological progress, have combined to allow states to know more about their citizens than ever before. however, it is questionable where the healthy line is drawn between the protection of citizens' privacy and personal data and the free flow of information about them, a balance which i do not believe an eu-us framework agreement could comfortably guarantee. among the initiatives, i would also like to highlight the requirement to meet the criteria for the transfer of personal data for commercial purposes at the highest level of standards, which i believe is in any case in need of further specification. similarly, the protection of the rule of law and the fundamental rights of eu citizens (including from threats to press freedom), the right of the public to impartial information and the protection of professional secrecy (including attorney-client privilege), as well as the enhanced protection of whistleblowers raise additional issues for me, which i cannot support without a precise clarification and full protection and guarantee of fundamental rights.
in writing. - the european parliament is concerned that the nsa's mass surveillance activities could lead to sensitive data being transferred uncontrolled to us databases. many argue for the suspension of the conventions (tftp, pnr, safe harbour). the libe review board's summaries and the october visit of the libe delegation to washington make it clear that trust in the transatlantic alliance must be restored by strengthening economic cooperation and ensuring the right balance between data protection and the right to security. referring to the communications from the commission, europol and swift, there was no breach of contract with regard to the tftp agreement. the issue of intelligence and police cooperation, the latter of which has improved significantly between member states in recent years, should be given special attention. the commission has confirmed that everything in the pnr area is in line with the original agreement and that internal security controls are functioning properly. i am firmly opposed to the suspension of the pnr convention (the need for which is illustrated by the tragedy of the malaysian passenger plane which disappeared just a few days ago). the majority of the ep is also in favour of suspending tftp. i would consider such a move rash and irresponsible. the tftp is a key instrument in the fight against terrorism. since neither the commission nor europol are aware of any misuse of the information provided by the tftp, i would not consider it fortunate to have a sudden weakening of the fight against terrorism and the creation of unnecessary security risks.
in writing. - the hungarian people's party group voted today not to support the göncz report, as the proposals contained in it go beyond the provisions of community law, violate the principle of subsidiarity, but the report does not offer sufficient guarantees that the proposed new mechanism would be applied objectively, without double standards. such a mechanism would only be acceptable if it could guarantee that it applies to all member states under the same conditions, without applying double standards and without using actual policy objectives. in the last decade, there have been many cases of actual violations of fundamental rights, confirmed by courts, in some member states, such as the serious violations in budapest in autumn 2006 or the case of malina hedvig, which also involved violations against national minorities. in these cases, the european parliament never acted militarily against the offenders, nor did the european commission evade any investigation. since there is no guarantee in the report that it proposes the creation of an objective mechanism, there is a good chance that it could only be used for politically motivated attacks, as we have seen in the case of hungary.
in writing. - the principle of subsidiarity is the most important provision of the european union, and one of the main factors in ensuring its diversity and competitiveness. the report attacks this very point and, moreover, seeks to give the european commission additional powers without adequate guarantees and safeguards. the new mechanism proposed in the document is questionable as to whether it will be adequate, balanced and proportionate, given that in the past there have been many cases of double standards by the eu institutions in violation of fundamental rights. think of the serious and violent violations in budapest in the autumn of 2006. since then, the participants who acted on behalf of the violent organizations have been held accountable in court. interestingly, the recently adopted report on the state of fundamental rights in 2013 already states that police and riot police should be clearly and unequivocally identifiable, almost 8 years after the events in hungary. unfortunately, in these cases, the european parliament never acted, just as the european commission has always resisted such investigations. a mechanism is needed that is genuinely automatic and applies even to the largest member states, and not only when the interests of a selected minority or social group are affected, but that can be applied without exception and that offers genuinely adequate guarantees of impartiality of enforcement. that is why i voted against the report.
in writing. - i voted no to the report by göncz kinga on the evaluation of the judiciary, the state of the rule of law. i find it incredible, and i think it is a sign of the european parliament's disrepute, that a member of parliament with no expertise in the field, who was a minister in a government that attacked its own people in the autumn of 2006, and who used the judiciary for vile political purposes in defiance of the rule of law, should write a report on this subject.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the preparation of the full convergence of the audiovisual world. the regulatory framework of the audiovisual media services directive has so far provided for certain minimum rules at european level for classical (linear) television content and non-linear electronic information and communication services and online services. with the digitalisation of broadcasting and media technologies, the convergence of technical transmission systems and the rapid development of mobile and hybrid terminal equipment, traditionally regulated broadcasting and on-demand services are now competing with previously unregulated content from the open internet on the same screen. the majority of european consumers continue to receive audiovisual content primarily through traditional television sets, but there is a strong trend in the complementary use of mobile terminals. this raises consumer, youth and data protection issues. the problem is widespread, but younger users in particular want to access audiovisual content and electronic services everywhere, at any time and on any device. i agree with the rapporteur that the main emphasis is on market-based funding and access models, but i also welcome the european commission's green paper which provides a comprehensive overview of all audiovisual sectors.
in writing. - i welcome the report on the preparation of the full convergence of the audiovisual world, because i believe that the questions raised by the increasing convergence of traditional broadcasting services and the internet, due to the ever more rapidly changing market structures, must be answered urgently at european level. i support the promotion of innovation based on convergence, as new forms of cooperation between businesses and sectors are needed to ensure that the user can access audiovisual content and electronic services anywhere, anytime and on any device. i believe that, in line with the report, the key is to increase access and transparency, and therefore to ensure non-discriminatory, transparent and open internet access for all users and providers of audiovisual services. i think it is important to highlight the report's view that broadband internet coverage is an essential condition for convergence and innovation (especially in rural areas) and i support the proposal to ensure that users are provided with it. this shortfall can be addressed through short-term investment campaigns.
in writing. - they're dumping an unlimited amount of information on humanity, which is the most effective way to completely dumb it down. but the digital world is also an opportunity for valuable knowledge to be mined. that report emphasizes the importance of a free and open internet, net neutrality, but also the potential for abuse of market dominance by content providers, so i supported it. the web is just a tool, it's up to the people, the education, the parents, what they use it for and how much of their lives is wasted on it by the young and the old.
in writing. - the european union has declared 2013 the european year of citizens, with the aim of promoting the rights associated with eu citizenship. the objectives of the theme year included the organisation of forums where stakeholders could discuss what the european union should look like in 2020. in addition to the public consultations, citizens of the union have also objected in a number of petitions to the fact that discrimination against national minorities continues to be experienced in some member states. the possibility and impact of the punitive loss of citizenship on the status of union citizen in the case of dual nationals merit particular attention. it is common knowledge that, according to the case law of the european court of justice, the conditions for citizenship are governed by the national legal systems of each member state. however, it should be stressed that in order to guarantee citizens' fundamental rights and in particular legal certainty, closer cooperation and a meaningful exchange of best practices between member states in the area of citizenship rights should be promoted. in my amendments to the report, i therefore urged that the relationship between the concepts of citizenship and citizenship of the union be clarified and that national minorities be granted all the rights guaranteed by the charter of fundamental rights of the union.

i have to say, madam president, in the eurozone, the biggest risk is deflation linked to insolvency. this is a remarkable success for the troika. structural reforms cannot be introduced without side effects. deflation in the euro area is becoming more likely for three reasons: first, today's economic performance in the euro crisis countries is lower than before 2008. this huge production gap will only slowly close. structural reforms need time and pressure to succeed. a second reason is the relative adjustments required in the euro crisis countries. costs and wages must fall, increasing the risk of deflation. a third reason is the very limited capacity of the european central bank to improve financing conditions in a sustainable way. the most important asset of a central bank is its credibility. the ecb has earned this credibility. credibility can be damaged by wrongdoing, but it can also be damaged by inaction. it is therefore time for the ecb to act swiftly and decisively.

the next item is the statement by the vice-president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy on the union's priorities for the 25th session of the un human rights council (2014/2612 ((rsp))
it is astonishing how arbitrarily the european union has chosen to define the areas of human rights that are of paramount importance to it. i would suggest that one that the un has long treated as an absolute human rights issue, is known in the literature, in the english-language literature, as "land grabbing". the issue of land grabs as a human rights issue. this means that individuals and corporations in economically better-off countries buy up land in poorer countries, depriving the people of those poor countries or parts of them of their livelihood. this phenomenon has long characterized the third world, but unfortunately it also exists within the european union. my own country, hungary, is a victim of this. and i ask that the european union itself, in its legal disputes, treat this issue as a human rights issue, in accordance with un standards.
when my colleagues from the right, the left and the center sharply criticized the human rights defense of states that violate human rights, i remembered the biblical saying that if salt loses its flavor, what can you salt it with? the un and the european union will have an important task to find the salt with which human rights issues can be rationally sautéed. i have received five motions for resolutions tabled in accordance with rule 110 (2) of the rules of procedure. i'll close this discussion. the vote will take place on thursday 13 march 2014 at 12 noon.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, i welcome the report we have discussed today. i am pleased that we can finally debate a proposal aimed at achieving a high level of network and information security within the european union. i congratulate my colleague andreas schwab on the fact that he has managed to compile a dossier, proposing amendments to the commission's proposal that can improve legal certainty, strengthen the security and protection of individuals and privacy. its proposals will help individuals to control their personal data and to trust the digital environment. the report also helps to develop a culture of risk management and to improve information sharing between private and public actors. i welcome the proposals that require all member states to have well-functioning computer emergency response teams. however, i believe that the relationship between the new system and the proposed notification system under the gdpr may remain a serious problem. in my view, further clarifications are needed in this area. in addition to the above, the actual financial and administrative impact, including the total social costs, must of course also be taken into account. companies that produce their software by surface work cannot in all cases be protected by the clause in the user terms and conditions that they do not accept any liability for the malfunctioning of their software. they should be encouraged to ensure that the software they develop is safe and error-free to use. in my view, the key to the success of the directive is its ability to encourage active participation by market players. without it, you're doomed to failure. overall, i find the report supportive. i thank my colleague for his many years of work and i will certainly support him with my vote.
, in writing. as the people's party shadow rapporteur of the libe committee, i welcome mr schwab's report, which supports the commission's proposal for a high and uniform level of network and information security in the union. the report aims to ensure that operators of critical infrastructures are able to protect their systems against the latest threats. the operator of an energy network, a road network, a hospital or a banking system must ensure that the most appropriate solutions are used and that the equipment is always up to date. in the eu, cooperation has been voluntary, but has not proved very effective, as network and information security safeguards are struggling to keep up with the ever-changing threats. different member states have different capacities and preparedness, which is also a challenge. i support the rapporteur's proposal that each member state should choose the cybersecurity governance model that best suits it, but be careful to avoid duplication of institutional structures, which can cause parallelism and communication disruptions. i would like to draw attention to the fact that in most member states there are more than one cert (national network emergency response team) responsible for different events. however, the commission directive does not take into account the different nature and composition of the existing certs. it should also be ensured that the cloud computing environment provides competent protection for sensitive and critical infrastructure data.

- the honorable officer! my fellow members of the house of representatives! a united europe starts with the dismantling of physical barriers. the key to a competitive europe is the full digitalisation of the economy and society. a europe of solidarity will ensure that everyone has access to the internet. the directive before us aims to make the internet accessible, cheaper and faster for the masses and to encourage investment by telecommunications companies. it is in this area, with its fragmented market in global competition, that europe is at a competitive disadvantage, where falling behind can be fatal. the limited or non-existent availability of networks impedes competition and keeps prices high. it is safe to say that the single market is still incomplete in many areas. we can save 110 billion euros a year, and in the meantime, broadband can be one of the most important tools for social inclusion. nowadays, if you're cut off from the internet, you're cut off from society. we need to reach out to rural areas with lower population densities, so that digital assets can reach them and people in rural areas do not feel like second-class eu citizens who lack access to jobs, information, services, education or healthcare. broadband is not a miracle weapon, it is a basic need of the 21st century. what is the final debate today? the purpose of the bill is to reduce the cost of building high-speed internet networks. a lot of compromises, which i have to thank my co-tax partners for. after much deliberation and debate, we have the draft. the construction of physical infrastructure is the most important cost factor, the most important element of which is combining public databases and supporting joint investments of the different network sectors. this cost-effective digital infrastructure build-out can help us recover from the crisis. we need to make sure that the energy sector, the sewerage system, the water system, the district heating, the gas pipelines, the public transportation lines can be used to build pipelines cheaper and faster. we need to ensure that new residential buildings, public buildings in the future are able to receive and distribute broadband. buildings should be broadband certified to make it easier for individuals and families to find a property. my fellow members of parliament! we can't set ourselves any smaller goal than achieving 100% internet coverage. and we can set ourselves no less of a goal than to make the europe 2020 objectives - that every european should have at least 30 mbps and half the population a 100 mbps connection - a minimum programme. and it's important to me, and to my fellow speakers, that the moment we achieve that goal, we double down on our goals, because the speed of data is very important to be able to keep up with the demands of traffic. we can't hold it! we consider it a very great achievement that, in consultation with the council, we have managed to shorten the implementation period considerably. thank you to the partners and staff for supporting my work. i thank the council and the greek presidency for their successful cooperation. thank you for the flexibility and support of the commission, the commissioner and her staff. we can say that we have done our job in the interests of all europeans.
mr. president, i'm sure you are. my fellow members of the house of representatives! invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, invest, what is that? only through investment in broadband, high-speed internet, can we eliminate a two-speed, even a three-speed europe. because the situation is currently unacceptable, that if you travel within 50 km of any major european city - london, budapest, rome or madrid - the rural population can only connect on very low-speed or non-existent internet lines. we need to achieve a society, democratic institutions and jobs - and you have not questioned this - so that every european, whether living in the countryside or in the city, can access high-speed broadband internet services, both basic and specialised. so, to force the network companies to invest, to cooperate, that's our job, and that's what we have to do together tomorrow!
, in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the telecommunications sector is of strategic importance in the european union. it provides the necessary basis for development and innovation. the development of the telecommunications sector could be the way out of the crisis. it is clear today that europe has lost its digital competitive advantage over asia and the united states. so our main task now is to restore europe's competitiveness. the regulation mentioned by pilar del castillo covers several key areas. the single notification option for roaming, net neutrality and an umbrella organisation of national telecoms regulators in european countries will also help bring us one step closer to the single telecoms market. first, i would like to stress the abolition of the differential between domestic and roaming charges, which is a precondition for a functioning internal market. roaming charges would be abolished as from december 2015, which would be a significant step forward, especially in terms of increasing data traffic. secondly, the legislative package ensures net neutrality across europe for the first time. it also contains additional guarantees that oblige service providers to treat all internet traffic equally and without restriction, regardless of the sender and recipient, and regardless of the content. thirdly, i would like to draw attention to the importance of the uniform notification procedure. this possibility facilitates the provision of services in other eu member states by guaranteeing that national authorities treat all providers equally.
is the europe of the 28 freer, safer and more democratic than it was four years ago when we adopted the stockholm programme? yeah, well, it's not like the picture's contradictory. the number of people studying or working in another member state has increased, but some governments are trying to restrict free employment. we have concluded visa-free, visa-facilitation agreements with several countries, but bulgaria and romania's accession to schengen has been delayed ever since. the charter of fundamental rights is part of the treaty, but we cannot enforce it: we see that the independence of the democratic institutions can be destroyed, that the rule of law can be circumvented, that populism and nationalism are on the rise across europe. i am pleased that our common response at the end of the cycle was the eu rule of law mechanism, but it will also have to be applied consistently. i sincerely hope that the next european parliament will be equally committed to freedom, security and the rule of law.

, in writing. in this cycle, the eu has made a number of progress in strengthening security, combating terrorism and international organised crime, combating money laundering and corruption, and combating cybercrime. as a precursor to the eu cybersecurity strategy and the network and information security directive, i regard the establishment of the european computer crime centre (ec3) within europol as a major achievement. the operation that led to the arrest of 29 suspects is a success story for the center, which has been operating for over a year. the suspects misused the payment details of 30,000 credit card holders to gain 9 million euros. this also shows that it is relatively easy to get into the business networks of cybercriminals, and with the rapid development of technology, criminals are always one step ahead of law enforcement. children are also exposed to the dark side of the internet, and ec3 is currently supporting 9 major operations in cooperation with police authorities across the eu to tackle the sexual exploitation of children online. the extension of the mandate of enisa (european network and information security agency) for seven years and the extension of its tasks can also be considered as one of the key achievements of the last four years. the eu is thus committing itself even more to tackling cybercrime and to coordinating the work of law enforcement and data protection authorities.

- mr president, i would like to thank you for your kind words. as rapporteur for the final accounts of the european external action service, the european court of auditors and the european court of justice for 2012, i would first like to thank the european court of auditors, the secretariat of the committee on budgetary control, its experts and, of course, my fellow members for their assistance during the final accounts procedure, for the excellent cooperation. ladies and gentlemen, the european court of justice receives a record number of new applications every year. the main problem remains the significant backlog of cases and the continuing increase in the number of pending cases. the council may decide to extend the mandate of a judge to a further period of three years. my report urges the council to approve the pending drafts which propose the appointment of additional forensic judges and could contribute to a faster case-handling and a reduction in the number of pending cases. of the nearly 2000 staff at the european court of justice in 2012, almost half are involved in translation and interpreting. this is higher than for any other institution and leads to extremely expensive administrative costs. it would certainly be necessary for the european court of justice to develop some strategy to rationalize and reduce translation and interpretation costs in the future. there is also a significant difference between the costs of a page of translation calculated by the institutions. the european court of justice can translate a page for an average of €98, while the european court of auditors costs an average of €246. the final accounts report shall invite the institutions concerned to apply a uniform calculation method for the calculation of translation costs. the report calls on the european court of auditors to take better account of the professional priorities of the european parliament and the committee on budgetary control when preparing its annual work programme. my discharge report also indicates that there is a need to further strengthen the geographical balance within the court, in particular in the higher echelons, of countries that have been under-represented in the past ten years. i am convinced that the operational and personnel costs of the european external action service are disproportionately high. the european external action service has 514 staff at grade ad12 or above. that's more than half of all ad positions in the service. staff working in the delegations of the service represented an additional cost to the union budget of approximately eur 8 000 per head on average per month in 2012 in addition to their normal salary. among those in employment, the member states which have joined in the last 10 years remain underrepresented at managerial and above levels, at only 14%. of the 131 heads of delegation posts in the european external action service, only 20 are staff from countries that have joined in the last 10 years. in addition to the shortcomings and problems raised, my report aims to grant the exemption to all three institutions, as the financial problems were mainly related to the efficiency of management. as the european court of auditors stated in its report, the financial audit did not reveal any significant deficiencies or irregularities.

, in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the amendment of the ets directive to international aviation emissions is also of particular importance for market predictability and foreseeability. i find the text of the final compromise generally favourable and congratulate the rapporteur, peter liese, who has worked hard to achieve this result. the legislation, which was drawn up on the basis of consultations with the council, can be considered balanced, since it has solved the question of temporal scope, the question of the distribution of quota revenue and the question of territorial scope. the ets's aviation rules seek to reduce emissions in the fight against climate change, an objective that has been blocked from the outset by international protests, causing a lot of uncertainty in the markets. this long debate can be brought to a close by the compromise now reached, which provides a reassuring framework for the sector's emissions in europe until a comprehensive global agreement is reached. i therefore ask my colleagues to support the report in the vote tomorrow.
mr president my fellow members of parliament! i welcome the inclusion of the financial services report on the agenda. i am confident that the proposed conditions will improve the conditions under which the internal market operates. the adoption of a cap on interchange fees for transactions at union level is a very important step towards improving the functioning of the internal market and towards achieving a consumer-centred flow of goods, persons, services and capital within the union. it is, however, essential to provide the appropriate means to prevent the possibility of circumvention of the existing prohibition. this regulation should apply to all card-based payment transactions. including retail payment instruments and services and transactions with commercial cards. for this reason, we cannot support any amendment which would narrow this framework. thank you very much, and congratulations to the speakers.
in writing. i welcome the reports on financial services, as i am confident that the proposed regulations will improve the conditions for the functioning and development of the internal market for payments. it is certain that the adoption of a cap on interchange fees at union level is an important step towards improving the functioning of the internal market and towards the consumer-centred implementation of the principle of free movement of goods, persons, services and capital within the union. it is, however, essential to provide the appropriate means to guarantee the effectiveness of the proposed regulation, which will prevent the circumvention of the proposed prohibition. i am convinced that, in accordance with the principle of technological neutrality as set out in the digital agenda for europe, as applied by the commission, this regulation should apply to all card-based payment transactions, regardless of the environment in which the transaction takes place, including retail payment instruments and services, which may be offline, online or mobile, and transactions carried out with commercial cards. for that reason, i could not and cannot support amendments that would grant additional exemptions to certain types of cards. overall, the proposals put forward by the ep's rapporteurs are very balanced and well thought out, and i personally support them, and i encourage my colleagues to vote in favour of the reports on financial services tomorrow.

my fellow citizens! on 1 may, it will be ten years since the biggest enlargement in the history of the european union took place, with the addition of ten new member states. in my final speech to the house, i would like to take stock. for us, the central european and baltic states, there was no alternative to european union membership, there is no alternative and there will be no alternative. it was a tough fight, and for the better part of a decade, the union was in the midst of the biggest crisis in its history. we, the new member states, had to fight for equality. the success is demonstrated by the fact that six of the ten new member states are now members of the euro area. we have a lot of work ahead of us, the union can already deal with economic deficits, but it cannot yet deal with democratic deficits. but the process has begun and the european democratic control mechanism will certainly be established, as 40 million minorities want.
in writing. - special report no 25/2012 of the european court of auditors was voted on by the committee on budgetary control of the european parliament on 18 february 2014. the report was adopted by the committee without a vote against. as vice-chair of the committee on budgetary control, i voted in favour of the adoption of the report on the effectiveness of the implementation of the european social fund as a financial instrument. it is essential that the necessary lessons are drawn from the deficiencies and weaknesses identified in the audit and that they are corrected in the period 2014-2020. these necessary steps and measures shall be summarised in the report of the committee on budgetary control. i supported the adoption of the report in the plenary vote.
in writing. - the un's cooperation with the eu is key to peace-keeping and conflict prevention, especially in crisis zones such as syria, south sudan, the central african republic and the middle east conflict. the chemical weapons attack in syria in august shows that we must not give up on the goal of a world free of weapons of mass destruction, which must reflect the common will of all parties, based on reciprocity. with regard to unhindered access to humanitarian aid, i would like to draw attention to the threat posed by terrorist groups operating under the auspices of legitimate umbrella organisations, which is considered one of the greatest challenges in the fight against terrorist financing. unfortunately, nonprofit organizations (ngos) often operate under the guise of charity, funneling large sums of money to terrorist groups. in order to facilitate counter-terrorism work, greater attention should be paid under the un umbrella to making the activities of ngos more transparent (e.g. channels of money transfers should be controlled). citing the eu counter-terrorism coordinator, mr gilles de kerchove, there is an increasing risk of travel to syria by hard-to-identify 'lone wolves' and foreign fighters (including more than 2000 european jihadists). there are fears that returning fighters could become radicalised, which could increase the risk of terrorist acts in europe. the eu could play an important role in prevention by introducing a european legal framework to deter and deter those who travel to syria via routes that are circumvented, even if they have valid documents.
in writing. - i welcome the priorities set out in the proposal, the right to guarantee and protect freedom of expression and media throughout the union, as it supports the values enshrined in the eu treaties, namely human rights and fundamental freedoms, including freedom of expression. i consider it of paramount importance to draw attention to the fact that independent media support and reinforce citizens' respect for free expression, cultural diversity, diversity of views and non-violent conflict resolution, tolerance and respect as fundamental values. i support the recommendations to the council, the commission and the european external action service, stressing the importance of recognising the importance of free european media, in particular financially sustainable and independent public service broadcasting, in promoting the democratic values of the union and informing the public, in particular by helping to understand the issues that unite or divide europe and by bringing european citizens closer together through a better understanding of foreign policy developments. i agree with the rapporteur's suggestion that the european media broadcasting internationally should adequately reflect the varied opinions and circumstances within the union, including the perspectives and reality of the new member states.
in writing. - the eu has not yet had a comprehensive legal framework to enforce its rights under the world trade organisation (wto) and other trade agreements. this shortfall was exploited by several third countries. in many cases, the wto dispute settlement body's decision and international agreements were not implemented. it is in the interest of the european economy that the eu has a tool that strengthens the enforcement of the union's trade rights, consolidates the eu's credibility and ultimately convinces trading partners that they must respect the rules of the game laid down in trade agreements. the wto specifically recognises the right of the injured country to apply transitional retaliation of a protective nature against the infringing country if that country does not implement the decisions of the dispute settlement body. i therefore welcome the legislation on penalties for breaches of international trade rules. this was a timely creation. i also agree that services should be included in the union's trade policy measures, alongside goods and public procurement.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because, like the rapporteur, i am convinced that the overall objectives of sustainable economic development, decent working conditions and poverty eradication in bangladesh are priorities and that this regulation should be guided by these values. i consider it of particular importance to draw attention to the fact that the uruguay round is the basis for the preferential market access for products from the least developed countries, including the preferential import agreements for rice of 1994, and that the specific council regulation on imports of rice from bangladesh was adopted in november 1990. at the same time, i would also like to emphasise that, according to the rapporteur's interpretation, council regulation (eec) no 3491/90 does not currently affect the import agreements between bangladesh and the union. the rapporteur therefore calls for the harmonisation of existing legislation in the field of the common commercial policy. i would point out that the commission proposal mentions an amount of export tax corresponding to the reduction of the import duty to be collected by the exporting country as a condition for exporting to the eu. on this point, i think the rapporteur's position is to be supported, that he is not opposed to bangladesh levying an export tax, but he believes that this decision should be taken by bangladeshi policymakers and not by the european institutions.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because, like the rapporteur, i believe that the review of the current regulatory framework for medical devices is a priority, especially given the fact that the review of the framework, now 20 years old, was particularly timely and that the current legal framework has been improved in many respects. i welcome the amendments to the commission proposal to classify medical devices into four categories depending on the level of risk they pose to patients. i believe that this also strengthens transparency and accountability. i consider it particularly important to draw attention to the point in the report where it points out that a number of weaknesses in the conformity assessment procedure have emerged over the years, including a lack of transparency, the rapid approval and placing on the market of medical devices despite insufficient testing in patients and consequently insufficient clinical data and consequently the risk to patients . i am convinced, on the basis of the above reasoning, that medical devices presenting the highest risks to patients should be subject to a procedure more stringent than the conformity assessment procedure. i urge the report to seek the establishment of an acmd committee.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because, like the rapporteur, i am convinced that the purpose of this regulation is to guarantee high quality and high safety of medical devices and, at the same time, a high level of protection of the health and safety of patients, users and other persons. i welcome the priorities set out in the report, which are above all the regulation of standards for safety, quality and efficiency. i would like to stress in particular the importance of health and safety in the report, which states, inter alia, that: 'devices and manufacturing processes should be designed in such a way as to eliminate or minimise the risk of infection to patients, professional or lay users and, where appropriate, other persons. the devices must not cause injury to patients, users or others. i also consider it of the utmost importance to draw attention to the importance of training of healthcare professionals, so that healthcare institutions should ensure that their staff receive training on the proper use of medical devices, equipment and procedures in order to safely apply new medical technologies and surgical techniques. i support the need to ensure adequate protection for all healthcare workers through vaccination, regular diagnostic testing, and the use of personal protective equipment in order to reduce healthcare-associated infections and other adverse effects.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because, like the rapporteur, i am convinced that the applications that are part of the programme play a role in our daily lives, are vital to key sectors of the economy and contribute to our security. space applications and derived services, together with space research, have become critical for union policies and for promoting technical progress, industrial innovation and competitiveness. in light of our increasing reliance on space-based services, it has become vital for our society to be able to protect space infrastructure. however, i also think it is important to highlight the message of the report that space infrastructures are increasingly threatened by the risk of collisions between spacecraft and, even more so, by the collision of spacecraft and space debris, which is a serious threat. i welcome the priority of the report to establish an independent european space observation and monitoring capability to prevent and monitor the increased risk of collisions between space infrastructures and other spacecraft or space debris. i consider the progress made in the two flagship european programmes, galileo and copernicus, to be of great importance, as it has raised awareness of the need to protect the eu's space infrastructure. galileo is the eu's first major space project and remains one of the key building blocks of the eu's space drive, while copernicus has significant space-based units.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because, like the rapporteur, i believe that the four areas of animal health, plant health, plant propagating material and official controls are of paramount importance in the examination of the question of the legal framework for food chain safety. in addition, a high level of safety of food and food production systems, improvement of animal health and welfare, detection and eradication of pests and ensuring the effectiveness of official controls are equally high priorities. i welcome the report's commitment to simplifying the current legal framework as a key element in promoting food security. i would like to stress, in agreement with the commission and the rapporteur, that in order to assess more effectively the impact of food chain safety policies, it is necessary to define precise and consistent indicators. i also welcome the proposal for a total budget of eur 1 [nbsp ]892 million and call for the allocation of expenditure to be defined, as this will determine the priorities between the different parts of the programme. i also welcome the proposed rationalization of the funding ratio of grants and the fixing of the base rate at 50% of eligible costs. however, this percentage may be increased to 75% and 100% respectively under certain conditions.
in writing. - the regulation on clinical trials of medicinal products for human use fills a long-standing gap in the european union, which we hope will help to speed up and streamline authorisation procedures, thereby helping to develop new, effective products. it is important that ethical issues are included as a new element in the proposal, leaving it to the member states to regulate in detail the participation of ethics committees. i also consider it particularly important for hungary that the protection of vulnerable groups - minors, those with limited capacity for action, pregnant women and other groups in special situations - has been broadened, as the rules are already stronger in hungary and we have been able to maintain them. on these grounds, i voted in favour of the report.

in writing. - i welcomed and supported the report by a positive vote, as i share the same position on the current legislation on clinical trials. i believe that it needs to be urgently reviewed, given that over the past few years there has been a serious decline in the number of clinical trials being conducted in europe, which has not only serious economic consequences but also hinders the progress of medicine to the detriment of patients. i welcome the priorities set out by the rapporteur to ensure that a high standard of care and care for patients is maintained in the union, while encouraging scientific research and innovation. i also think it is very important to draw attention to the fact that given the current economic crisis, we should not waste money on medicines that are not effective and people should be able to make informed decisions about their health. europe must be competitive and attractive for pharmaceutical companies to conduct research here, while supporting scientific research and encouraging development of medicines for rare diseases. i am convinced that reducing bureaucracy and increasing transparency are also essential factors in achieving the objectives set out above.

in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary and then to the committee on budgetary control. following the presentation of the report, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union for the eighth, ninth and tenth european development funds in the context of the 2012 closure procedure. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the european development fund is the main financial instrument for supporting acp countries and octs. it is not part of the union budget, the member states finance the fund directly, and the european commission is responsible for the implementation of the budget. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the commission notes that the commission did not provide any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiaries. i voted in favour of granting the discharge in the committee vote and in the plenary vote on 3 april.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report because it confirms the need for urgent action to limit greenhouse gas emissions from aviation. the aviation sector is rapidly increasing its greenhouse gas emissions and, if no action is taken, the increase in these gases could hamper the achievement of the intergovernmental panel on climate change's 2050 reduction target of no more than 2 degrees celsius. i agree with the rapporteur that amendments are needed to ensure more ambitious targets and equal treatment with other industries, as currently the aviation sector only needs to reduce its maximum emissions by 5% and the other sectors by 21%. i am also convinced, in line with the report, that it is unacceptable that third countries such as china or india are unwilling to comply with the provisions of eu law on flights within the european union. on the basis of the above, i believe that when it comes to climate issues, it is crucial that all parties involved are team players, working together to achieve a common goal. in addition to achieving a global solution, the union must prioritise the interests, competitiveness and operational opportunities of our aviation sector in this highly competitive field.

in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary. the european commission's final report was drawn up by markus pieper of the people's party and contains a number of so-called reservations. some of these are also reflected in the annual reports of the commission directors-general and the german rapporteur has also made reservations of his own. the report also includes a commitment by the members of the newly established commission concerned, at their parliamentary hearing, to address the concerns relating to their areas of expertise. the report strongly criticises the eight member states responsible for 90% of the errors and irregularities affecting the eu budget. finally, the european parliament's plenary also supported the presentation of the commission's final accounts.

in writing. - the european court of auditors presented its annual report for the financial year 2012 to the european parliament's plenary. the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out the financial control of the general budget of the european union in respect of the european council and the council. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. in its 2012 annual report, the court of auditors concluded that, on the basis of the court's audit work, the payments made by the institutions and bodies in respect of administrative and other expenditure for the financial year ended 31 december 2012 were, overall, free from material error. the final accounts report also states that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. however, during the discharge procedure, the council did not provide parliament and the committee with a number of documents necessary for the closure of the accounts procedure, without which the financial year 2012 could not have been closed, and no agreement was reached between the two institutions on a number of disputes concerning interinstitutional cooperation. in the plenary vote, i voted in favour of postponing the granting of immunity.
