in writing. - the court of auditors considers that the european external action service has continued to implement its budget without material error in its second financial year of existence and that most of the problems identified in the court's 2011 annual report regarding the establishment of the service have not re-emerged. i therefore vote in favour of parliament's discharge to the high representative of the european union for foreign affairs and security policy in respect of the implementation of the budget of the european external action service for the financial year 2012.
in writing. - i welcome the priorities set out in the report, which relate to the simplification of beef labelling rules and the clarity and applicability of legislation on the identification and traceability of bovine herds. these guidelines are essential, in particular, to achieve an optimal level of food safety. i am convinced, in line with the report, that food safety is one of the issues of greatest concern to european citizens and that optimal protection for citizens must be ensured through provisions that are frequently updated and kept up to date. in particular, i would like to emphasise that the rapporteur welcomes the commission's proposals to introduce the electronic identification of bovine animals (eid) on a voluntary basis, on the grounds that this technology offers a number of advantages to users, in particular in terms of traceability and stock management. i agree with that argument and support it to the fullest. i also consider it very important to draw attention to the fact that i support the commission's intention to simplify the labelling of beef by abolishing the provisions on voluntary labelling, which, in addition to being a serious administrative burden, do not represent a proportionate added value from the point of view of food safety.
in writing. - the main problem for the court of justice remains the substantial backlog of cases and the continuing increase in the number of pending cases. the council shall, in accordance with the procedure referred to in article 25 of the statute of the court of justice of the european union, appoint the judges of the court of justice of the european union and the judges of the court of first instance of the court of justice of the european union. almost half (47.3%) of the almost 2,000 persons employed at the court of justice in 2012 work as translators and interpreters. this is higher than for any other institution and leads to extremely expensive administrative costs. the report calls on the court of justice to develop a strategy to rationalise and reduce translation and interpretation costs in the future. the discharge reports also indicate that there is a need to further strengthen the geographical balance within the institutions concerned, in particular the countries that have joined in the last 10 years are under-represented in the higher echelons, and the report urges the proportional representation of member states at all levels of administration. i voted in favour of granting the discharge in the plenary session of the european court of justice on the final day of the proceedings.
in writing. - the main problem for the court of justice remains the substantial backlog of cases and the continuing increase in the number of pending cases. the council shall, in accordance with the procedure referred to in article 25 of the statute of the court of justice of the european union, appoint the judges of the court of justice of the european union and the judges of the court of first instance of the court of justice of the european union. almost half (47.3%) of the almost 2,000 persons employed at the court of justice in 2012 work as translators and interpreters. this is higher than for any other institution and leads to extremely expensive administrative costs. the report calls on the court of justice to develop a strategy to rationalise and reduce translation and interpretation costs in the future. the discharge reports also indicate that there is a need to further strengthen the geographical balance within the institutions concerned, in particular the countries that have joined in the last 10 years are under-represented in the higher echelons, and the report urges the proportional representation of member states at all levels of administration. as rapporteur for the final report of the european court of justice, i supported the granting of the discharge in both the committee and the plenary vote.
in writing. - in its annual report for the financial year 2012, the court of auditors found that the court of justice of the european union had not identified any significant weaknesses in the areas audited related to human resources and procurement. however, we urge the court to find a solution to deal adequately with the systemic increase in the number of new cases and the workload, as internal reforms and the revision of the rules of procedure will not be sufficient in themselves to significantly reduce the number of pending cases at the court in the coming years. we consider it possible to reduce the number of weeks without hearings and consultations.
in writing. - the financial audit of the european court of auditors was carried out by an external auditor, which shows that the financial report for 2012 gives a true picture of the financial management of the court of auditors and that the accounts were found to be in order by the external auditor. it is welcome that the court has been able to significantly reduce the time needed to prepare special reports, from over two years in 2008 to just 20 months in 2012. the institution aims to reduce the average time taken to prepare each special report by one and a half years. the report calls on the court to take better account of the political priorities of the european parliament and the committee on budgetary control when preparing its annual work programme. the draft discharge report also includes the need to further strengthen the geographical balance within the institution, in particular the under-representation of countries that have joined in the last 10 years in senior posts. i voted in favour of granting the discharge in the plenary on the final statement of the european court of auditors.
in writing. - the financial audit of the european court of auditors was carried out by an external auditor, which shows that the financial report for 2012 gives a true and fair view of the financial management of the court of auditors and that the accounts have been found to be in order by the external auditor. it is welcome that the court has been able to significantly reduce the time needed to prepare special reports, from over two years in 2008 to just 20 months in 2012. the institution aims to reduce the average time taken to prepare each special report by one and a half years. the draft report calls on the court to take greater account of the political priorities of the european parliament and the committee on budgetary control when preparing its annual work programme. the draft discharge report also includes the need to further strengthen the geographical balance within the institution, in particular the under-representation of countries that have joined in the last 10 years in senior posts. as rapporteur for the final report of the european court of auditors, i supported the granting of the discharge in both the committee and the plenary votes.
in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary. as regards the management of the european economic and social committee for 2012, the final accounts report concludes that the financial controls were carried out in an orderly manner and that no deficiencies were found during the audit. so in the plenary vote on 3 april, i voted in favour of granting the discharge.
in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary and then to the committee on budgetary control. following the presentation of the report, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union in the context of the 2012 closure procedure, concerning the implementation of the budget of the european economic and social committee. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. as regards the management of the european economic and social committee for 2012, the final accounts report concludes that the financial controls were carried out in an orderly manner and that no deficiencies were found during the audit. the commission notes that the commission did not provide any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiaries. i voted in favour of granting the discharge in the committee vote and in the plenary vote on 3 april.
in writing. - in the context of the closure of the accounts for the financial year 2012, on the basis of the court of auditors' annual report and the recommendation to the committee on budgetary control, i am voting in favour of parliament's granting the secretary-general of the european economic and social committee discharge in respect of the implementation of the budget of the european economic and social committee for the financial year 2012.

in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary and then to the committee on budgetary control. following the presentation of the report, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the implementation of the agencies' budgets in the context of the 2012 closure procedure. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. as regards the management of the european union agencies for 2012, the horizontal final accounts report concludes that the financial controls were carried out in an orderly manner and that no deficiencies were found during the audit. the problems highlighted in the report and to be addressed by the agencies include the need to increase transparency in public procurement procedures, to make the conflict of interest procedure more effective and to further strengthen internal control systems. in the plenary vote on 3 april, i supported the adoption of the report.
in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary and then to the committee on budgetary control. following the presentation of the report, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the implementation of the agencies' budgets in the context of the 2012 closure procedure. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. as regards the management of the european union agencies for 2012, the horizontal final accounts report concludes that the financial controls were carried out in an orderly manner and that no deficiencies were found during the audit. the problems highlighted in the report and to be addressed by the agencies include the need to increase transparency in public procurement procedures, to make the conflict of interest procedure more effective and to further strengthen internal control systems. the committee decided at its meeting on 17 march to adopt the report and to close the agencies' accounts for 2012. i supported the adoption of the report in the committee vote and in the plenary vote on 3 april.

in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary and then to the committee on budgetary control. following the presentation of the report, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the implementation of the agencies' budgets in the context of the 2012 closure procedure. the panel recommended that the discharge of the body of european regulators for electronic communications be postponed. berec started its operations in 2011 and 2012 was its first full financial year. the first year of the audit revealed several procurement errors and weaknesses in the internal control systems. the riga-based institution was taken over by its hungarian director, lászló ignéczi, in january this year. the reasons for the postponement of the discharge were established long before he took office, in 2011 and 2012. the agency has adopted an action plan and is doing its utmost to remedy the problems identified by parliament, and the rapporteur acknowledged the efforts of the current management. however, the committee recommended a delay, and a new report will be prepared in october, a decision i supported.

in writing. - today i voted in favour of the report by my colleague petri sarvamaa on the discharge of the european police college (cepol). i would like to congratulate the director of cepol, mr ferenc bánfi, on the fact that the ep has today found no objection to the functioning of the institution in its report on the discharge of the european police college. the problems that preceded his appointment as director are now over. the institute has proved itself to be worthy and capable of playing an independent role as a community institution. this is also important because the inter-institutional conflicts of recent months have called into question the need for cepol. it is important that today's report indirectly confirms the need for the police academy (cepol) and europol to operate independently of each other. all of which underscores the importance of police training without compromising the work of either institution.

in writing. - the european court of auditors has prepared its annual report for the financial year 2012, which it presented to the european parliament's plenary and then to the committee on budgetary control. following the presentation of the report, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning the implementation of the agencies' budgets in the context of the 2012 closure procedure. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. as regards the management of the european chemicals agency for 2012, the final accounts report states that the financial review was carried out in a proper manner and that no deficiencies were found during the audit. the commission notes that the commission did not provide any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiaries. i voted in favour of granting the discharge in the committee vote and in the plenary vote on 3 april.

in writing. - following the presentation by the european court of auditors of its annual report for the financial year 2012, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning europol in the context of the closure of the accounts procedure for 2012. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the european parliament has approved the closure of the accounts of the european police office for the financial year 2012. the court of auditors has satisfied itself that the annual accounts of the european police office are true and correct in all respects as regards the financial position of the office for the year 2012 and that the underlying transactions were legal and regular. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. so in the plenary vote i voted in favour of granting the discharge.
in writing. - following the presentation by the european court of auditors of its annual report for the financial year 2012, the european parliament's committee on budgetary control carried out a financial audit of the general budget of the european union concerning europol in the context of the closure of the accounts procedure for 2012. the committee's primary task is to ensure and monitor the proper and efficient implementation of the community budget. the european parliament has approved the closure of the accounts of the european police office for the financial year 2012. the court of auditors has satisfied itself that the annual accounts of the european police office are true and correct in all respects as regards the financial position of the office for the year 2012 and that the underlying transactions were legal and regular. the final audit report concludes that the financial review was carried out in an orderly manner and that no major deficiencies were found during the investigation. the committee decided at its meeting on 17 march to grant the discharge, to close the 2012 accounts, and i supported the discharge in the vote in the committee and in the vote in plenary.

in writing. - the annual report of the european parliament's committee on budgetary control on the protection of the european union's financial interests and the fight against fraud will be drawn up in the context of the closure of the accounts for 2012. the proposal summarises the efforts made in 2012 by the european commission, olaf and the member states to protect the community's financial interests and to strengthen the sound use of community funds. the report shall also adequately identify areas where further action is needed and where action so far has not been successful. the report welcomes the fact that the european commission at the initiative of the european parliament has started the legal environment review for the establishment of the european public prosecutor's office, the creation of which will allow for a stronger response to financial fraud cases in the community. it also welcomes the results of the 2011 vat reform. the report also outlines the necessary changes to olaf's governance and urges the commission to further strengthen its action against cigarette smuggling. i supported the vote on the report in committee and in plenary.
the purpose of the soil is to produce food. consequently, no arable land can be used for the production of fuel. do you agree with my position? question no 2: a new term: 'fracking' means exploratory research into shale gas extraction. this is done by among others a multinational company in romania, where television footage shows people protesting for fear of their safety, exposed to police violence. what does the commission know about this, what does the rapporteur know about this? third question: is it by chance that this whole anti-russian hysteria, in which the european union and america are fighting together against the so-called anti-russianism, is it because the united states wants to export shale gas to the european union, my brother would ask, but since i have no brother, i am forced to ask this. thank you very much.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, my congratulations first of all to mr gualtieri for his report. i am happy to go along with most of what he has said, if not, indeed, all. it seems to me there will always be tension between politicians and lobbyists, principally because their objectives both overlap and converge. lobbyists offer information and politicians can find this useful, but that information may turn out to be slanted. the problems arise when either the information is dubious in some way or when the lobbying becomes intrusive and inappropriate. not least, lobbyists can be in possession of considerable financial resources, which can then pressure politicians. it is to tackle these potential and actual abuses that the transparency register – the subject of an interinstitutional agreement – seeks to develop a framework that will further clarify practices. lobbyists are expected to register, but this is not mandatory. in this connection it would make a major difference if the commission were to take steps to provide a proper legal base for the transparency register. all the same, the net is cast wide. lobbyists are expected to declare their interests and the identity of their clients. they should not become fronts, hiding whom they are representing, and they should not be intrusive. law firms should declare the identity of clients, but lawyer/client confidentiality is respected – and incidentally, regions do not, as such, fall into the category of lobbyists. there are carrots as well as sticks. registered lobbyists will be placed in a superior position to those not registered in terms of access and participation. in all, this report represents a very welcome step in the direction of greater transparency.
madam president, congratulations to roberto gualtieri for his excellent work. the perceived and often genuine lack of transparency of the european institutions remains one of the main issues raised by european citizens, one that contributes to the growing gap of distrust towards the european union and politicians in general. while an increasing number of issues are being regulated at eu level it is therefore paramount to ensure that activities of interest representatives are properly monitored. dialogue with and involvement of civil society is crucial in the work of the european institutions, and it has to be conducted in a transparent manner within a clear legal framework. in this respect the setting up of the transparency register has been an important step. it is now time to be more ambitious and to aim for the establishment of a mandatory register that would be joined by the council as well. this is why i welcome the position outlined in this report. we are now counting on the commission to provide an appropriate proposal, based on a sound legal basis, that would enable us to continue on the way to a more transparent european union for the benefit of all.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! from time to time, i wonder how politicians can make such evil and inhumane decisions that impoverish and make miserable millions of people across europe, while banks and multinationals flourish. now the austrian trade union federation and a few other organizations have released a scientific report that gives the answer. it's clear from the report that the decisions are not made by politicians, but by lobbyists, by those international financial interests that spend 120 million euros a year on lobbying. they have 1,700 paid lobbyists, and before anyone can say, "well, there are good lobbyists", they have seven times as many financial lobbyists as there are civil lobbyists, and they spend 30 times as much money on influencing politicians as the civil sector.
the interest is huge, 24 of the 766 members of the house are in the room, which is an extremely good ratio. it is also an important topic, because it is about enterprises and their ability to create jobs. it would be good to finally understand for the state and the leaders and the political decision-makers in my own country, hungary, that these are not two separate topics. it is not that creating jobs means that hungarians are completely deprived of their right to work, and that they are forced to work for pennies in the assembly plant of a foreign employer. it is about putting businesses in a position to provide them with opportunities, and it should be done, even if it is late, but from above to start a real, healthy, civilization in hungary. to this end, i would draw the attention of the heads of state to paragraph 54 of the report, where they point out that more needs to be done to provide clear and comprehensive information on funding opportunities to current and potential entrepreneurs. don't waste it and give your buddies the eu money, they give it to the entrepreneurs.
my fellow members of parliament! in the parliamentary elections on april 6, citizens of hungary again won a two-thirds majority for the fidesz and christian democratic people's party alliance. it is also unique in europe for a party alliance to win a constitutional majority twice in a row. with this majority, the hungarian electorate has most decisively rejected extremism, whether left or right. they rejected the incitement of hatred that focused on the country's international reputation instead of the real problems, and they rejected the populist propaganda that put the dangerous programme of leaving the european union under its banner. hungarian citizens have given the government the authority to continue to reorganize the economy and create jobs. continue to strengthen social security and reduce the burden on families. these are the most important problems of hungarian society today, and they are at the heart of our party's program. my fellow members of parliament! fidesz-kdnp is the guarantee of stability in hungary today and the only guarantee against extremism.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission is preparing a report on the implementation of the budget. we are discussing a very important issue in this high-profile debate. as a shadow rapporteur, i should like to address mr weidenholzer's report on the report on public utilities from the point of view of consumer protection. i think it is very important that this report, despite the initial difficulties, has been very well developed and that a good compromise has been reached, which is primarily in the interest of consumers in the long term. the rapporteur identified four main themes: telecommunications, postal services, transport and energy markets, which are sufficiently embedded and integrated into the internal market. i am pleased that the final conclusion was that, although these areas are very important, there is no need for common eu-level regulation, as sectoral regulations are adequate and can fulfil the role they are needed to fulfil from the point of view of consumer protection. i also think it is important to stress that regulation at european level would have raised issues of subsidiarity and parallelism, which would not be appropriate to raise now. the only area i would like to stress is the energy market, which the commissioner has also mentioned. significant efforts have been made to improve transparency, but it must also be seen that although regulation has been introduced here, competition is not at a level that would have allowed consumer prices to fall in a free market. it is therefore important to leave the member states' competences, for example in terms of regulation or even billing, and to obtain an overview of them accordingly. as for smart metering, a thorough cost-benefit assessment is needed. i am pleased that the rules that have been established in both areas have created something that provides additional security for consumers.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! access to affordable and high-quality public services for consumers is essential for the well-being of union citizens and the competitiveness of the economy. the prolonged economic crisis has left more and more people struggling to pay their high utility bills. in 2010, for example, hungarians paid the highest gas price and the second highest electricity price in the eu. thanks to the exemplary measures taken by the hungarian government, the cost of recession has been reduced by 20% in recent years. i would like to highlight three other important aspects of public services. first of all, i agree that the regulation of public utilities should respect national competences, as member states have different developed markets and different social policy objectives. secondly, i would like to emphasise the economic aspect of public services. the hungarian example also shows that reducing recession costs leaves families with more money, so the measures have not only a social policy but also an economic stimulus effect. thirdly, i would like to stress consumer protection. since public services are essential to meet the daily needs of citizens, particular attention should be paid to the rights of vulnerable consumers and affordability of prices.
in writing. - electric transport has not been able to take off in europe in the absence of a properly developed infrastructure. at the same time, infrastructure development has been held back by the very few electric cars on the roads. i voted in favour of the adoption of the alternative fuels infrastructure directive, which will help promote electric transport in line with the hungarian government's jedlik ányos plan. the expansion of electric transport is also of major interest to hungary because of its reduction in oil dependence and air pollution. the expansion of the paks nuclear power plant and the increase in renewable energy sources will allow the use of domestically produced electricity in transport to increase.
in writing. - one of the key objectives of the digital agenda is to make broadband available to all citizens. the deployment of high-speed electronic communications networks is particularly important to ensure internet access in sparsely populated areas. the legislation adopted today will make it cheaper and more efficient to build broadband internet. there are still many municipalities in hungary without broadband internet access. i therefore voted in favour of using existing public service networks to build up electronic communications networks, thus ensuring broadband access for those living far from large cities.
in writing. trust in public institutions is one of the most important indicators of a democratic society. the present and future of the eu's review of freedom of information and public access to documents has been discussed on several occasions in this parliamentary term. the principle of transparency extends to the decision-making and administrative activities of the union institutions in a broader sense, which is why the establishment, adoption and application of the transparency register will be an important milestone in interinstitutional relations. as a member of the committee on petitions and a former opinion-maker on the institutional processes for public access to documents, i myself have advocated transparency. the work of the union institutions should be made more transparent and efforts to develop good administration techniques should be encouraged. as a first step, it is welcome that the bureau intends to introduce a significant number of incentive measures to encourage participation in the transparency register. however, in addition to the public, the protection of privacy rights and the enforcement of security considerations must also be adequately and proportionately addressed by union legislation.
i was enriched by a good experience today, because i heard from the left-liberal side of the house that they were terribly concerned about the rights of hungarian and other eastern and central european workers. until then, i sat here for five years in this almost empty plenary chamber and said that hungarian workers have two options: either work at home in your western european-owned assembly plants for 200 euros, or go to your countries as economic refugees and experience the deprivation of rights that the representative of the juri committee spoke about. i have just one question, my fellow left-wing mps. where have you been for the past five years? why didn't you protect the workers' rights? and where were they when decisions had to be made about this? i'll tell you where they were. they were there, and they pushed the buttons when this colonial system was created, when this disenfranchisement was created. now you're up for election, all of a sudden you're protecting the workers. dogs are not bacon... in writing. the tragedy of the ship carrying african refugees which sank off lampedusa last october has drawn our attention to the fact that the existing eu instruments and mechanisms are still not sufficient to ensure effective and uniform coordination between member states to prevent deaths at sea. the problem of illegal immigration is more acute in southern countries such as italy, spain, malta and cyprus, but in line with the principle of solidarity and shared responsibility enshrined in the stockholm programme, we must monitor whether each member state respects the fundamental rights of refugees equally. the eu is not empowered to regulate search and rescue operations in a general way, as described in the eu treaties. i would stress that the proposed draft regulation is not intended to allow this, but only to regulate search and rescue situations for migrants arriving by sea in maritime operations coordinated by frontex. the creation of a general obligation would thus bridge and eliminate differences in interpretation and practice between member states in relation to maritime operations. i support the development of a union-wide maritime security strategy, as the union faces a number of maritime security threats (e.g. proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, smuggling of illegal weapons and drugs, potential for terrorist attacks against critical maritime infrastructure). you have to be prepared to deal with these dangers.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, yes, more than 3 million vehicles are transferred from one member state to another each year. it could be a simple registration register, but it's not what's happening. it's a very long process, five weeks, 400 euros, and we're talking about this tonight, that it's in the public interest, and we want to address this real need, and we can't come up with a completely simple regulation or directive that is just for simplification. of course, as my colleagues have already said, there are lessons to be learned from this process and indeed from the council's approach, but it is important that some progress has been made and that this is in the interests of consumers and citizens. i think it is important, of course, that taxation issues should be discussed, although, as president harbour said, it is clear that this step could have been taken in time and that the impact assessment could have been carried out, but it is also clear that some member states are perhaps short-sighted, perhaps looking at the substance of the matter, and see taxation issues as a reason for this. i see a tax issue that could be specifically aimed at tax evasion, tax avoidance, because there would be the possibility for some people with improper registries to evade tax. there's a reference to that in the report, unfortunately, a contradiction between the location and the location of use. we need to solve it, because i think this is an opportunity. it is important that transparency has increased, or can increase transparency, the registration of stolen cars can not happen, and i also think that there will be other benefits, because the car rental companies can also reduce the fee, and thus, for example, tourists can also reduce the rental fee. the report is very substantial in its entirety and it would be important to be able to implement it as soon as possible, if not already, at least during the autumn.
in writing. when we remember the first world war and the 16 million europeans who lost their lives in the bloodshed, we say no to the 'resolution of conflicts by force', to the violent change of borders, to the rhetoric of war, to nationalist and chauvinist sentiments, to the incitement of hatred that pits peoples and groups against each other, to stigmatisation, to exclusion. we say yes to reconciliation, to settling disputes through diplomacy, to solidarity, to democracy, to respect for human rights. we say yes to european integration, which has resolved centuries of hostility, fomented by narrow political elites who want to preserve their power at all costs, into a lasting peace. we europeans take it for granted that we live in peace. strasburg is now a subject that few people remember about franco-german reconciliation. but sad events of the past and present - the balkan wars, the conflict in crimea and eastern ukraine - also remind today's generations that peace must be fought for every day. despite all the rumours to the contrary, belonging to a community of europeans is still an attractive goal that brings together former adversaries and enemies. to live in prosperity and democracy is a goal that encourages the masses, the people, to stand up to authoritarian systems and leaders and, if necessary, to fight for their rights outside of parliament. as members, we tend to underestimate the power of the european community. but we can only maintain our attractiveness if we are ourselves true to our european values.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the past 100 years have shown that europe can only succeed if the nations of europe come together and imagine a common future. for us europeans, fundamental human rights have become increasingly important over the last few centuries, and i am convinced that we must not let go of the freedoms we have won in the future either. europe has had an unprecedented almost seven decades of mutual respect and peace. the eu has an undeniable role to play in ensuring that european societies can live freely today and that the situation of oppressed minorities in europe continues to improve. there are many who are desperate for this common outcome and would like to turn from openness to closure, to isolation. in 2009, i was elected to the european parliament as the candidate of a party that rejects european unity and the results it brings. today, as a hungarian, a european and a jew, i wish the peoples of europe that the european unity that the eu embodies today will continue to work for a long time, giving all people hope that there is a possibility of a world in which people can live together in peace and respect. with these thoughts, i bid farewell to my five-year term as a member of the european parliament. thank you for your attention.

in writing. - i'm glad that after a long time, this very important legislative issue has finally been brought up. i supported the report, if only because i consider it crucial to harmonise eu legislation on the obligations of economic operators. i consider that, to that end, the alignment of the decision and this regulation should be carried out as strictly as possible, that is to say, the text of the provisions already introduced in the decision and incorporated into this regulation should not be altered. i believe that special attention should also be paid to vulnerable consumers in the single market. moreover, penalties and fines should be simplified and targeted in order to ensure that the cost of market surveillance activities is borne by the largest part by the unfair traders.
in writing. - the report is linked to the commission communication entitled 'education openness: innovative teaching and learning for all through new technologies and open educational resources'. quality education is of particular importance in the light of the economic crisis and the high level of youth unemployment. the integration of digital competences into european education and training systems is a very important aspect of education development, enabling more innovative teaching and learning methods and facilitating the development of digital competences needed in the labour market.
in writing. - i support the report which will help to deepen the eu-south korea relationship, particularly in terms of preventing illegal migration. south korea is one of the eu's most important strategic partners in areas such as corruption, counter-terrorism and cultural diplomacy. the framework agreement on cybersecurity will be indispensable. south korea is at the forefront of cyber development, they have the fastest internet connection in the world, and they have one of the most competitive information communication infrastructures. like the eu, they are also threatened by cybercrime and cyberterrorism. it is therefore important that the eu works closely with south korea to build a secure global digital environment.
in writing. - the commission proposal states that council decision 2009/831/ec of 10 november 2009, adopted on the basis of article 299 (2) of the ec treaty (now article 349 tfeu), authorised portugal to apply a reduced rate of excise duty until 31 december 2013 to rum and liqueur produced and consumed in the autonomous region of madeira and to liqueur and liqueur produced and consumed in the autonomous region of the azores, in view of the small size, isolation and low level of automation of agricultural holdings and in order to compensate for the additional costs of transporting raw materials and putting equipment into service in these remote and insular regions. portugal has requested the extension of the authorisation until 31 december 2020, which is justified as the development of the outermost regions in question could otherwise be jeopardised, while the internal market would not be distorted by the extension of the authorisation.
in writing. - on 4 march 2013, spain requested the commission to prepare a council decision authorising spain to grant an exemption or reduction from the aiem tax for the period 2014-2020 for certain locally produced products in the canary islands, amending the list of products and the maximum rates applicable to certain products. since the measure has the effect of stimulating the economy and competitiveness without undermining the union legal order, i consider it to be eligible.
in writing. - given that the proposal in question aims to promote the competitiveness of the french overseas departments in order to compensate for their isolation, dependence on raw materials and energy, the need to stockpile more, the small size of the local market and the low level of exports, i suggest that it be considered. however, the commission should be allowed sufficient time to carry out its analysis and to submit a comprehensive proposal.
in writing. - since about one million eu citizens are posted to another member state each year, the issue of posting of workers is of crucial importance for the development of the internal market in the field of crossborder services. strengthening labour mobility between member states is particularly important in times of crisis, as it has a positive impact on employment and thus on economic growth in the union. cooperation between member states in this area should therefore be further strengthened in order to ensure that the rights of posted workers are adequately protected. the report was prepared in the spirit of enhanced cooperation and aims at improving the implementation, application and enforcement of directive 96/71/ec on the posting of workers in the framework of services.
in writing. - the european parliament decided to postpone the adoption of its final annual accounts for 2012 at the european parliament's plenary session in brussels two weeks ago because the president of the european parliament had deleted an article from the report that was unpleasant for him, citing the house rules. the procedure used by mr schulz has given rise to a great deal of debate among the political groups in the european parliament and the committee on budgetary control, of which i am vice-chairman, has asked that the report be put to the vote in plenary, together with the item in question. i voted against the report in the strasbourg plenary vote because, together with my colleagues in the committee on budgetary control, i consider the procedure followed by the president of the european parliament in relation to this report to be unacceptable.
in writing. - the tasks and the way in which the public employment services operate have changed as a result of the economic crisis and the unemployment that has followed. the commission's initiative to concretise the existing network of representatives of public employment services, which will allow a detailed comparison of the effectiveness of the labour market measures for which the member states are responsible, is therefore welcome. this will help to identify the strengths and weaknesses of each measure and will be used in the development of member states' strategies.
in writing. - the main proposals in the commission's new legislative proposal for the solidarity fund are: clarification of the scope of the solidarity fund, adoption of clearer eligibility rules for regional disasters, possibility of advance payments, shortening of the administrative procedure for the payment of aid, measures to support prevention strategies aimed at reducing the risk of disasters. given that the need for the solidarity fund has never been questioned, but only criticised for its inflexibility and slowness, the above amendments are therefore necessary in order to increase the efficiency of the fund.
in writing. - the central issue in the amendment of the honey directive was whether pollen was a natural constituent or component of honey. according to today's decision, strict food labelling requirements for gmo content cannot be applied to honey, which is contrary to the interests of beekeepers producing hungarian gmo-free honey and health-conscious citizens. i therefore voted against the idea that the union should favour trade with third countries, disregarding the interests of its own producers and seriously infringing the freedom of choice of consumers.
in writing. - the report by gál kinga is of particular importance because it clearly confirms that budapest will be the future seat of the european union police training agency. the adoption of the report and the strasbourg decision is a joint success for gál kinga and the hungarian people's party mps and a great recognition for hungary. it is important that this report in line with the earlier adopted decision on the european police office (europol) also opposed the merger of cepol and europol and confirmed the need for the two institutions to operate independently of each other. the report thus underlined the importance of police training, but did not jeopardise the work of either institution. i voted in favour of the report in the plenary.
in writing. - the european police college is a key institution in the community's internal and judicial sphere, and it is therefore important that cepol and europol can operate independently of each other. the institution is based in the united kingdom, but has to move out by september 2014 under a previous decision of the uk government. as the only new applicant member state, hungary is in an excellent professional, financial and cultural position and the budapest application has therefore deservedly won the unanimous support of the council. i voted in favour of the european parliament confirming the council's decision and allowing cepol to continue its activities in budapest.
in writing. - cepol started its mission as an agency of the european union in 2005 with the aim of helping to build links between senior police officers in the member states and to promote cross-border cooperation in the fight against crime through training. it is no coincidence that the european council did not support the merger with europol and, by its decision last october, designated budapest as the next headquarters of cepol. hungary has submitted to the council a comprehensive and professionally balanced offer to host the agency. hungary has been a loyal partner of the transatlantic alliance in the past, which has always recognised the high-quality professional work of the hungarian law enforcement agencies. hungary will provide highly favourable conditions for the efficient and sovereign functioning of the european police college.

in writing. - it is an undeniable fact that the use of new psychoactive substances poses significant social and health risks to civil society and can often lead to violent behaviour and crime. sadly, according to a 2011 eurobarometer snapshot, 5% of young people have used such mind-altering drugs. the eu must prevent these substances from being placed on the market and made available legally or illegally. the fact that most of these substances can be legally used by industry (e.g. chemical, pharmaceutical, high-tech industries) and are sold via the internet makes it difficult to act against them. member states have different ways of dealing with this dangerous phenomenon, which can result in these substances being smuggled from one country to another via the black market. i therefore support action at eu level to combat the spread of new types of psychoactive substances.
in writing. - i welcome the commission's proposal to introduce uniform criminal law provisions for new psychoactive substances, the use of which, like drugs, poses a number of risks to society. one of the most obvious means of distributing psychoactive substances is the internet. we need to fight against mind-altering substances that are being sold on the online market and produced in secret laboratories outside the eu (india or china), and are being sold under the guise of being legal. although each eu member state has the right to decide how to tackle the spread of both illicit and psychoactive drugs, national measures alone cannot be the solution for europe. i therefore consider it necessary, in addition to strengthening the effectiveness of law enforcement cooperation between member states, to introduce minimum uniform criminal law rules where there is no longer a geographical barrier to the trade in psychoactive substances.

in writing. – the message from voters to the italian presidency is clear: over the coming years, the shared priority has to be to create growth to reduce unemployment. in the previous legislature we drew the lessons of the crisis; this year, the italian presidency needs to get employment and social cohesion again. there are a number of major challenges that we need to face together: climate change, security in europe, the situation in our neighbouring countries, immigration. in addition, we need a europe that makes life easier for individuals and businesses. therefore, we need now a presidency which is able to focus on a limited number of strategic priorities: to strengthen our industrial competitiveness, move towards energy independence and build a digital content. i hope that the european parliament and the european council can work together to bring to a successful conclusion a number of the initiatives that the commission has launched but that remain to be adopted by the co-legislators.

my fellow members of parliament! during the past week, meps from the democratic coalition addressed a written question to the commission about the new advertising tax introduced in hungary, which is extremely restrictive of press freedom. the only unspoken but admitted purpose of this tax is to try to make rtl klub, the largest independent commercial television station in hungary, which is not yet under government control, impossible to operate. over the past four years, the hungarian government, abusing its two-thirds parliamentary majority, has built a pro-government media empire of a scale unseen anywhere in the european union, while imposing all sorts of punitive measures on the remnants of the still independent media. therefore, we can say that, unfortunately, as a result of all these measures, it is no longer possible to speak of an independent press in the european sense, of press freedom in hungary. for all these reasons, i have asked the european commission, and i ask it again, to launch an investigation into the advertising tax in hungary, to stop it from taking its place in the european union and to take effective measures to restore freedom of the press in hungary.

that's a two-card answer. i do not think that the two should be treated together. so the hungarian government's position, and the fidesz government's position, is clearly in favor of ukraine's sovereignty and support. the other issue, which is an economic issue, is a completely different matter.
this is the free trade agreement between the european union and the united states, it's a beautifully packaged but very harmful commodity. the nice packaging means that there is a need for job creation, whereas there is absolutely no evidence that a free trade agreement like this would create a mass of jobs. in fact, there are examples to the contrary. the real goal is to advance the interests of global corporations over national governments. american corporations can sue national governments in private courts of their choice if their decisions harm their profit interests. the further liberalisation of food trade, the risk of the introduction of genetically modified products, is particularly dangerous. this partnership agreement infringes the sovereignty of the member states and even of the union, and is not in the interests of the european population, and therefore it should not be concluded.
i'm not one of those who are against something that they don't know, but i'm not one of those who are for it. i'm going to talk about the basic conditions for a possible future grant. the goal of the transatlantic free trade negotiations is to increase competitiveness so that more jobs are created. small businesses should also benefit from this contract. to be able to compete with their american counterparts, european companies need to reduce energy prices. there's no way that's going to work with current energy prices. if the market cannot do this on its own, then, as in the case of the hungarian rescission, the regulatory authorities, the state and the union must also intervene. we did that with the roaming charges. the conclusion of the convention must not jeopardise human health, the environment or the interests of consumers. poultry washed with chlorine or gmo is not. it is also essential for support to be provided to member states, the european parliament and all those involved. not only transatlantic, but transparent from start to finish. because what we don't know enough about, we can't responsibly support or oppose.

, in writing. the economic, financial and social crisis has had a very negative impact on employment, especially for young people. in the eu-28, unemployment was 10.4% in april 2014, 22.5% for young people and in some member states it was over 50%. 7.5 million europeans under 25 are not in employment, education or training. the union's 'youth guarantee' initiative contributes to the three objectives of the europe 2020 strategy: to raise the employment rate of 20 to 64-year-olds to above 75%, to reduce the rate of early school leaving to below 10% and to lift at least 20 million people out of poverty and social exclusion by 2020. however, eu initiatives are not enough, active involvement of member states is essential to reduce unemployment. according to eurostat, the most significant decrease in youth unemployment over the past two years has been in hungary. thanks to the action plan for job protection launched by the orbán government in 2012, the level of employer burden decreased by 14.5% for every salary of an employee under 25 years of age, and by 27% for two years for those starting their career (up to huf 100 thousand). this measure has helped some 200 000 young people. promoting employment and reducing unemployment remains one of the key objectives of the orbán government.
, in writing. european results so far are modest there are very few countries where youth unemployment is not rising but falling. it is clear that there is a great deal of confusion in europe today on this issue, and the reason for this is that everyone knows that the easiest way to fight unemployment is to reduce taxes or social security contributions, so that businesses can more easily put people to work. but since the governments are in debt, they can't use this tool, so they have to dance on the edge, trying to find ways to get results without tax cuts. but real recovery can only come from tax cuts and the economic growth that follows. unemployment among those under 25 is on average close to 24% in the eu countries. the negative record is held by spain and greece, where 56 and 61.5 percent of those aged 18 to 24 are looking for work, respectively. in hungary, the unemployment rate among young people is around 27 percent. the european union has recently decided to take a number of targeted measures to support the almost 6 million young unemployed in europe, including the implementation of the so-called youth guarantee. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the programme to include the following areas: the environment, the environment policy, the environment and the environment policy.
, in writing. in the eu today, more than 5.5 million young people under 25 are unemployed, while in hungary one in three young people cannot find a job. the european council has set aside a total of €6 billion over the next seven years to tackle youth unemployment. i therefore urge the european commission to consider the ilo recommendation that €21 billion per year is needed to effectively reduce youth unemployment. the introduction of the youth guarantee in hungary is of particular importance. due to the continuous austerity, the economic policy that chases away investments, the institutional link in higher education, young people have lost hope that the current hungarian government can change their hopeless situation. the situation of hungarian youth is further aggravated by the fact that the biggest reduction in resources in higher education was experienced in hungary, along with greece. in hungary, the education policy of the orbán government has cut the amount of money available for higher education by 47% compared to the 2006 level, drastically reducing the number of state-funded student places. the european youth guarantee programme offers hope to hungarian youths who have lost their faith. i therefore ask the european commission representative here, mr andor lászló, to do everything possible to ensure that the youth guarantee is implemented in the member states and that adequate resources are provided. you can count on the dk meps for this work!
in writing. currently, there are 5.4 million young people out of work in the european union, and high youth unemployment is reducing the union's social cohesion and economic competitiveness. in hungary, the youth unemployment rate is over 27 percent. even more distressing is the fact that in the past few years, the number of economic migrants has increased tenfold, with 600,000 people, mostly young people, leaving the country to seek a better life abroad. as a member of the european parliament and a social democrat politician, i feel it is my duty to create institutional protection for the interests of this community and to find all the means to help and support those who need it to return home and to live at home, to start their hopeful business. the youth guarantee and microfinance programmes provide a substantial leverage for this, but the long-term outcome requires much broader strategic solutions that protect the principle of free movement of labour but actively support repatriation. it is positive that jean-claude juncker, the newly elected president of the european commission, will use public-private partnerships to boost economic growth, particularly by putting young people into jobs, by €300 billion over the next three years. but i also suggest that we find the platforms and resources that can be targeted to support economic migrants. to guarantee that we can provide them with opportunities in their own country, regardless of what national governments are doing.

in writing. we hear voices from member governments that sanctions against russia are not effective. the hungarian prime minister is saying we're shooting ourselves in the foot. the european parliament has two problems with this statement and must reject it outright. the first problem is that the claim is not true. the moscow stock market is plunging, the ruble has fallen to historic lows against both the euro and the dollar, $51 billion of capital has fled the country. all of this proves that these are real, strong, effective sanctions. on the other hand, it is true that the sanctions also cause damage to european economies, but they are much smaller than what russia suffers. russia needs us europeans more than the other way around. international law, the protection of the ukrainian people who are victims of territorial aggression and respect for european fundamental values are more important than the temporary problems caused by sanctions. moreover, it is important that the eu cannot credibly criticise a non-eu state until it has resolved its fundamental rights problems within its own borders. unfortunately, the hungarian example has shown that there can be a backsliding in the rule of law and democratic rights after accession to the eu. the eu is unfortunately currently without the means to act in the interests of fundamental democratic rights. a community solution must be found as soon as possible. it's the only way to pursue a credible foreign policy towards russia.

the following point is inserted after point (a) of article 2 (1) of directive 2009/72/ec:
the european youth initiative is aimed primarily at supporting young people under 25 who are not in employment, education or training in regions where the youth unemployment rate exceeded 25% in 2012. on the basis of this criterion, regions in 20 member states (including hungary) are eligible for support under the youth employment initiative. the aim is well aligned with the employment objective of the eu's europe 2020 strategy, which aims to achieve a 75% employment level for people aged 20-64 by 2020. this is a very important issue, since youth unemployment is twice as high as that of adults and their chances of entering the labour market are much lower. even if they do find work, they often find it only temporarily. the lack of harmony between qualifications and labour market needs is a major problem, contributing to the more than two million unfilled jobs in the union today. in the light of the above, i consider the european youth initiative and its successful implementation to be of the utmost importance.
in writing. dear colleagues! the european commission rightly places a high priority on the youth initiative, which has been discussed and promoted by the european parliament on many occasions. as rapporteur, i had the good fortune to deal with the issue of the youth initiative in the committee on employment and social affairs, and in view of the expectations there, i must say that something has finally moved. however, this is not enough, we must continue to work together, and the commission and the member states must make efforts. i can repeat what i said in july: i am convinced that by increasing competitiveness - including quality training, quality training for young people - we will be able to make real improvements in these measures. i'm warning you all again, this is a last minute situation. because in the next 10 years, practically, if we don't address this issue in depth, we're going to re-create the problem, and by the time that happens, 30 million unskilled young people will be back in the labor market. so we have to prevent that.
in writing. europe is ashamed that in recent times neither the majority of member states nor the union as a whole has been able to reduce youth unemployment effectively. the social democratic youth guarantee scheme is a good initiative, but it can only be effective if we increase the resources allocated and raise the age limit to 30 years. in several member states, the use of resources has not even started. but the problem is there, and the steps can't wait. two months ago, i also drew attention to the need to monitor the use of resources in the member states at eu level. only in this way can it be guaranteed that resources are used for the right purposes and efficiently everywhere. the question now is whether the commission has taken any steps to implement the eu's vigorous control and how does it intend to encourage the development of appropriate national programmes and thus accelerate the use of resources?
in writing. with the entry into office of the juncker commission, a new era and a new timetable will begin in the political governance of the european parliament, the european community. first of all, i would like to thank mr andor lászló, the eu commissioner for employment, for his dedicated work over the past years, with a number of important initiatives and achievements to his credit. but there was still much work to be done. last year, 26.6 million people were unemployed in europe, and young people were hit hardest by the economic crisis: in 2013, the number of unemployed under 25 in the european union reached almost six million. the youth employment initiative, including the youth guarantee programme, is an important anchor, but it is far from sufficient. pressing economic pressures have led hundreds of thousands to leave their homelands - mainly in eastern and central europe - to find a better life in more developed member states. there are hundreds of thousands of people who are not migrating for the sake of 'adventure', for the purpose of learning or to take advantage of the benefits of european labour mobility, but simply out of a painful need to make a living. the european union must respond and help the hundreds of thousands of people who would return from economic emigration if they could. as a hungarian representative, that's why i started the hungarian home waiting program, providing targeted help and searching for social refugees in my own country. i will initiate, if necessary, week by week, a comprehensive eu programme to support young people who wish to return to their homeland to build a new existence there. europe cannot allow its citizens to be forced to migrate!
in writing. - the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to provide additional support to workers affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade. the commission proposal provides for the mobilisation of eur 330 000 from the fund for technical assistance to the commission. the technical assistance shall be aimed at financing monitoring, information and the establishment of a knowledge platform, as well as advice to member states on the use, monitoring and evaluation of the egf. article 11 (1) of the egf regulation provides that, on the initiative of the commission, 0,5% of the annual ceiling of the egf may be used for technical assistance each year. i supported the report in the plenary vote.
in writing. - the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to provide additional support to workers affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade. greece requested assistance from the globalisation adjustment fund because nutriart s.a., a company located in the regions of central macedonia and attica, had laid off around 500 workers. the commission considers that the measures described above constitute active labour market measures falling within the scope of the eligible activities under article 7 of the egf regulation. personalised services for redundant workers, self-employed persons who are leaving their activity and neets are the following measures which together form a coordinated package of personalised services: career guidance, training, retraining and vocational training, advice on becoming an entrepreneur, assistance in setting up a business, financial assistance for job search and mobility support. i supported the report in the plenary vote.

on behalf of the verts/ale group. – mr president, as a seasoned diplomat told me the other day in relation to the gaza war: once hostilities stop, the incentives for politicians and other actors to engage in serious diplomacy quickly fade. we have a very small window of opportunity. the title of this joint resolution indicated that the eu, including the european parliament, was willing to take a bold step towards becoming a serious player in the middle east peace process. unfortunately, the resolution does not deliver on its title. i wish i could say it did, but we are unfortunately unable to co-sign it, basically because, unless the forthcoming negotiations in cairo are broadened to include much more than simply the ceasefire agreement between israel and the gaza authorities, there is no hope for any meaningful peace process to be started. mr della vedova said that the eu would leave no stone unturned. i hope he is right about that, but this resolution may be more notable for what is missing from it than for what it says. this is, in fact, a resolution full of unturned stones. my colleague has already mentioned a number of them: i will add two. accountability for all violations of international humanitarian law by all sides is a minimum requirement for a meaningful peace process. endorsing the request by the united nations relief and works agency (unrwa) for these accountability measures and for humanitarian aid to go unimpeded into gaza is a minimum requirement, but it did not find its way into the joint resolution. nor did a minimum request for an ad hoc delegation by this very parliament to see the facts on the ground. at the end of the day, unfortunately, we cannot support this text, and the window of opportunity is closing very quickly.

, in writing. there was perhaps no country in europe this year that was not struck by some natural disaster. in addition to several member states, serbia and bosnia-herzegovina were devastated by the floods of the century. climate change, the capriciousness of weather, has made floods and other disasters more frequent. it seems that humanity's recklessness in protecting the environment has perpetuated and systematized these situations. this also means that the scope and cost of prevention, control and recovery are fixed and permanently reflected in the member states' budgets, in most cases further burdening the already fragile budgetary balance. the eu solidarity fund has faced major challenges in recent years, mainly due to the lack of funds, but also because of the constant criticism of the criteria for the use of the fund. despite the commission's best efforts, the procedure is still too complex and the response time is too slow. human behavior is unlikely to change any time soon, so environmental destruction continues, with all its harmful consequences. i therefore believe that we need to set up a european disaster relief fund with substantial financial resources, which will enable us to deal with emergencies in a professional, efficient and, most importantly, rapid manner. because it seems that in the future, natural disasters and related prevention or recovery activities will be an integral part of the lives of europeans.
in writing. last year, on june 9, 2013, budapest experienced the highest flood of the danube in its history (891 cm). however, on the basis of the experience gained in the 2002 danube and 2006 danube and tisza, and in 2010 in northern hungary, the national directorate-general for disaster management and other bodies have made appropriate preliminary investments and preparations. thus, in june 2013, an unprecedented nationwide effort was made to avert the greatest flood threat ever faced in hungary. i would like to emphasize the importance of preparation in flood protection, taking the example of hungary. i welcome the spring reshaping of the eu solidarity fund processes, which will allow member states to access financial assistance from 2015 onwards in the form of advance payments, up to 10% of the final expected amount of support. as the current serbian example shows, the possibility of advance payments is of prime importance: the floods in serbia took place in mid-may, the commissioner responsible for regional policy announced the amount of aid expected in mid-october, but parliament and the council will only approve it later. the way in which member states show solidarity and solidarity in the face of natural disasters is symbolic, beyond the obvious practical help.

in writing. - the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to provide additional support to workers affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade. spain has requested assistance from the globalisation adjustment fund to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant in 142 spanish companies in the machinery and equipment sector as a result of the global financial and economic crisis. spain thus lost some 600 000 industrial jobs between 2008 and 2012, of which 150 000 were in the manufacture of metal products. the loss of jobs represented 24% of total industrial employment and 35% of total jobs in the metal products sector. the coordinated package of personalised services to be provided to redundant workers includes the following actions: career guidance, placement, training (vocational training, further training, development of transversal skills, on-the-job training), support for entrepreneurship, support for starting a business, job search support, contribution to the costs of going to work (incubation) and employment incentives. the commission considers that the measures described above constitute an active labour market measure falling within the scope of the eligible activities under article 7 of the egf regulation and that, following the request of spain, it is entitled to receive a financial contribution of eur 1 019 184. i supported the report in the plenary vote.
in writing. - the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to provide additional support to workers affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade. belgium requested assistance from the globalisation adjustment fund as the 512 redundancies of which 479 workers are expected to participate in the measures at the assembly plant of ford-werke gmbh in genk and at the supplier of ford genk 10 in belgium in the reference period from 1 july to 1 november 2013 have caused significant local labour market tensions. the request concerns the mobilisation of a total of eur 570 945 from the egf for the benefit of belgium. the commission has concluded that the application meets the requirements for mobilising the egf as laid down in regulation (ec) no 1927/2006 and that the measures taken by the belgian authorities constitute a coordinated, personalised package of services and active labour market measures aimed at reintegrating workers into the labour market and will help some 479 redundant workers to re-enter the labour market. i supported the report in the plenary vote.

in writing. - the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to provide additional support to workers affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade. france requested assistance from the globalisation adjustment fund because, following the redundancies at peugeot citroën automobiles involving 6120 workers, mainly in the île-de-france (aulnay) and brittany (rennes) regions, there is a significant industrial changeover at the sites concerned and measures are needed to help relocate the local workforce. the commission considers that the conditions laid down in article 1 (a) of the egf regulation are met and that france is therefore entitled to a financial contribution under that regulation, which constitutes an active labour market measure falling within the scope of the eligible activities under article 7 of the egf regulation. a financial contribution of eur 12 704 605, requested by france, should be mobilised to provide personalised assistance to redundant workers: reception structure and case management, external expert advice, thematic workshops, training, training grants, start-up grants. i supported the report in the plenary vote.
, in writing. the barroso ii commission operated in difficult circumstances as a kind of single issue commission, having to deal almost exclusively with the various aspects of economic crisis management, contrary to its original intentions. it is to be appreciated that it has also taken important and partly successful initiatives in crisis management that are worth pursuing (such as tackling youth unemployment). at the same time, the underlying approach and the current european economic policy practice are seriously lacking, to the detriment of the objectives of growth and genuinely effective quality job creation and european solidarity in the social field. the past has taught us a valuable lesson: if necessary, particularly in difficult periods of european integration, the commission must be able to act more effectively against member state governments which threaten the community and the european project from an economic and/or political point of view.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! the european council's decision to put security of supply on the agenda is a good one for two reasons. on the one hand, the situation in russia and ukraine gives the matter a clear urgency, and on the other, it is the member states that have a lot to do in this area. over the past years, the work of the commission and the parliament has identified all the existing instruments, measures and action plans that can contribute to the security of supply in europe. many of these have already come true. however, in many cases, member states are lagging behind in implementing or enforcing the legislation. for example, the introduction of mandatory physical two-way traffic and the development of simplified infrastructure authorisation processes are slow. as a result, the focus in the coming period should be on much more effective implementation. i would like to highlight two areas. one of the most important previous commission initiatives was to coordinate action with external strategic partners and suppliers. it is therefore welcome that the high representative-designate for foreign affairs and security policy has set the objective of creating the conditions for appropriate coordination between member states. the other priority medium-term measure, accelerating the implementation of projects of common interest, has also been discussed for some time. i support the proposal that the commission should play a greater role in project coordination. in conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, let me stress once again that the ball is now in the court of the member states. parliament has done most of its work. the success of the enhanced security of supply now depends primarily on the council and the member states.
