the following is the order of business for the debate on the report on the general budget of the european union for the financial year 2015, together with the related budgetary conclusions, by eider gardiazabal rubial and monika hohlmeier (a8-0014/2014) on behalf of the committee on budgets:
my fellow members of the house of representatives! first of all, i want to congratulate the performers, they did a great job. 1500 amendments had to be decided in the committee on budgets. the decision was facilitated by the fact that the proposers had created acceptable compromises. the fidesz meps support the motion on our table, mainly because the epp first adopted the amendments we had tabled, which then became part of the compromises mentioned above. i would particularly like to stress that parliament would increase the payment of the structural funds programmes in the 2015 budget by some eur 4 billion compared to the council's original proposal. we want to ensure that the process that several of my colleagues have already spoken about regarding unpaid bills does not continue in 2015. the european parliament also called for the eu to take action to combat terrorism and to ensure that the eu's external action is coordinated with the eu's external action. in this respect, we do not consider amendment no 1 tabled by the european commission to be supplementary, and we have some concerns in this respect.
this report also points to a major contradiction in the european union, but it cannot solve it. the autonomy of the member states is declining, we are moving towards a federal state, but the budget for common tasks is only 1% of the total income. it is an order of magnitude less than that required for a federal state to perform common tasks, which is a state arrangement we naturally reject. active contributors complain that they cannot bear more burdens, but they do not take into account that 100 billion euros a year flow to them from the periphery countries to the core countries. cohesion funds are declining, and the gap between regions has widened in the last cycle. this budget clearly shows the difference between reality and eu rhetoric.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the election of the european commission, as the commission under juncker's leadership has more democratic legitimacy than ever, thanks to the treaty of lisbon, which has ensured greater transparency of political processes, including the role of the european parliament and increased participation of european citizens. in this democratic process, the president of the commission was directly elected following a wide-ranging european campaign, and its members were accepted or rejected by the ep's committees after a thorough and critical hearing. the new commission can set out with a strong political mandate and a programme focusing on effective pro-growth european crisis management, job-oriented restructuring, european solidarity and a stronger commitment to fundamental rights and values. as a guarantee of the validation of leftist values, within the new, more focused structure of the juncker commission, the areas that are perhaps most important for the future (external relations, sustainable development, energy, fundamental rights) are supervised by vice-presidents who are aligned with the left. it is regrettable that, because of the negative european policy and the unfortunate actions of the hungarian government, hungary did not engage in the democratic process that led to the formation of the juncker commission, but instead became further marginalized and took on political burdens. the new european commission will be led by tibor navracsics, whose position is heavily weighted by the fact that he was not considered suitable for the post by the competent ep committee because of his political background.
in writing. - i supported this report because i believe that all cuts introduced by the council should be restored and it proposes using all the flexibility instruments of the multiannual financial framework in the 2015 budget, leading to an additional amount of more than eur 4 billion in payments in order to effectively address the situation. we need to promote growth and jobs with a credible and solidary budget that strengthens scientific research, erasmus+, the cef and smes. it therefore does not accept the council’s position, presenting cuts and deepening the payments problem.
in writing. - the objectives of recovery and job creation should be the main considerations in the negotiation of the eu budget. therefore, cohesion and agricultural aid, which are engines of development and job creation, should be increased in the first place. any attempt to reduce these resources would be to ignore the european union's main objectives, which the new commission has also set as its flagship: economic growth and job creation. it is sad that the council has finally adopted a position on next year's budget which is openly at odds with our objectives. i therefore voted in favour of the report proposed by the committee on budgets, which takes full account of the position of the committee on agriculture and rural development. we will not allow next year's rural development funds, the advance recognition and operating grants to producer organisations and the beekeeping and wine subsidies to be reduced by a penny. it is also a great success that the amendments which my colleague mr deutsch and i tabled together have been incorporated into the parliament's proposal. so we would be providing an extra €7 million for school fruit and €4 million for school meal programmes. this step, in addition to helping our dairy and fruit and vegetable sectors, serves the health and well-being of our children, which is in the interest of all europeans of good will to achieve.
in writing. - in my view, a way to improve the efficiency of the european semester is to make sure that reasons and causes for unsatisfactory implementation of csrs are properly analysed and eliminated. another is to radically strengthen national ownership of the csrs in individual member states to mobilise sufficient political support in favour of reforms. i also believe that more involvement by national parliaments could help. i support this ini report because i share the rapporteur’s position that the ep should support the european commission in its efforts to fully use the framework of the european semester to improve economic governance in europe
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report, which was the result of a long and difficult debate, because it contains a number of recommendations which should be included in the annual growth report and in various competition law documents, for example. this will be the commission's first task, to which we have sent very important signals. i voted in favour of the report, adding that we had clearly established that, although the economic recovery in some places was welcome, it did not mean that the recovery from the economic crisis had begun at european level, thanks to the structural reforms that had been implemented in a flawed manner. i believe that a correct and effective structural reform cannot be carried out with total disregard for social considerations. my vote in favour was also conditional on the report also taking a firm stand that public and private investment are both important and necessary elements if we want to restart the engine of europe. i should also stress that the report calls for further progress on tax avoidance and tax fraud, and on equal opportunities for men and women. moreover, in this report the european parliament does not advocate an increase in excise duty but a much more balanced tax system and the right to free movement of workers is clearly highlighted, which must remain an inviolable fundamental principle of the european union.
in writing. – first of all, i would like to express my regret that the european union has been so silent on the situation in hong kong. in my view we should not be shy about defending democracy in all parts of the globe. universal suffrage has no value if the validation of candidates is extremely restricted. personally, i hope that both sides show restraint and avoid the use of force, which would only complicate dialogue. as a vice-president of parliament and a member of the delegation for relations with the countries of southeast asia and the association of southeast asian nations, i will be determined not only to strengthen and enhance our relations but also to further develop our partnership in the best interests of both sides and of the international community.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your kind words. first of all, i would like to thank the court of auditors, the committee on budgetary control and, last but not least, bogusław sonik, the original author of the report. we will conclude a nearly one-year process with tomorrow's vote, by accepting my proposal as rapporteur and the proposal of the committee on budgetary control, to reject the council's final accounts for the fourth consecutive year. my new colleagues may not know this, but the rejection of the council's discharge is not new, since the european parliament has refused to grant the council discharge for the previous three years. this decision was taken in committee and in plenary, mainly because the council refused to cooperate with parliament in the discharge procedure. the european council refuses to participate in the committee hearing and refuses to cooperate with the committee responsible, citing a 'gentlemen's agreement' dating back to the 1970s. the fundamental problem is that the council does not recognise that parliament has the right to carry out the final accounts vis-à-vis it, in the same way as it does for all other eu institutions, although this right of the parliament has been confirmed on several occasions. the commission has also taken note of the fact that the council has not yet adopted a decision on the discharge to be given to the european parliament and the council in respect of the implementation of the budget. our committee asked for information on a number of issues, but in none of the following cases did the council provide parliament with the necessary documents, so that the discharge procedure could not be carried out in a meaningful way. serious shortcomings have been reported in the construction of the council's 'building europe'. the schuman square building has been under construction since 2008. despite the committee's repeated requests, the council did not provide any information on the costs of the project to date, the total amount of the investment or the method of financing. the construction is linked to the purchase and demolition of the so-called 'residence palace', which was previously partly located on the same site, and the council's office also refused to provide information on the costs of this. the committee on budgetary control has contacted the council on several occasions regarding the strategy for the transformation of the council's administration and the savings achieved, but to no avail. without answers to these open questions, we cannot recommend granting the exemption. on the basis of all this, ladies and gentlemen, i, as rapporteur, propose, as the committee on budgetary control has also supported, that parliament does not grant the council discharge for the financial year 2012.
president. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr pisani, for his report. first of all, i would like to thank my colleagues for their support and their valuable thoughts on what has been said in this plenary session and on the joint work that has been done in the committee on budgets. i think it is my conviction that the current situation, that the two fundamental institutions of the european union, the european parliament and the council, the european council, cannot agree on the procedure for the final accounts, is a poor certificate from both institutions and, as we often say, a very bad message to the citizens of europe. i believe that one of the most important tasks for us as members of the european parliament in the coming parliamentary term is to get the present situation out of the way, to start a dialogue and to work out an agreement that can finally resolve this situation. as mr liberadzki has said, the council has already made several proposals which give rise to confidence, and we are still considering the proposal made by the italian presidency at today's meeting as such, the credibility of which will be given by the substantive negotiations in the weeks and months ahead and, we hope, by the agreement. but in the present situation we have only one tool, parliament has only one tool, and that is to reject the discharge now definitively, i.e. in the second closure report i propose to reject the discharge for the 2012 financial year in the council at the final vote tomorrow.
with regard to the solidarity fund, madam vice-president, i believe that more flexibility and faster payments are needed for the member states eligible for assistance, and in particular for the candidate countries which are also eligible for assistance. this is particularly true for serbia, which was hit by severe flooding in the spring, further exacerbating the country's difficult economic situation and energy dependence, while it received perhaps the most positive country report from the european commission among the countries in the region. the eu's effective and swift support can empower serbia's leaders to continue on the path of european integration and building a strong rule of law in this delicate and complex foreign and security policy environment. the preservation of peace and stability is a priority for the eu, and it is particularly important for hungary, because there are 250,000 hungarians living in serbia, as is well known, and i am one of them.
madam president, energy security is a cornerstone of the common european energy policy. while the risk of russian gas dependency has been widely recognised, there has been much less discussion about nuclear investments. furthermore, russian energy companies are known to provide a means of influence for the russian government beyond mere business transactions. the hungarian government has agreed with rosatom to build two new reactors and the finnish government has also taken a decision to cooperate with rosatom on a new nuclear power plant. these investments will strongly affect european energy security as they will strengthen the trap of technology dependency, link the european energy supply to a nuclear-fuel cycle largely dependent on russian capacities, and make european energy supply more vulnerable through closer financial ties to the russian state and companies’ integral links to it. europe should take urgent action to deal with member state decisions that threaten europeʼs energy security.
madam president, at this very moment, the state celebrations commemorating the hungarian revolution of 1956 have begun in hungary. our national holiday is not characterized by the atmosphere of celebrating peaceful european democracy, but by social exclusion, fear of poverty, despair because of everyday life in hungary. that is why i would like to draw attention to a general european problem. hundreds of thousands of young people from hungary, almost 600,000 of my age, have emigrated to western europe, voting with their feet because of the hungarian economic and social conditions, and they are looking for their place, looking for their own new family opportunities. i will not leave the juncker commission alone, i will call attention to it every week, we need initiatives that help these young people to return to their own homes, to continue their lives there. i would like to thank commissioner andor lászló, the previous hungarian commissioner, for his work on the youth guarantee programme, but there must be a continuation, a home-coming programme.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, with the further advance of the islamic state, we are witnessing one of the greatest political crises in iraq in the early 21st century. incidents along the turkish border are a warning that jihadist expansion has reached the edge of the euro-atlantic sphere of interest. europe is one of the greatest hopes of the persecuted today. we in central europe, in hungary, have known since 1956 what it means to suffer military aggression. today, iraqis, most of them christians, are fleeing and suffering martyrdom. europe must not again be left behind by political considerations. pope francis, who is coming to visit us soon, earlier this week called for a real political commitment from the international community. what can europe do today? religious-based charities are an example. one of them, aid to the church in need, is spending millions of euros to build shelters as winter approaches. i repeat: europe's responsibility is growing, europe must save lives today, and our own citizens expect us to do so.

in writing. twenty-five years ago, the dream of many people was realized when the symbol of communism, the berlin wall, fell. the winds of change have already squeezed the gates of what was thought to be impenetrable. by the end of the summer, the first east german groups had crossed the hungarian border, and by september the iron curtain had disappeared. my generation was more or less a spectator of the rebirth of europe, but we believed that we would be citizens of a europe that was free and united. but many of the politicians of that time did not pretend to be fighting for themselves or for a new world. over the years, it became clear that our dreams of democracy had largely failed. we're going to hit walls again today. the political majority, with their nepotism, their political games, their laundering of hundreds of billions and their constant blame-seeking, have made it impossible for us to have meaningful public discourse, to have real politics. the economic shock of the transition failed to create sustainable economic policies, and the breakdown of solidarity plunged millions into desperate poverty, deprived them of their livelihoods, and made them victims of punitive social policies - calling into question the social contract itself. yet, november 9th is a day to celebrate with pride, and we hungarians feel our souls warm up after decades. even if we have had failures, it is now our responsibility to make things better. we know this is all part of a process, and we believe there is hope. it is the hope that, as part of a european value-creating community, we can make a reality of what is already being lost.

in writing. the fight against tax fraud is one of the main tax challenges facing eu member states today. effective measures to this end can improve the efficiency of tax collection and play an important role in increasing tax revenues, as a higher rate of vat collection is one of the possible tax consolidation measures. in the present difficult period, in which all the countries of the european union are making considerable efforts to consolidate public finances effectively, i can only welcome any initiative which can support these efforts, whether on the revenue or expenditure side of the budget. since vat fraud is such a sophisticated international crime that damages public finances, i believe that effective cooperation between the tax authorities of the member states is essential to combat it. i would therefore welcome a greater effort by the eu institutions to organise activities that would contribute positively to this cooperation. i believe that the introduction of a rapid reaction mechanism would have an impact on the duration of the approval process of special measures implemented by member states to combat tax fraud and would increase the likelihood of effectively addressing this type of action.

in writing. the gas wealth near cyprus is estimated to be between 140 and 200 billion cubic metres, which is also significant for the security of european supply. although it is not yet decided which route will be used to market the extracted gas at the end of the decade, either via an lng export terminal or via the feeding into a regional gas network, there is no doubt that the eastern mediterranean region can play an important role in reducing the volume of european gas imports. the current trends, project plans, point in the direction that the need for diversification of resources in central and eastern europe will not be met by one large alternative project, as was thought until the demise of the nabucco project, but by a number of smaller opportunities of a large scale, which can be combined to provide diversified supply. that is why it is important to exploit all the opportunities in europe. i support the continuation of the dialogue between the greek cypriot and turkish cypriot communities. a long-term settlement can only be achieved through peaceful means.
in writing. the un has declared the situation in south sudan a level 3 humanitarian crisis, the most serious. the situation is catastrophic, with 1.9 million people having fled their homes since december 2013 in the mainstream data alone, and nearly half a million of them having arrived as refugees in neighbouring countries. a million people are in danger of starving to death if we don't make some moves. despite the eu's substantial financial support to the region, aid is regularly attacked and aid workers are at risk. this is why today's debate is necessary and timely, as international actors must take immediate action to prevent the situation from worsening further.

in writing. the association agreement between the european union and moldova, negotiated between 2010 and 2013, will create new opportunities for trade and economic growth and will directly benefit moldovan businesses and citizens. the eu will benefit from smoother trade and better investment conditions with moldova. it saddens me to see that russia continues to use trade to weaken the region, including moldova. it imposes various import bans on moldovan products, in violation of russia's commitments under the world trade organization. the eu also expresses its solidarity with the moldovan people by signing the association agreement. however, eu citizens, particularly farmers, are also affected by russian import bans. for example, hungarian agricultural exports suffer losses of eur 80 million annually due to the ban. that's a loss of 70 million forints a day. the eur 344 million that the commission has so far released for compensation is not enough to help eu producers. on the other hand, it is outrageous that the commission intends to take this money out of the agricultural crisis fund. this would result in a reduction in direct payments next year. i therefore demand that the commission immediately provide the necessary funds from budgetary reserves and assigned revenue, as well as from non-agricultural resources, to compensate european union farmers.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, it has become clear that the europe 2020 strategy has not yet been able to raise employment levels, whereas in america, which has already emerged from the crisis, the unemployment rate has fallen to 6%. in the european union, unemployment has fallen by only two percentage points to 10.7% in the recent period. the european parliament resolution underlines the need to shift the tax burden towards more sustainable forms of taxation in order to stimulate growth and job creation, i.e. the economically more powerful, larger players must also shoulder their share of the tax burden. i think that's an important message. hungary has followed this path since 2010 and the employment rate has increased by 3% and unemployment has fallen from 10% to 7,6%. i believe that the message of the resolution should be taken into account by all member states and the commission, because the citizens are the losers of the crisis and it must be brought to an end.
i agree with the questioner that since the financial and economic crisis, the number of citizens exposed to unemployment and poverty has increased significantly. we need to use all the tools available to us in each of the eu policies to reduce unemployment. one of the breakthroughs in job creation, in my view, is agriculture. we need to increase global food production by 70 percent by 2050 to feed all of us. the european union has the natural and economic resources to take the lead in this process. sustainable farming practices and responsible use of resources also offer the potential for substantial production increases. agriculture and related industries, such as wood and furniture production or energy production from biomass and biogas, can create many permanent and seasonal jobs. we must therefore use all the means available to support agriculture. on the one hand, eu aid, in particular direct aid, must be kept at a level. on the other hand, undue financial and administrative burdens on farmers and businesses should be reduced. new unjustified animal welfare, food safety or environmental restrictions such as the increase of the ecological focus area should not be imposed. thirdly, the eu must protect european farmers from the dumping of agricultural goods from third countries. free trade agreements may be concluded which do not result in a reduction in the competitiveness of european farmers.
in writing. all my colleagues agree that one of europe's greatest tasks and obligations is to eradicate youth unemployment as soon as possible. in the union, more than five million young people are unemployed, and in hungary the figure is over 27%. in the newly joined countries - and in my own country as well - one of the most pressing problems is also linked to youth unemployment: economic emigration due to lack of prospects. in hungary, nearly half a million young people have already decided to emigrate. mass migration causes not only emotional but also social and economic hardships. and what they may not see and understand in western europe is not just the countries involved. according to the latest figures from the future of work research institute in bonn, five million of the citizens of the central and eastern european countries that joined the union since 2004 are now living in the western eu member states. researchers believe that this process can adversely affect the health and quality of life of the native population. the free movement of labour is an important asset of the european community and must be safeguarded. but we cannot allow, europe cannot allow, that some of its countries are brought to their knees by the forced economic migration of their own citizens. as a social democrat representative, i have therefore launched the homecoming programme in hungary and i call on the european community to extend it. we must find a common solution, because this is a common european problem.
in writing. the hague convention on jurisdictional arrangements applies to exclusive jurisdiction clauses concluded in civil and commercial matters. in the course of these proceedings, any dispute arising between the parties in relation to specified matters shall be settled in the courts of a specified state. the widespread adoption of the convention would provide greater legal certainty with regard to exclusive international jurisdiction agreements. it would thereby facilitate and make trade between the participating states more efficient. hungary's foreign economic relations have strengthened in recent years. a predictable commercial legal environment is essential for a successful continuation. that is why the convention needs to be approved.

my fellow members of parliament! i'm here to inform you that our session is now in session. first, allow me to make a few remarks on yesterday's minutes. the minutes of the meeting have been distributed. i ask my fellow members if anyone has any comments on the minutes? i see that there is none, so i declare the minutes to be accepted. we will then move on to our next item on the agenda, which is item 1 of the afternoon partsession: budget 2015: conciliation committee outcome is the title of the debate. as my colleagues in the house of commons know very well, we are in the aftermath of fruitless negotiations. the european parliament and the council were unable to reach an agreement and left the negotiating table without being able to reach an agreement either on the additional resources for 2014 or on the necessary and sufficient items for 2015. i'll give the floor to the chairman of the committee first, and the chairman has three minutes.
i'll close this discussion. the commission shall be assisted by the committee established by article 15 of regulation (eu) no 182/2011.
the european parliament wanted to achieve the full adoption of all budgetary amendments for 2014 in the conciliation negotiations. this would have raised the payment levels for 2014 by eur 4.7 billion to cover the outstanding invoices already submitted by member states in cohesion policy. if this is not ensured, an unpaid bill of up to eur 26 billion could accumulate by the end of 2014. we would have paid out €6 billion more than the council in 2015, for a total of €146.4 billion, to have enough resources to pay out the remaining cohesion programmes that were due to close in 2015, among other things. in the ep's reading, our amendments, confirmed by the people's party and then the rapporteur, increased the funding for convergence areas by eur 4 billion. the lack of liquidity on the payment side also threatens the implementation of other programmes: horizon 2020 calls may have a reduced pre-financing rate, delays in calls for tender and this may cause liquidity problems for universities, research centres and other institutions that have won the calls. in the case of erasmus, it may mean reduced pre-financing, which may reduce the number of people who can afford to go abroad to study with this scholarship, as they have to cover a significant part of their costs. raising the ep would have solved these problems. if the 2015 union budget is not adopted by 31 december 2014, the next year may start with 1/12 of this year. with tight deadlines and a willingness to compromise responsibly, the european budget for next year can be drawn up in time.

in writing. violence against women is present in all societies, whether physical, psychological or sexual. a recent report by the fundamental rights agency shows shocking data in this area. the survey found that one in three women had experienced sexual and/or physical violence since the age of 15. it is important to note that these data do not refer to third countries but to eu member states. violence against women has a serious impact on individuals and on society. yet, in most cases, the victims choose to remain silent. surveys in this area show that there are many more cases in reality than the justice statistics say. this urgent problem needs to be addressed at eu level, as violence against women is a violation of fundamental rights.
sexual violence and violence against women are still a major social problem that permeates our daily lives, not only at home, behind closed walls, but also in the workplace, in public places, on the internet and even in schools, but despite the fact that these cases are common, they are often only revealed in the rarest or most extreme cases. the prevalence of violent acts is confirmed by the results of various research and police statistics, but it is certain that many cases remain in obscurity, that the intimidated victims do not dare to go public, that many of them do not even dare to admit that what they have experienced is not a natural part of everyday life and that it should be dealt with as strictly as possible. i think that we need to take this to a higher level, we need to have the political will to take this problem seriously at both the social and eu level. the commission's proposal is to set up a european network of national experts to assess the impact of the eu's policies on the environment.

mr president, commissioner, i hope i am not making a big mistake when i say that it is part of european practice that if there is a problem in the banking system, we pour money into it with a fire-extinguishing syringe, but there are certainly institutions which are concerned about the use of this money. i want to focus on one issue, and that is that if a bank is owned by a government and it receives capital from the government, for example, the european commission usually proposes restructuring, reducing spending or some kind of spin-off. however, some opinions argue that the european central bank interprets these public funds as private investment. i ask you, is there a contradiction in this, and if so, how can it be resolved?
in writing. the €300 billion jobs, growth and investment package announced by the new european commission is a guarantee that the european union will emerge stronger from the biggest economic and social divide in its history. the investment package also offers hope to the nearly half a million unemployed hungarian citizens. by working to overthrow the new european commission, fidesz meps are saying no to new jobs, to the start of economic growth. the statement by fidesz member gáll-pelcz ildikó last week that they will vote on the motion of no confidence in the president of the european commission exposed fidesz. they're not only willing to cooperate with jobbik, but they're also supporting the views of european extremists. the motion of no confidence in the commission president, jean-claude juncker, is expected to be voted on by only the far-right meps, apart from fidesz. if juncker and the new european commission fail, the last chance to kick-start growth is lost. i suggest to my fidesz colleagues that they finally keep in mind the strengthening of the european union and hungary within it, as well as the real interests of the citizens.
mr. president, i have to go. thank you for the word. the key issue at the forthcoming conferences is whether the united states and china, the two biggest polluters, can be persuaded to make a real commitment and to deliver. if it's just to make the mood better, it's not going to do anything. the union is at the forefront of reducing carbon emissions. you set an example, that's right. but it also sets unrealistic and unrealistic goals. climate change is a global issue. that means it can't be solved locally. the european union is responsible for 10 to 12% of emissions, and this will fall to 4 to 5% by 2030. so really, the only way to make progress in this area is on a global scale. it should be recognised that the commitment of the member states also means that they determine their energy mix. for example, some countries are promoting nuclear power as a clean source of energy. of course, it would be good to have more green, renewable energy, but we cannot ignore the costs. yes, we who say we represent the interests of the european population never talk about the issue of energy poverty here, that for many families in europe the cost of energy is a huge item, so you cannot exclude the cost from this issue. thank you so much for listening.
in writing. in 2009, the european union unilaterally committed to reducing greenhouse gas emissions by 20% by 2020 compared to 1990 levels. the european council of 23 and 24 october 2014 committed to an intra-eu emission reduction of at least 40% by 2030 compared to 1990. for the first time since the united states and china announced their commitment in november 2014, i see hope that we can effectively tackle climate change through a real international partnership. although the target dates set by our partners are far off (2025 and 2030 respectively) and their performance can only be measured after many years, there is a chance that the european union has played a pioneering role in the initiative. this does not mean that europe can also bring in its competitive disadvantage. regardless of these commitments, the us energy price is expected to remain well below the european average for decades to come. in china, we have a very different competitive advantage, where the price of human resources is much lower. i am confident that as a result of the ttip negotiations, europe's leadership in green technologies will be even stronger than it is today, and european investors will be able to achieve significant successes in the us market.
in writing. mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, according to the european commission, the european union is responsible for 11% of global greenhouse gas emissions and is well on track to meet its own reduction commitments. with its seal of approval and the targets of the recently sanctioned 2030 climate and energy framework, europe can therefore once again sit confidently at the negotiating table at the un climate conference in december. but will there be other good students in lima, or will the eu once again be alone in the international community? the fight against climate change will only be successful in the future if other developed countries and currently large developing emitters make ambitious commitments comparable to those of the eu before the 2015 paris conference. the new international convention should be applicable, enabling, equitable and legally binding for both developed and developing countries. the union cannot solve the global emissions reduction deficit on its own. and while pursuing his own ambitious goals, he cannot exploit his own industry indefinitely. we need to look back for a moment and wait for the others. with the historic announcement by the united states and china, hopefully, europe's followers will soon be within sight.
in writing. - the ecb should continue to provide substantial support to risk monitoring and analysis work, drawing on its statistical expertise, through its macroprudential overview, which provides detailed statistical information on the various aspects of systemic risks affecting national and eu financial systems. in order to address the challenges posed by the changing nature of risks in the eu financial sector, the ecb should continue to participate in the review and further development of the european systemic risk board's risk indicators. by providing statistical information on the risks and vulnerabilities of the financial system, the risk warning system is a key communication tool and an important element in meeting public accountability requirements. i also consider that an exchange of accurate information and effective cooperation between the authorities is essential and therefore voted in favour of the report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the amendment, as the ssm regulation entrusts the ecb with specific tasks relating to policies relating to the prudential supervision of credit institutions, for the performance of which these amendments are essential. however, it should be underlined that the european union should minimise the continuation of parallel activities while maximising transparency. european citizens have a legitimate expectation that both the member states and the eu institutions will strive for cost-efficiency and a reduction in red tape. a good example of this is that the same data will only have to be collected once under this amendment. however, it should be noted that the transfer of confidential data will need to be accompanied by adequate protection at all levels, including with the receiving authorities.
in writing. - monetary policy has operated in conditions of continuous but somewhat moderating financial diversification and difficult challenges. we have had many discussions with mr draghi at the econ committee, where we have heard good results. despite these results, i believe that further efforts will be needed to avoid a negative interaction between problem countries, different economic prospects and the vulnerability of banks at member state level. further progress in the establishment of banking union can of course make a major contribution to overcoming these obstacles, and, if necessary, there can obviously be talk of sanctions in line with mrs swinburne's recommendations. i welcome the report and have voted in favour of it.
in writing. i shall vote in favour of the motion for a resolution. we need to act now, because climate change is already causing serious problems for every country. for example, in hungary, excessive rainfall and flooding, as well as frequent alternations between lack of rainfall and drought, have become common. as regards the objectives set out in the proposal, i would like to draw attention to the following: on the one hand, the eu's strict commitments are worthless unless other economies, which are much more polluting and emit more carbon than the eu economy, commit themselves to at least the same level of commitment. the eu negotiating delegation should therefore demand that it only commits itself to stricter commitments and reductions in greenhouse gas emissions if other economies responsible for the earth's greenhouse gas emissions, such as the united states or china, do the same. on the other hand, the role of renewable energy production and energy efficiency in households and industry should be given much greater prominence. the eu must refrain from any action that hinders the use of renewable energy. for example, we should not restrict the planting of useful varieties of trees that provide biomass feedstock, the production of first-generation bioethanol from surplus crops or the promotion of combustible biomass plants. thirdly, we must refrain from introducing any kind of accounting system in agriculture. this would place an unnecessary burden on farmers and member states. and it's also hard to measure carbon emissions here. emission reductions in this area are already secured.
in writing. - according to the european commission, the european union is responsible for 11% of global greenhouse gas emissions and is well on track to meet its own reduction commitments. the fight against climate change will only be successful in the future if other developed countries and currently large developing emitters make ambitious commitments comparable to those of the eu before the 2015 paris conference. the union cannot solve the global emissions reduction deficit on its own. following the historic announcement by the us and china, it is hoped that they will soon catch up with europe in their emission reduction commitments.

vice-president of the commission, on behalf of the vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. - mr president, i would like to say to the honourable members of the european parliament that upholding the rights of children and preventing and responding to any kind of violence against children will remain a top priority for the eu. this is also witnessed by the fact that, together with our latin american and caribbean friends, we table every year at the un in new york a resolution addressing specific challenges for children. this year it was discrimination and inequality. many challenges remain when it comes to complete fulfilment of the rights of children worldwide and ensuring that the post-2015 world will be a better place for children. nevertheless, we are encouraged by this 25th anniversary, and the almost universal ratification of the un convention on the rights of the child proves that there is an abundance of political will to make a lasting difference. we are also encouraged by the staunch support shown by this house with its resolution on the rights of the child. the commission and the european external action service look forward to close cooperation with parliament in ensuring that the rights of the child are effectively mainstreamed in legislation and policy, as requested by this house.
, in writing. the un convention on the rights of the child has been in force for 25 years. the convention is based on the principle of subsidiarity and is open to all member states. the civil report sent in february 2014 emphasises that the situation of children in hungary has deteriorated significantly between 2006 and 2012 and has become unacceptable. it's worse than the country's economic situation would warrant or the government would like to make it appear. we have a crisis. in hungary, according to a newly published eurostat survey, one third of people (33.5%) are at risk of poverty or social exclusion, an increase of 5 percentage points compared to a year ago (!). according to the latest data from the child poverty charity, 43 percent of all minors are affected by poverty. the first step to breaking out of poverty is education and social services. this system has been gradually dismantled over the last five years: the lowering of the school leaving age from 18 to 16, the nationalization of the school system, the open and direct state-sponsored segregation of schools and the significant reduction and conversion of unemployment benefits into public works, the abolition of housing allowances and free meals for the most deprived children. hungary has been completely dismantled, and in many of its neighbourhoods, conditions are prevailing that we have only seen in the third world. we have a duty to act against child poverty within the union.
i'll close this discussion. the commission shall be assisted by the committee established by article 15 of regulation (eu) no 182/2011.

thank you for the opportunity, mr. president. listening to this debate today, what i see first of all is that there is a very strong will in the european parliament to see that we create the most advanced digital single market in europe. i don't want to get into a terminology debate, but if i may suggest that, let's talk about digital union from now on. that would be the right thing for our ambitions, not just a single internal market. but at this point, i have to say that while everyone is pushing for the wider adoption of the internet, in my country, hungary, the hungarian government is currently planning to impose a heavy tax on internet users. i think you'll be paying attention, because the european community cannot allow this. thank you very much.
- thank you for your time, mr. president. my fellow members of parliament! as the commissioner pointed out, we discussed the steel report earlier this year. i was rapporteur for that dossier then and i still think it is important to emphasise some of the long-term priorities for industry initiatives. the key to boosting european steel demand is to improve the business environment and to create a competitive industrial framework. to achieve this goal, two key areas need to change: lower energy prices and reduced regulatory costs, otherwise competitiveness will remain a pipe dream. firstly, as regards the decrease in energy prices, energy costs in the steel industry represent a significant part of the production costs. the rapid implementation of the single european energy market, the completion of missing infrastructures, the elimination of bottlenecks in cross-border capacity and the provision of competing energy sources are essential to reduce energy prices. the implementation of these measures is a priority for the council and the member states. second, reducing regulatory costs for industry. europe must continue to lead in the areas of sustainability, green economy and climate protection. however, this should avoid the risk of carbon leakage and the risk of jeopardising the competitiveness of the steel industry. the reform of the eu ets cannot be allowed to put the best performing companies in the steel industry at a disadvantage. my fellow members of parliament! without these measures, the dismal employment trend in the steel industry of recent years, with a continuous decline in the number of jobs in many european countries, including hungary, would continue. i believe that european measures to protect jobs are still not enough, but i hope that the 300 billion euro juncker plan can also be part of the solution in this area. the reindustrialisation of the european economy can only be achieved through concerted action. thank you for your time, mr. president. in writing. - cohesion policy is the eu's main investment policy, the engine of european growth and job creation, with a budget of over €350 billion for the period up to 2020. the strong delay in the programming process is a serious problem for the effectiveness of cohesion policy, as only a small part of the planned operational programmes will be adopted by the end of 2014. it is of course important that quality operational programmes are drawn up and adopted, as this avoids reprogramming at a later stage, but the current delays make it difficult for national, regional and local authorities to make effective use of the structural funds for 2014-2020. another problem is that, as cohesion policy (together with co-financing by the member states) accounts for the bulk of public expenditure on growth in the union, we are slowly undermining the efficiency of investment. in addition to the delays in the programming process, there is the problem of recurrent arrears, which currently amount to eur 23 billion for the 2007-13 programming period. this deficiency significantly undermines the credibility, effectiveness and sustainability of cohesion policy. it is important and urgent to reach an agreement on the backlog as soon as possible on the basis of the new proposal from the european commission. it is also essential that as many programmes as possible are placed in the 'ready to receive' category by the end of 2014.
in writing. the european parliament debated the most pressing issues concerning children's rights at its plenary session today, in the context of the 25th anniversary of the adoption of the convention on the rights of the child by the un. on the 25th anniversary of the convention, eu member states must strengthen their commitment to respect and protect children's rights in practice. we have a vital role to play in protecting children's rights within and outside the union, as respecting children's rights is both a legal and a moral obligation. hungary is setting a good example in this area as well. think of child-friendly justice or the new elements in the hungarian criminal code that increase the protection of children.
in writing. the creation of the eu's digital single market is of paramount importance to citizens and businesses, as it has the potential to create millions of new jobs and allow europe's gdp to grow by 4% by 2020. strengthening consumer rights strengthens trust and ultimately the economy, so i voted in favour of the commission doing everything it can to ensure that companies providing such services operate on a level playing field in the online search market. the legislative framework for data protection and adequate protection of intellectual property rights has not yet been finalised and this should be brought to the attention of the present resolution.
mr president, thank you, although i would prefer to keep the old procedure, one in favour, one against, and one presenting the whole thing, so as not to complicate our business. the commission work programme is a very serious exercise, especially in the absence of a right of initiative on behalf of parliament. however, this year is a special year and our programming exercise does not foresee a resolution because the commission only took office on 1 november, so there was no possibility to have this sequence, with one resolution in september and then another one. so i do not think it is good to have a hasty resolution on this, especially given that what we want to see after an election year is work, real work, not resolutions in january about a programme which is to be adopted tomorrow by the commission. so my proposal is not to have a resolution but to start to work.
on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, the green group and i warmly welcome and support the accession of the eu to cites, which is one of the world’s most powerful tools for biodiversity conservation. it is clearly a necessary and desirable development. however, as i see it, this step is only a drop in the bucket. let me remind you that, despite the eu’s commitment to halve the loss of biodiversity in the eu by 2020 and to restore biodiversity wherever possible, in the longer term – as well as in the long-term vision for biodiversity for 2050 – natural capital and ecosystems services are being continuously depleted. this will have profound consequences for our wellbeing. as stated by the commission itself, over half of the habitats and species covered by the eu habitats directive are considered to be in an unfavourable conservation status. there is evidence that the status of many ecosystems is reaching, or has already reached, the point of no return. it is estimated that each year we lose 3% of our gdp due to the loss of biodiversity. besides undermining the economy through diversity loss, changes in habitats and ecosystems also erode social cohesion in and outside the eu. thus we must step up efforts in europe to achieve our biodiversity targets. we must ensure that none of the policies of the eu or of its member states have harmful impacts on biodiversity. we must reinvest in natural capital, construct green infrastructure and ensure that all spending under the eu budget is supportive of halting the loss of biodiversity. i therefore urge the european commission and all european decision-makers to set their priorities and structure their work mindful of the biodiversity targets, and not to compromise biodiversity, as it is crucial to sustaining the ecological systems and the services we all depend on.
on behalf of the ppe group. thank you very much for the word. madam president, distinguished officer, fellow members of the house of representatives. reducing the greenhouse gas emissions of fuels is an important part of the european union's efforts to combat climate change. the european union, as a pioneer in this fight at international level, cannot afford to let its objectives go unfulfilled. at the same time, we must achieve these objectives by taking account of the interests of industry. we need to create regulations that place as little bureaucratic burden on economic actors as possible. we are responsible for protecting our environment, for the competitiveness of our continent, for the preservation of our jobs. in my view, the commission's proposal combines these aspects in an appropriate way. i would like to call the attention of my colleagues who wish to vote against the commission's proposal to the fact that, despite the best intentions, they are risking five years of work. it is a compromise proposal, so it is far from perfect, but after lengthy negotiations, a consensus solution has been reached, which will finally allow the work on reducing emissions to begin. it sounds good for distributors to report the greenhouse gas emissions of the raw materials they actually use. however, the practical implementation of this does not seem realistic. on the one hand, it is not possible to verify that the extracting countries provide the eu with reliable data, and on the other hand, obtaining the necessary certificates would impose a disproportionate administrative burden on industry. moreover, this methodology may also put at a competitive disadvantage operators who have no intention of importing, for example, canadian oil sands but have limited possibilities to source raw materials. the average gross value is, in my opinion, a good tool to bridge these problems. my fellow members of parliament! i therefore propose that the commission's proposal be accepted. a possible rejection could also jeopardise the 6% emission reduction target. thank you very much for the floor, madam president.

written. the crisis has divided euro area and non-euro area member states in terms of economic performance, a duality that raises serious questions about the effectiveness of any common economic policy. i believe that our future together depends on whether we can bridge this disturbing gap. although the process has begun, it is progressing at an alarming rate and it is feared that by the time the economically well-functioning and crisis-stricken countries, especially those in southern europe, come together, it will be too late. it has been proven that overcoming the crisis also requires a disciplined fiscal policy, including structural fiscal rules laid down at constitutional level. it became clear that the measures launched in 2011 in the european union were unable to significantly increase the employment rate (68%), whereas, for example, my country, hungary, has been steadily increasing it since 2010 in small, measured steps, from 60,4% to 63,2%. i think hungary should set a good example in this area. the main issue under review now is the extent to which the new rules introduced by the sixth and second packages have been effective in achieving the objectives set and have contributed to lasting convergence in the economic performance of the member states. i look forward to seeing what the commission will do to restructure the company.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of re-electing emily o'reilly as european ombudsman, in recognition of her personal competence and her work to date and of the ombudsman's outstanding role in the functioning of an effective, transparent and democratic european union. although the european ombudsman's tasks and powers do not extend to investigating anomalies in the governance of individual member states, he has an important role to play in guiding and stimulating relations between the european institutions, bodies and citizens, in particular in the fight against a citizen-friendly and open eu and against euroscepticism. its more successful and visible activities can be a particularly important encouragement for citizens of member states where human and civil rights are still not fully respected or where the government does not fully ensure the conditions for the functioning of a democratic rule of law. the ep's convincing vote means that the european ombudsman can rely on reinforced political support and legitimacy in this important mission in the period ahead.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the proposal for a directive of the european parliament and of the council repealing council directive 93/5/eec of 25 february 1993 on the support of the commission by the member states for scientific investigations into foodstuffs and on the cooperation of the member states with that directive. the commission has launched a programme to provide businesses, workers and citizens with a simple, clear, stable and predictable regulatory framework. in this context, the commission undertook to repeal the rules that had become redundant or obsolete. the purpose of the repealed directive was to ensure the proper functioning of the scientific committee for food by encouraging scientific support from the member states and by organising cooperation with the competent national bodies on scientific matters relating to food safety. under regulation (ec) no 178/2002, efsa became the competent authority responsible for organising scientific cooperation with the member states and the corresponding national bodies active in the areas covered by the authority's mission. in my view, these and similar simplification measures lay the foundations for the effective functioning of the european union. these are not just technical changes, but positive changes that can be felt in the daily lives of eu citizens.
in writing. ‒ the washington convention on international trade in wildlife species is a major international instrument aimed at ensuring the protection of endangered species of flora and fauna by regulating international trade in specimens of these species. the text of the convention originally allowed only states to join. for this reason, the european union has so far participated in the convention only as an observer. this is changing, as it is now legally possible for the eu to join the convention as a party to the treaty and to represent itself indirectly, not just through the member states. the accession of the european union to the convention will allow the eu to be adequately represented at the meetings of the organisation established by the convention and to play a full role in the work of the convention.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the motion because by joining the convention on international trade in endangered species of wild fauna and flora we can take a more vocal stance against the illegal trade in endangered species. i should note that until now, only sovereign states could join cites, which changed in 2013. from now on, federations of states, organisations formed by sovereign states, can join this important international instrument, and the european union has the opportunity to do so. i consider it important that, as the recommendation states, the accession of the european union to cites is a logical and necessary step towards the full achievement of the objectives of the eu's environmental policy. accession will allow the commission to represent the union's united position on matters relating to the convention on behalf of the european union and to play a significant role in the negotiations of the conferences of the parties.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of renewing the agreement on scientific and technological cooperation between the european community and ukraine. ukraine's scientific and technological development, its connection to european and international scientific networks and access to know-how play a very important role in the country's european integration and economic stability. in the european union, we also see a response to economic development in strengthening r & d and innovation activities, and this is no different in ukraine. the current state of the ukrainian economy and the situation of the ukrainian energy sector also have an impact on the european union. however, scientific and technological cooperation is an area that can greatly improve these and contribute to the stability and development of the country and thus the region as a whole.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the signing and provisional application on behalf of the european union of the agreement on the participation of the republic of croatia in the european economic area and three related agreements. as a member of the joint parliamentary committee of switzerland, norway and the eu/iceland/eea, i also consider it important that croatia participates in the european economic area. the importance of this issue is shown by the fact that the eea agreement brings together the eu member states and the three members of the european free trade agreement: iceland, liechtenstein and norway, as a single market. the eea agreement requires all eea states to adopt eu legislation on the four freedoms - free movement of goods, services, persons and capital. the agreement also covers cooperation in other important areas such as research and development, education, social policy, environment, consumer protection, tourism and culture (accompanying and horizontal policies). i should note that the treaty entails obligations and not merely benefits, as the agreement provides equal rights and obligations in the internal market for eea citizens and economic operators.
in writing. ‒ a council decision of february 2004 granted france concessions for certain products until 1 july 2014, including the 'octroi de mer' tax applied in the french outermost regions, in order to compensate for and encourage the competitiveness and social and economic development of the outermost regions, which are facing many of the major difficulties (such as their small size, limited dependence on the market, geographical distance, low export capacity). france has requested that a tax system similar to the one described above be maintained until 31 december 2020. the european commission has carried out a thorough investigation and proposed the creation of a new legal framework for the octroi de mer tax for the period from 1 january 2015 to 31 december 2020, which defines the products to which the exemptions and preferences can be applied. this measure does not undermine the unity of the internal market or the union legal order, aims to stimulate economic activity and competitiveness in the outermost regions and is therefore to be supported.
in writing. ‒ on 28 october 2014, the commission adopted a proposal for a decision to mobilise the european globalisation adjustment fund for italy to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by the limited liability company whirlpool europe in economic sector 27 (manufacture of electrical equipment) of nace rev. 2 as a result of the global financial and economic crisis. this is the 18th application to be examined under the 2014 budget and is for a total of eur 1 890 000 from the egf for the benefit of italy. the application concerns a total of 608 beneficiaries. the request was submitted to the commission on 18 june 2014 and supplemented by additional information until 13 august 2014. the recession has had a serious impact on the consumption habits of italian households, as they have reconsidered their purchasing decisions, in particular with regard to the purchase of consumer durables: in four of the five years between 2009 and 2013, household consumption in italy decreased compared to the same period of the previous year. consequently, the italian market for household appliances decreased from eur 3174 billion in 2010 to eur 2649 billion in 2013, a decrease of 16,5%. this explains why production in the 27th sector of nace rev. 2 decreased at the level of the 28 eu member states for 6 consecutive years (2008 to 2013). the fidesz-kdnp delegation supports the motion in solidarity. it is important that dismissed workers receive appropriate support for their reintegration into the labour market.
in writing. ‒ on 10 november 2014, the commission adopted a proposal for a decision to mobilise the egf in favour of poland to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant by the fiat auto poland s.a. plant in tychy, silesia, poland, as a result of major structural changes in world trade due to globalisation. this is the 19th application to be examined under the 2014 budget and it is for the mobilisation of a total of eur 1 259 610 from the egf for the benefit of poland. the request concerns 829 redundancies at the fiat plant in tychy and 250 redundancies at 21 suppliers and downstream processors during the reference period from 21 january 2013 to 21 may 2013. the request was sent to the commission on 29 july 2013. the commission has concluded that the application complies with the requirements for mobilising the egf, as laid down in article 2 (a) of regulation (ec) no 1927/2006. the coordinated package of personalised services to be co-financed comprises the following measures for the reintegration of 777 dismissed workers: reimbursement of training and education-related costs, entrepreneurship training, training grants, traineeship grants, reimbursement of traineeship costs, intervention costs, support for self-employment and encouragement of recruitment. the fidesz-kdnp delegation supports the motion in solidarity. it is important that dismissed workers receive appropriate support for their reintegration into the labour market.
in writing. ‒ this is the twenty-first application to be examined under the 2014 budget and is for a total of eur 6 444 000 from the egf for greece. the application concerns a total of 600 beneficiaries and 500 neets. the request was lodged with the commission on 29 july 2014 and supplemented by additional information until 23 september 2014. the commission has concluded that the conditions for a financial contribution from the egf are met in the application, in accordance with the relevant provisions of the egf regulation. the coordinated package of personalised services for redundant workers and neets includes the following actions: career guidance, training, retraining and vocational training, assistance for business start-ups, financial assistance for job search and training, mobility support. the fidesz-kdnp delegation supports the motion in solidarity. it is important that dismissed workers receive appropriate support for their reintegration into the labour market.
in writing. ‒ on 11 november 2014, the commission adopted a proposal for a decision to mobilise the european globalisation adjustment fund for france to support the reintegration into the labour market of workers made redundant as a result of major structural changes in world trade patterns due to globalisation. the coordinated package of personalised services to be co-financed comprises the following measures for the reintegration of 3886 workers made redundant: counselling and career guidance, training, assistance in setting up businesses, regular information and communication activities, retraining assistance and mobility support. the fidesz-kdnp delegation supports the motion in solidarity. it is important that dismissed workers receive appropriate support for their reintegration into the labour market.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of appointing the chair, vice-chair and members of the single resolution board and thus allowing the new body to start work soon. this body, together with the single resolution mechanism and the related financing mechanism, is an important building block for the establishment of the banking union. the purpose of establishing a single bank resolution mechanism is to minimise the cost to taxpayers and budgets of the recovery or liquidation of a bank or financial institution in the event of its failure. this objective is certainly to be supported. the functioning of this mechanism will be facilitated by this key expert and independent body.

in writing. i fully agree with the commission's objective of reasonable legislation. like the citizens, i expect the commission to propose solutions to key economic and social challenges such as fighting unemployment, energy security or improving competitiveness. in areas where the member states are in a better position to act, the eu should intervene less in people's daily lives. i welcome the commission's withdrawal of its proposals on school milk and school fruit, the air quality package and the production and marketing of seeds and propagating material. the existing eu rules in these areas are working well and citizens should not be burdened with new, more complex rules. i am particularly pleased that the commission is willing to make fundamental changes to its proposal on organic farming, as requested by the council and parliament. finally, i believe it is important that the efforts to simplify and streamline should affect as many of the major regulations as possible. significant simplifications will be needed in the regulation of greening, especially in ecologically significant areas. when evaluating the common fisheries policy, every effort should be made to prioritise environmentally friendly closed-system freshwater aquaculture as an excellent example of green management. and we need to support renewable energy production much more strongly than we currently do if we want to achieve long-term energy security in the eu.
in writing. i am convinced that in the medium and long term, the european economy can be boosted by research and development, innovation, educational development and, therefore, knowledge. i find it disappointing that the european commission's 2015 work programme has only mentioned r & d and innovation once in a meaningful way: the energy union vision includes support for these activities, an initiative i share widely. even more disappointing is the fact that the commission's investment incentive package, which has just been announced, also leaves r & d and innovation behind. moreover, a larger amount from the existing programme for r & d development (horizon 2020) was transferred to the strategic fund, with a still rather uncertain outcome. this shift is reducing predictability in an area where the availability of resources is crucial for multiannual, long-term projects. i suggest that the european commission should pay particular attention to european r & d and innovation activities in its work, as the further development of our skills and capabilities in this area is essential for the re-industrialisation of europe.
in writing. it is extremely worrying that the new commission has withdrawn draft legislation that is essential for sustainability, including the circular economy package and the air quality regulatory plan, as well as the draft revision of the energy taxation directive. the withdrawal of the circular economy, resource efficiency and waste recycling project is also shocking because it was the result of a serious social consultation and would serve the long-term interests of both the public and the economic operators. the waste targets, as indicated in the baseline studies, the impact assessment and the commission's own communication, would create around 500 000 new jobs, stimulate innovation, significantly reduce harmful impacts on human health and the environment and significantly reduce the demand for rare raw materials. the move was opposed by a number of organisations and member states, and the weight of the matter is indicated by the fact that the issue was put on the agenda for tomorrow by the council of ministers, under pressure from the italian eu presidency, which is made up of 23 member states. the withdrawal of the proposals is also a concern because it is not clear what criteria will be used to prepare the new version. this package of measures is not only not conducive to restoring confidence, but it also fails to meet the requirement of transparency. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any evidence that the commission has taken a decision to impose a financial penalty on luxopco.
in writing. i support all measures to boost investment and job creation, but these measures will not be successful as long as tax evasion and avoidance in the european union is at this level. the estimates are well known, but this double circle must be supplemented by the question of over-dose, which is also an obstacle to economic development. in hungary, for example, the indirect food surcharge is 27%, and additional special taxes are added to it. as a result, traffic has been significantly reduced, forcing operators to resort to legal avoidance techniques. another example from my country is that the overselling of tobacco products and the transformation of the tobacco distribution system caused a 40% decrease in turnover, which can be explained and demonstrated by the increase in the turnover of black market products. i encourage the european commission to address these issues in its work programme. i would like to ask the commission whether it plans to take further steps in the field of tax harmonisation and whether it will include these steps in its work programme for 2015.
in writing. yesterday, the european parliament's committee on budgets adopted by a large majority the agreement on the eu budget for 2015 and the surplus for 2014. the agreement has the enormous advantage of allowing the eu's 2015 budget to be adopted this year, meaning that next year not only a twelfth of this year's budget could be paid out every month. the ep agreed to the adoption of the eu budget for next year on condition that the european commission develops a plan to settle the outstanding accounts. in 2014, eur 23,4 billion of the previous year's arrears are to be settled. the agreement between the ep and the council provides for an additional funding of almost eur 4 billion. during the negotiations on the 2015 budget, we defended important cohesion and agricultural resources for hungary. we therefore rejected the european commission's proposal to create a hedge to mitigate the damage caused by the russian embargo by transferring 1% of direct payments to farmers. we can count it a success that we have protected the direct payments that are important for hungarian farmers! the adoption of the 2015 budget could be jeopardised if the plenary adopts a single budgetary amendment to the proposal. that would require reconciliation negotiations and we would not be able to adopt the budget this year. the hungarian people's party delegation will make a responsible decision in representing the national interests of our country, that is, it will vote in plenary on the agreement and reject any amendment proposals that threaten it.
it's a two-card draw. mr pons, you mentioned in your speech that you attach great importance to the fight against tax evasion and tax fraud, and i would like to ask you, with respect, whether your party and you personally are prepared to take the same strong action against the over-selling of certain member states, which is a particularly serious problem in my country, hungary, for example, where it is a way of opening up the black economy.
madam president, mr. president, ladies and gentlemen, members of the house. there is agreement between parliament, the commission and the council that the european economy needs to be stimulated. i support the objectives of the investment plan myself. however, i have some doubts and reservations about the way in which it will be implemented. firstly, in fact, outside the eib funding, there seems to be no new money for the investment plan. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the guarantee fund and its implementation in the light of the results of the pilot project. on the other hand, the multiplier effect envisaged by the commission is, in my view, overly optimistic. the estimates of transfers are unfortunately not realistic on the basis of the information available and further member state involvement would be very necessary. thirdly, taking into account the above concerns, particular attention should be paid to the selection of projects. only a good quality list of projects can lead to long-term growth. we can only achieve the desired stimulating effect by supporting projects that serve our objectives in all respects. my fellow members of the house of representatives! i think that's what energy projects are like. so let's seize this opportunity and take a big step towards solving the problem of european security of supply. the barroso package has already focused on energy infrastructure, but we are still talking about dependency and lack of connections. the central and eastern european region remains the most vulnerable. the only way to ensure a secure and affordable energy supply for european citizens and businesses is to create a single energy market. in the long run, it is cheaper energy that can make europe more competitive.
