it's a two-card draw. trust is needed to get the economy going. for example, in europe, in connection with the commission's work for the next year, and as a hungarian, i see that there are countries - not only our country - where the parties, and for example the prime minister himself, regularly slander the european union. so i ask you, mr. speaker, what do you think about the fact that the prime minister has pointed to the european union for example because of the introduction of a new tax in hungary, when we both know that this is not true? is this method permissible?
that's a two-card answer. thank you for your question, mr. member. it gives me a chance to say something. you don't like to praise your own horse, but if you have already done so and asked a question, then let me tell you in this plenary that hungarian economic growth is today at the forefront of the european union, more than 3%. unemployment is falling steadily in hungary, as is the public debt, and the deficit is stable below 3%. these are the indicators of the hungarian economy. all this after we took over a crumbling economy from the government you represented.
madam president, mr. president, colleagues, i would like to thank you for your kind words. do you know the term "skype grandparent" or "skype grandson"? we in hungary use it for families that have been torn apart by the economic, social crisis. for those who had to move thousands of kilometres from hungary, young people who, together with their children, are now quite certain to see their grandparents less often, since they have settled in another country because of the economic crisis. it's not good for anyone. this is obviously causing social tensions in the wealthier european countries, where there are a lot of eastern european workers, but it's not good for us at home, because there's a shortage of nurses, there's a shortage of doctors, there's a shortage of craftsmen. 600,000 young people have emigrated from hungary in the last four years. i ask the commission to develop a concrete action plan on how we can help these young people to find their place, and not just to stay in touch with their parents and grandparents on skype.
i would like to mention two things, although i could have mentioned one of them in the context of a pink card. i would like to draw mr juncker's attention to the fact that tax avoidance and tax evasion, as two words that run side by side, do not cover the whole tax problem, the problem of tax evasion, because there is a notion that the economy is being blackmailed, in fact, blackmailed, by excessive taxation. hungary, my country, just to give an example, is where the food trade, thanks to the staggeringly high food tax of 27%, to which the government has now added an additional 6%, essentially half of the food trade is going to the black economy. another, it's a shorter sentence. i'd like the resources of the investment stimulus package not to drain the existing funds.
in writing. the next test of hungarian competitiveness will be how much of the €300 billion to €330 billion investment package announced by the european commission the respected hungarian government can bring to hungary. the government represents hungarian interests well when we receive ten billion euros of investment, which is about three percent of the package, because we also receive about that proportion from the eu budget. if less than this amount of investment is coming into our country, creating jobs, the government has failed to represent hungarian interests well. the hungarian government's success propaganda test whether hungary, which has been in regional competition since 2010, is able to propose competitive projects to investors. the hungarian government should propose programs that reduce our country's energy dependence. projects are needed that exploit the traditionally good hungarian grey stock, for example by further developing the szeged laser centre. the juncker package will be an objective measure of the competitiveness of the hungarian economy, of the hungarian government's socially insensitive economic policy. hungary must attract as many investments as possible not with cheap labour but with an innovative, competitive economy. it is unsustainable and a long-term concern that today only one hungarian company is on the list of the 1000 european companies that invest most in development. dismantling higher education, ridiculing it, and training talented hungarians to become skilled workers obviously won't help to reverse this.
in writing. the european parliament today adopted by a large majority the amendments to the eu budget for 2015 and the surplus resources for 2014. the agreement has the enormous advantage of allowing the eu's 2015 budget to be adopted this year, so that next year more than a twelfth of this year's budget could be paid out each month. this will provide an additional €4 billion for 2014, of which €3.5 billion will be 'fresh money', i.e. not the result of transfers. the ep only agreed to the eu budget for next year on condition that the european commission develops a plan to reduce unpaid bills to sustainable levels. in 2014, €23.4 billion of the previous year's arrears are due. today's vote will provide an additional funding of almost 4 billion euros. the european parliament's decision today also secured hungary's agricultural and cohesion resources for this year's and next year's budgets. the hungarian people's party delegation, together with the european people's party, in representing the national interests of our country, responsibly decided and voted on the 2015 eu budget and all the reports contained in the related agreement. the agreement also includes reports to mobilise the solidarity fund, which mitigates the damage caused by natural disasters in different regions of europe.

in writing. i voted in favour of adopting the eu budget for 2015. today's decision by the european parliament has fully covered the agricultural and cohesion resources due to hungary from the eu budget for this year and next. if that had not been achieved, only one twelfth of this year's budget could have been paid out each month next year. this would not have allowed the aid to be paid out at 100%, which would have been contrary to hungarian national interests. this budget is also important for hungarian farmers. on the one hand, we have managed to protect next year's agricultural resources. the reductions proposed by the council (e.g. in beekeeping and wine aid and in the financing of the operating funds of producer organisations) were prevented. on the other hand, we have been able to prevent budgetary withdrawals from the agricultural crisis fund. that's why the area-based grants won't be cut next year. so it was a success that we, together with mr deutsch and mr sándor fazekas, the minister of agriculture, defended direct payments that are important for hungarian farmers.

in writing. reducing the greenhouse gas emissions of fuels sold is a key element of the european union's efforts to combat climate change. the european union cannot afford to fail to meet its emission reduction targets. at the same time, we must achieve our objectives in a sustainable way, taking into account the interests of industry. we need to create regulations that reduce the bureaucratic burden on economic actors and effectively promote sustainable climate protection.
in writing. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution opposing the adoption of the draft directive, since the commission does not make any distinction in this draft concerning the different fuel sources. the directive would introduce a single value for the greenhouse gas intensity of all types of fuel. this means, among other things, that shale oil (or shale oil) will not be distinguished from conventional crude oil, although it is responsible for more greenhouse gas emissions. this will make it easier for unconventional fuels to reach europe. the draft was submitted to parliament after some five years of preparation, with the basic objective of reducing the oil industry's greenhouse gas emissions by 6% by 2020. however, it seems that this project has not fully lived up to the expectations attached to it. the socialists and democrats in the committee on the environment, public health and food safety were unanimous in their opposition to the draft. in my opinion, some elements of the draft are acceptable, but overall it does not lead to a result. my vote was intended to encourage the european commission to come up with a new alternative proposal to remove these obstacles.
although this resolution was undoubtedly adopted by a very large majority in the european parliament, it is appropriate that i should perhaps also have a word from those who abstained from voting on it. the reason for this was that i wanted to express my reservations about the content of certain elements of this decision, but above all about its timing and the international impact it is expected to have. while those who think that this resolution is a step forward for peace may be right, i believe that it is most important that the european union should play a more decisive and substantive role in promoting the middle east peace process, in the development of a lasting peace settlement leading to a negotiated two-state solution. to this end, the efforts of the high representative mogherini to establish a coherent and targeted european position deserve all the support that can be given, which will of course require time and meaningful cooperation from the european parliament, the european institutions and the member states. the call for the unilateral recognition of the state of palestine by member states in the absence of political conditions in the present situation seems to me to be more likely to undermine this important objective, losing sight of the fact that recognition should be the result of the peace settlement and not its instrument.

on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, first of all let me thank our rapporteur, our colleague mr andrejs mamikins, for a balanced report which contains many important and good recommendations from which both the georgian government and anyone in georgia interested in politics and interested in a european future could profit. now certainly this is the time first of all to celebrate. association agreements, if they are meant to be serious agreements, are to be celebrated and i propose that we do that tomorrow and i ask all of you to support the association agreement and report with your vote. we need to pause for a moment and congratulate the georgian authorities who went through considerable changes already. of course there is much to be done. the report contains many of those important elements and the commissioner has already mentioned many of them so i am not going to repeat them. ultimately, however, i want to highlight that i do not mean to contest the statistics cited by the commissioner, but we should bear in mind that free trade agreements are not unmitigated benefits. there are social costs to them, so i warn both the commission, the georgian authorities and this house that we should follow very closely what happens at the regional level to the georgian economy and to the georgian social fabric and year by year we should return to this question.
in writing. the conclusion of the association agreement is an important step in the history of georgia and the eu. the present association agreement provides a great opportunity for georgia to become more closely involved in the process of european integration. i voted in favour of the proposals contained in this motion, as i believe that it places an appropriate emphasis on reforming the judicial system and strengthening the rule of law and democracy.
in writing. by joining the deep and comprehensive free trade area (dcfta) under the eastern partnership agreement, georgia has voted in favour of adopting legislation, standards and common values. while georgia will gain market access to the largest trading bloc, the eu will enjoy a more favourable trading environment and investment conditions in georgia. my vote contributed to the modernisation of the georgian economy, the consolidation of eu investments and a more predictable political and regulatory environment.
